# hydo milestone history

New milestone sections go to the END of this file. Each new milestone also gets a one-line entry in the milestone index of `CLAUDE.md` and an update of the "Current state" (and, if needed, "Open items") section there.

Everything below was moved verbatim from `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-10-23 (the old handoff text, Milestones 1–69, the roadmap for booting Linux as L2, and the old "Key files" list). Old notes mention the nested-VM (L1) tooling, which was removed on 2026-10-20; later milestones supersede many older baselines, and `CLAUDE.md` lists only the current ones.

`hydo` is a **deterministic hypervisor**: it runs software so that, given the same inputs, execution is bit-for-bit identical every time — enabling record/replay, snapshot, and branch-from-snapshot. See `README.md` for the full architecture, the paravirtual ABI, the three clock strategies, and the verification model. This file is the operational context + current-frontier handoff; it does not duplicate the README.

## How to build & run (READ FIRST — non-obvious)

- **hvx (the VT-x module) runs on the bare-metal machine** (Milestone 33 on; regression `hvx/deploy-metal.sh [load] [flagships]`), and on Apple Silicon Macs via `hvx-mac/` (Milestones 48, 51, 53). **The nested-VM (L1) tooling was removed on 2026-10-20**: `hvx/run-l1.sh`, `hvx/deploy-hvx.sh`, `hvx/deploy-resume.sh`, `hvx/cloud-init/`, `hvx/kafka-once.sh` and the `hvx/run-*-demo.sh`/`run-fuzz*`/`run-spread*` runners. The milestone notes below that mention them describe history; restore them from git (`git show 2c04001:hvx/run-l1.sh`) if a nested VM is ever needed again (no bare-metal machine, or testing a host without a usable PMU). The build machine is now used only to build guest images.

- **hydo runs only on Linux (KVM); it will not compile on macOS.** You edit files locally on the Mac at `/Users/user/mine/rust/my_projects/hydo`, then build/run on the remote Linux box.
- **The build machine:** (NixOS, kernel 6.6.35, Intel VT-x, 31 GB RAM, 24 cores). `/dev/kvm` is world-accessible (unprivileged VMs work). **We have NO root there** (sudo needs a password we don't have). **Nested virtualization is enabled** (`kvm_intel.nested = Y`). `perf_event_paranoid = 1` (the user set it; the PMU fast-path needs it).
- **Workflow:** `./dev.sh "<remote cmd>"` rsyncs the tree to `~/hydo/` on the build machine and runs the command there (since M151 it reaches the machine through the git-ignored `machines.local.sh`, described by the tracked `machines.sh` at the repository root). Example: `./dev.sh "./build-guests.sh && cargo build --release && ./target/release/hydo verify --guest build/det64.bin --mode long --mem 16"`.
- **Toolchain on remote:** Rust 1.86, `clang`, GNU `as`/`ld`/`objcopy` (build guest blobs via `./build-guests.sh`). **No gcc/make/nasm** on the host (but `nix` can provide them). Dep pins that matter: `kvm-ioctls = "0.17"` MUST pair with `kvm-bindings = "0.8"`.
- Guest sources are `guests/*.s` (flat binaries, `# load: 0xADDR` on line 1). A real Linux kernel/initramfs are staged by `./build-linux.sh`.

## What works today (all verified on the remote)

- Deterministic KVM VMM with `verify` passing (replay / round-trip / branch-from-snapshot) across real-mode and long-mode guests, in all three clock modes. `cargo test` = 5/5, 0 warnings. Baseline fingerprints: `det64` = `ed882eafa4643e09`, `timer64 --timer 200 --insn-clock` = `0b3b7c95eadbcfae` (use these to detect regressions).
- Three clocks: exit-count (default), single-step instruction clock (`--insn-clock`), and PMU fast-path (`--insn-clock --fast`, ~33× faster, auto-retries on PMU descheduling).
- **A real Linux kernel boots to an interactive shell** under hydo (`boot-linux`, non-deterministic — uses KVM in-kernel irqchip/PIT).
- **Divergence harness** (`boot-verify`, `boot-probe`, `boot-step` in `src/detlinux.rs`): boots the kernel twice under the instruction clock and bisects the first divergence. Result: **two boots stay bit-identical for ~730 M retired instructions** (all of decompression + early setup), then diverge at **`RDTSC` in `native_calibrate_cpu_early`**. Masking RDRAND/RDSEED, dropping kvm-clock, and resetting TSC don't move the wall because RDTSC calibration comes first.
- Subcommands: `run`, `verify`, `resume`, `trace`, `pmuprobe`, `boot-linux`, `boot-verify`, `boot-probe`, `boot-step`.

## The wall, and the current frontier

The RDTSC divergence is a **hard KVM limit**: stock KVM does not expose the VT-x "RDTSC-exiting" control to a userspace VMM, so hydo cannot make `RDTSC` a function of the instruction clock. This is exactly why hydo needs its own hypervisor rather than KVM.

**Decision (user's):** build our OWN VT-x hypervisor — a Linux kernel module that owns the VMCS directly — so we can enable RDTSC-exiting (and later drive interrupt timing ourselves). This is the multi-month "be your own hypervisor" path.

### SAFETY MODEL — do not violate

- **Never load a kernel module on the host; never risk a host panic.** We have no host root, so this is also enforced by construction — do NOT attempt `sudo`/`insmod`/`rmmod` on the host; if something needs host root, STOP and ask the user.
- All hypervisor-module development happens **inside a throwaway nested VM**:
  - **L0** = the host (never touched).
  - **L1** = a Linux VM run under the host's KVM as an unprivileged `qemu -enable-kvm -cpu host` process (VMX exposed via nesting); we are root inside it and can rebuild/reload freely. If our code panics L1, we just restart the QEMU process — the host is unaffected.
  - **L2** = the guest run by our own hypervisor module inside L1.
- Get qemu/gcc/etc. via `nix shell nixpkgs#...`. Keep the module's authoritative source in the repo under `hvx/`; copy into L1 to build/`insmod`.

### Milestone 1 — DONE (independently reproduced)

Our own kernel module (`hvx/hvx.c`) does `VMXON`, `VMLAUNCH`es a trivial `cpuid; hlt` guest, handles the CPUID exit, `VMRESUME`s, handles the HLT exit, and `VMXOFF`s cleanly — all inside L1, host untouched. The whole VMX lifecycle runs inside `module_init`, pinned to one CPU with preemption/IRQs off, torn down before init returns (no held VMX state, trivial unload).

### Milestone 2 — DONE (EPT; verified twice in L1)

The guest now runs in its **own physical address space** via EPT (`hvx/hvx.c`, ~560 lines). Six host pages become guest-physical 0x0000–0x5FFF through a 4-level EPT structure (RWX, WB + ignore-PAT leaves); guest page tables (PML4/PDPT/PD, one 2 MiB identity page) are built *inside guest memory* at GPA 0x0000–0x2FFF so guest-virtual == guest-physical; the blob runs at GPA 0x3000 with CR3=0, stack at 0x4000, and writes magic `0x6879646f2d657074` ("hydo-ept") to GPA 0x5000, which the module verifies from the host side after teardown ("MILESTONE 2 PASS" in dmesg). Details that matter: secondary exec controls + `enable EPT` are checked against the allowed-1 MSR bits and the load **aborts before VMLAUNCH** if missing (a silently-dropped EPT bit with guest CR3=0 would walk host physical page 0); guest CR0/CR4 are derived from the `IA32_VMX_CRn_FIXED` MSRs; segments are synthetic (flat, no GDT — the guest never reloads them); `INVEPT` (global, falling back to single-context) runs before launch. L1's `ept_vpid_cap=0xf0106334041` (4-level walk, WB, all INVEPT types).

**Sandbox bring-up (host):** `~/hydo/hvx/run-l1.sh` (authoritative copy `hvx/run-l1.sh`) — idempotent/daemonized; re-run to restart L1 after any panic **or hard wedge** (a buggy guest that steals host interrupts can RCU-stall L1; kill+restart recovers, host untouched). ⚠️ **The KVM host is SHARED** — it also runs unrelated `qemu-system` TCG workloads (a deterministic-replay benchmark/fuzzer). NEVER `pkill -f qemu-system` broadly; scope kills to our instance only (`pkill -f l1-hvx`, or the pid in `~/l1/l1.pid`). Gotcha already fixed: `run-l1.sh`'s `nix build nixpkgs#xorriso --print-out-paths` now returns multiple store paths (a separate `-man` output), so the script selects the one containing `bin/xorriso` rather than appending blindly. **A shell in L1 (from the host):** a remote login to the L1 VM's forwarded port 2222 on localhost with the key `~/l1/id_l1` (plain heredoc form only; an interactive terminal through the double hop hangs).

**Rebuild+reload cycle (from build machine):** `./dev.sh "~/hydo/hvx/deploy-hvx.sh load"` — syncs, copies into L1, builds the module **and the `hvx_test` userspace driver**, rmmods kvm_intel, insmods, **runs `hvx_test` against `/dev/hvx`**, prints dmesg, rmmods. Source: `hvx/hvx.c`, `hvx/hvx_abi.h` (shared kernel↔userland ABI), `hvx/hvx_test.c` (userspace driver), `hvx/Makefile` (sets `OBJECT_FILES_NON_STANDARD_hvx.o := y` — objtool trips on the hand-written asm trampoline's `ret` under return-thunk mitigation), `hvx/run-l1.sh`, `hvx/deploy-hvx.sh`, `hvx/cloud-init/`. Gotcha already solved: CR-access helpers are named `hvx_cr0/…` (bare `read_cr0` collides with a kernel paravirt inline).

### Milestone 3 — DONE (userspace mini-KVM interface; verified + stress-tested in L1)

The module is now a **device, not a one-shot** (`hvx/hvx.c`, ~640 lines): guest memory, registers, and "run" all come from userspace through `/dev/hvx` (a misc device, mode 0600), in the shape of a tiny KVM-like ioctl API defined in `hvx/hvx_abi.h`: `HVX_CREATE_VM` (size guest RAM, build EPT + identity page tables), `mmap(fd)` (map guest RAM into userland to load code / read results), `HVX_SET_REGS`/`HVX_GET_REGS`, `HVX_RUN` (one VM-exit per call, returns reason/insn_len/qualification/guest_phys). Per-fd VM state (`struct hvx_vm`: guest pages, EPT, VMCS/VMXON pages, a software register file) **persists across ioctls**. `hvx_test.c` proves the whole guest is now userland-supplied: it loads a blob that writes the "hydo-ept" magic, steps over `CPUID` from userspace via the exit's `insn_len`, stops at `HLT`, then confirms **both** the register round-trip (guest `rax`/`rbx` came back through `HVX_GET_REGS`) and the memory write (read back through the shared `mmap`). "MILESTONE 3 PASS"; 5 back-to-back VM lifecycles on one loaded module leave dmesg clean (no BUG/taint/leak) and unload cleanly.

Design decisions that matter (read before extending):
- **VMX is NOT held across ioctls.** Each `HVX_RUN` is a self-contained VMX-root critical section — `VMXON → VMCLEAR → VMPTRLD → (fill VMCS from the register file) → VMLAUNCH → in-kernel run-loop → VMXOFF` — inside one `preempt_disable`/`local_irq_save` section. The run-loop VMRESUMEs through exits it handles in-kernel (RDTSC, see M4) and stops at the first userspace-visible exit; each *new* `HVX_RUN` starts with a fresh `VMLAUNCH` from the saved RIP, so there are **no stale-CPU / migration hazards**. Holding VMXON across ioctls stays an optimization deferred to single-stepping, not a requirement — a deliberate departure from the earlier "VMXON held while a VM exists" note, chosen for safety/correctness first.
- **Guest register file layout is load-bearing.** The asm trampoline `hvx_vmx_run(struct hvx_regs *, long resume)` indexes GPRs by byte offset (rax@0x00 … r15@0x70); `struct hvx_regs` in `hvx_abi.h` must keep that exact order. rip/rsp/rflags travel via VMCS natural-width fields, not the trampoline. The trampoline keeps the resume flag + regs pointer on the stack across the guest run (HOST_RSP points at them); if you touch the asm, keep the push/pop and `add $N,%rsp` counts balanced.
- **Guest RAM is individually-allocated order-0 pages**, not one high-order block. EPT maps guest-physical page-by-page anyway, and per-page allocation keeps each page's refcount correct when the mmap fault handler hands it to userspace — an early high-order-block version corrupted page state (`BUG: Bad page state`, nonzero refcount) because tail pages of a non-compound allocation aren't individually refcounted. Cap is `MAX_GPAGES = 512` (≤ 2 MiB, one EPT PT); userspace code is expected at `HVX_GUEST_CODE_BASE = 0x3000` (pages 0–2 hold the module-built identity tables).

### Milestone 4 — DONE (deterministic RDTSC interception; verified + stress-tested in L1)

hvx now does the one thing stock KVM refuses a userspace VMM — the wall that motivated this whole path. `setup_vmcs` sets the **RDTSC-exiting** primary proc-based control (bit 12) and aborts before VMLAUNCH if the CPU won't allow it (same discipline as EPT). `hvx_run_vcpu` became a small in-kernel **run-loop**: it VMLAUNCHes, and on `RDTSC`/`RDTSCP` exits it substitutes a **deterministic virtual TSC** into guest `EDX:EAX` (`ECX`=IA32_TSC_AUX for RDTSCP), advances RIP past the instruction, bumps the virtual TSC by `step`, and **VMRESUMEs** — so the guest never sees the host counter and no userspace round-trip happens per RDTSC. Userspace owns the policy via `HVX_SET_TSC`/`HVX_GET_TSC` (seed, step, aux, and a read-back exit count). A safety bound (`HVX_MAX_INKERNEL_EXITS = 1<<16`) caps the IRQs-off window against a runaway guest, returning `HVX_EXIT_THROTTLE` so userspace re-runs. `hvx_rdtsc_test.c` runs a guest that does RDTSC×3 into memory then HLTs, and asserts (1) the values equal the deterministic sequence `seed, seed+step, seed+2·step` (seed has its high dword set, so EDX is exercised) rather than the host TSC, and (2) a **second run from the same seed is byte-identical**. "MILESTONE 4 PASS"; 6 back-to-back RDTSC lifecycles + the M3 regression on one loaded module leave dmesg clean (no BUG/taint/leak) and unload cleanly. dmesg proof: the 3 RDTSCs produce **one** returned exit (HLT) with `rdtsc_count=3` — all three handled in-kernel via VMRESUME.

Caveat / honest scope: as of M4 the virtual TSC was a per-RDTSC-exit counter, not tied to the instruction clock. That clock arrived in M5 and RDTSC is wired to it in M6 (`HVX_TSC_ICOUNT`), so the caveat is now closed.

### Milestone 5 — DONE (MTF instruction clock; verified + stress-tested in L1)

hvx now has the determinism engine's core primitive: an **instruction clock** built on the **Monitor Trap Flag**. When single-stepping, `setup_vmcs` sets the MTF proc-based control (bit 27, asserted-supported like EPT/RDTSC — **and nested KVM in L1 does expose it**), so every guest instruction takes an MTF VM-exit (basic reason 37). The `hvx_run_vcpu` run-loop counts those (plus emulated RDTSCs) into `vm->icount` and can stop after exactly `max_insns` instructions. A new ioctl `HVX_RUNC` (`struct hvx_run_ctl`) drives it: `HVX_RUN_SINGLE_STEP` enables MTF, `HVX_RUN_RESET_ICOUNT` zeroes the clock, `max_insns` bounds the call (returns `HVX_EXIT_STEP` = 37 with the guest still alive), and it returns the running `icount`. The existing `HVX_MAX_INKERNEL_EXITS` cap now also bounds the single-step IRQs-off window (returns `HVX_EXIT_THROTTLE`; userspace just re-runs, which the test does transparently). `hvx_step_test.c` single-steps a statically-known guest — `mov $100,%ecx; loop: dec %ecx; jnz loop; hlt`, exactly `1 + 100·2 = 201` instructions — and asserts: (1) the full run counts **exactly 201**, (2) a second run counts identically (determinism), and (3) stopping at `max_insns=50` lands on `icount==50` (reason `STEP`) and resuming reaches the same 201. "MILESTONE 5 PASS"; 6 back-to-back single-step lifecycles + M3/M4 regressions leave dmesg clean and unload cleanly. Note: `icount` semantics — it counts instructions the monitor *observes retiring* (every MTF step + every emulated RDTSC/RDTSCP), so it is a full instruction count only while single-stepping; `HVX_RUN` (no MTF) still advances it on RDTSCs only.

### Milestone 6 — DONE (instruction-clock TSC; verified + stress-tested in L1)

RDTSC is now tied to the instruction clock, closing the M4 caveat. `struct hvx_vm` gained a `tsc_mode`; `HVX_SET_TSC` (extended with a `mode` field) selects it: `HVX_TSC_COUNTER` (default, M4 behaviour: return `tsc`, then `tsc += step` per call) or `HVX_TSC_ICOUNT` (return `tsc + icount·step`, where `tsc` is the base and `step` the ticks-per-instruction; `tsc` is not mutated). The RDTSC handler in `hvx_run_vcpu` computes the ICOUNT value from `vm->icount` **before** the shared retired-instruction bump, so it reflects instructions retired strictly before this RDTSC. ICOUNT is meaningful only while single-stepping (when `icount` counts every instruction). `hvx_tscclock_test.c` runs one guest — two RDTSCs with exactly 6 instructions between them (`rdtsc; mov; mov; nop; nop; nop; rdtsc`) — in both modes and asserts the delta `TSC#1−TSC#0` is `step` under COUNTER (one call) but `6·step` under ICOUNT (six instructions), plus `TSC#0 == base` and run-to-run reproducibility. dmesg-clean; 6 lifecycles + M3/M4/M5 regressions pass. Observed: COUNTER delta 1000, ICOUNT delta 6000 — a 6× spread from execution position alone.

### Milestone 7 — DONE (snapshot / branch-from-snapshot; verified + stress-tested in L1)

hvx can now capture and restore a VM's full deterministic state and replay from a mid-execution branch point bit-for-bit. Two ioctls (`HVX_GET_SNAPSHOT`/`HVX_SET_SNAPSHOT`, `struct hvx_snapshot`) copy the software state — the register file, virtual-TSC state (`tsc`/`step`/`aux`/`mode`), instruction clock `icount`, and `rdtsc_count`. **Guest RAM is not in the struct**: it's already mmap'd, so a full snapshot is `HVX_GET_SNAPSHOT` + `memcpy(save_buf, mmap, size)` and restore is `HVX_SET_SNAPSHOT` + `memcpy(mmap, save_buf, size)`. Host-transient state (VMCS/EPT/VMXON) is intentionally absent — it's rebuilt from the register file on the next run. `hvx_snapshot_test.c` runs a loop that accumulates instruction-clock-dependent RDTSC values into RAM (so the result depends on `icount`, the loop counter `%ecx`, AND the partial sum in RAM); it runs uninterrupted, then snapshots at exactly `icount=30` (mid-loop) and finishes, then restores and finishes again — asserting all three reach `icount=80, acc=0xb98c0`. Any state the snapshot missed would make the restored replay diverge, so equality proves completeness. dmesg-clean; 6 lifecycles + M3–M6 regressions pass.

Snapshot completeness — what's covered vs. deferred (checked against hydo's KVM-based `Snapshot` in `src/state.rs`, which captures regs+sregs+fpu+`kvm_vcpu_events`+RAM+device/PV state):
- **Covered now and sufficient for current guests**: register file, TSC policy/state, instruction clock, guest RAM. Snapshots are taken only at MTF single-step boundaries, which are clean instruction boundaries (no interruptibility shadow, no split-IO mid-flight) — hydo makes the same "snapshot only at reproducible boundaries" guarantee and *drains* pending IO before pausing.
- **Deferred, add when the matching feature lands**: (a) pending-event / interruptibility state (VMCS `GUEST_INTERRUPTIBILITY`/`GUEST_ACTIVITY_STATE`/`GUEST_PENDING_DBG` + any queued `VM_ENTRY_INTR_INFO`) — hydo's `kvm_vcpu_events` — needed once **interrupt injection (M8)** exists; (b) FPU/XMM/extended CR/segment state — needed once guests use them (our flat long-mode guests don't); (c) PV/device + PRNG-evolved state — none yet; (d) a **canonical field-by-field fingerprint** à la hydo's `Snapshot::fingerprint()` (BLAKE3, meaningful fields only, padding excluded) for a real `verify`/round-trip harness — the current test compares concrete outputs (`acc`+`icount`) instead, which is enough to prove completeness for these guests but not a general equality check.

### Milestone 8 — DONE (instruction-timed interrupt injection; verified + stress-tested in L1)

hvx delivers an interrupt at an exact instruction, deterministically. Three parts landed: (1) the guest now gets a **real protected environment** built into the free space of the page-table pages (`build_guest_system`): a GDT (null / 64-bit code 0x08 / data 0x10 / TSS 0x18), an IDT whose gates point at a tiny handler blob at `GPA_ISR` (`inc qword [GPA_INTR_COUNT]; iretq`), so the CPU can reload CS/SS from the GDT during delivery/IRETQ — `setup_vmcs` now points `GUEST_GDTR/IDTR/TR` at these. (2) `HVX_INJECT` (`struct hvx_inject {vector,type,at_icount}`) schedules a one-shot injection; the run-loop arms `VM_ENTRY_INTR_INFO = 0x80000000|vector` on the entry where `icount >= at_icount`. (3) the pending injection is part of `struct hvx_snapshot`, so a restored branch injects at the identical instruction. `hvx_intr_test.c`: baseline (no inject) = 30 insns, handler-count 0; inject vector 0x20 at instruction 15 → icount 33 (delivery + 2-insn handler), guest still finishes its own work via IRETQ (guest counter 10), handler ran exactly once; reproducible; and snapshot-before-15 → finish → restore → finish re-fires identically. 6 lifecycles + M3–M7 regressions pass, dmesg-clean, clean unload.

Two hardware subtleties this milestone forced (both load-bearing — do not remove):
- **External-interrupt exiting is mandatory** (`PINBASED_EXTINT_EXITING` in the pin controls). In non-root operation, physical host interrupts are delivered into the GUEST's IDT whenever guest `RFLAGS.IF=1`, gated by the *guest's* IF, not the host's masked IF — so `local_irq_save` does NOT protect us. Without this control a guest with IF=1 **steals L1's timer interrupts**, and L1 hard-wedges with an RCU stall (observed: had to kill+restart L1). With it, physical interrupts VM-exit (reason 1); the run-loop services them by opening a one-instruction `sti; nop; cli` window so the HOST IDT handles them, then resumes transparently (not counted as a guest instruction). We do NOT set "acknowledge interrupt on exit", so the interrupt stays pending for that window.
- **Injecting an external interrupt (type 0) requires guest `RFLAGS.IF=1`** — VM-entry enforces it (else entry-failure, invalid-guest-state, reason `0x80000021`). The injection test sets `rflags=0x202`; the ABI documents this.

Deferred (unchanged from M7's list): the snapshot still does not capture VMCS interruptibility/activity/pending-debug state or an in-flight (already-armed-but-not-delivered) `VM_ENTRY_INTR_INFO`. Our snapshots are taken at MTF boundaries where interruptibility is 0 and injections are only *scheduled* (software `inject_valid`, which IS captured), so this is correct for current use; capture the VMCS non-register state if a future guest can be snapshotted mid-STI-shadow.

### Milestone 9 — DONE (canonical fingerprint + verify harness; verified in L1)

hvx now has hydo's determinism-verification model natively — and it is **userspace-only** (no kernel change; the module stays at M8). `hvx_verify.c` computes a canonical fingerprint over the full state and runs the same three checks as `src/verify.rs`. The fingerprint (like hydo's `Snapshot::fingerprint`) feeds MEANINGFUL fields in fixed little-endian order so padding never enters the hash — `s.regs`, then `tsc`/`step`/`aux`/`mode`/`icount`/`rdtsc_count`/`inject_*`, then a length-prefix and the guest RAM bytes (via the mmap) — with a self-contained FNV-1a (not BLAKE3; the test only needs to detect divergence, so no crypto lib). The guest is the RDTSC-accumulator loop (final state depends on the instruction clock, TSC, loop counter, and RAM). Checks: **replay** (two fresh runs hash identically), **round-trip** (`SET_SNAPSHOT` then re-fingerprint == same), **branch** (step to the midpoint via `HVX_RUNC max_insns`, snapshot, finish → fp_orig; restore, finish → fp_branch; assert `fp_orig == fp_branch == fp_full`). All four fingerprints came back identical (`99393734aa84b0f5`); regressions M3–M8 pass; clean unload.

### Milestone 10 — DONE (PIO trap + serial console; verified in L1) — Phase 1 of the L2-Linux road

First step toward booting Linux as L2. Set the proc-based **unconditional I/O-exiting** control (bit 24, asserted-supported), so all guest `IN`/`OUT` now VM-exit (`EXIT_REASON_IO_INSTRUCTION` = 30) instead of hitting L1's physical ports — this also closes a real latent hazard (guest I/O was previously escaping to the host's 0x3f8/0x70/0x40/…). `hvx_pio()` decodes the exit qualification and emulates a 16550 UART: `OUT` to 0x3f8 (THR) accumulates into a per-VM `console[HVX_CONSOLE_SZ]`, `IN` from 0x3fd (LSR) returns `0x60` (THRE|TEMT = tx ready), other UART regs read 0, all other ports are inert (IN→0xff, OUT dropped); string `OUTS`/`INS` handled assuming GVA==GPA (true for identity-mapped guests). Handled in the run-loop's retired block like RDTSC (advance RIP, count, VMRESUME). `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` reads the buffer. `hvx_console_test.c`: a blob writes "HELLO\n" to 0x3f8 and reads LSR into GPA 0x5000; asserts console=="HELLO\n" and LSR==0x60. All M3–M9 regressions pass, dmesg-clean. Console is NOT yet in the snapshot/fingerprint (deferred to when real output makes it matter; note `hvx_verify` could hash `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` output when needed).

### Milestone 11 — DONE (guest RAM beyond 2 MiB; verified in L1) — Phase 2 of the L2-Linux road

Guest RAM now scales to 256 MiB (`MAX_GPAGES = 65536`). `struct hvx_vm` holds `guest_pages`/`ept_pt` as `kvmalloc_array`'d pointer arrays instead of a fixed 512-entry array; EPT still uses 4 KiB leaves (L1's EPT does support 2 MiB pages — `ept_vpid_cap` bit 16 set — but guest RAM is deliberately non-contiguous order-0 pages, which 2 MiB leaves can't map), with `n_ept_pt = ceil(n/512)` PT pages (128 for 256 MiB) hung off the single EPT PD. Guest identity paging now fills all 512 PD entries with 2 MiB PS pages (covers the low 1 GiB, like the KVM path); only EPT-backed GPAs resolve. `hvx_bigmem_test.c` creates a 256 MiB VM and a guest writes/reads GPA 200 MiB, verified both by the guest's read-back and via the shared mmap. All M3–M10 regressions pass. Note: EPT PT pages and the two pointer arrays are freed in `hvx_vm_free_pages`; the fault-based mmap and `gpa_host` are unchanged (still index `guest_pages[]`).

### Milestone 12 — DONE (deterministic CPUID + MSR emulation; verified in L1) — Phase 3 of the L2-Linux road

hvx now owns CPUID and RDMSR/WRMSR **in-kernel** — the last classic "the CPU tells you about the real host" sources that would diverge two runs, and state a guest OS reads very early. All three are handled in the `hvx_run_vcpu` retired-instruction block (like RDTSC/PIO/MTF: emulate → advance RIP by `VM_EXIT_INSN_LEN` → `retired=true` so it counts on the instruction clock → VMRESUME), so **no CPUID/MSR exit reaches userspace**. CPUID (`hvx_cpuid`) writes a FIXED synthetic table into guest EAX/EBX/ECX/EDX (low 32 of RAX… zero-extended): leaf 0 = max-leaf 7 + "GenuineIntel"; leaf 1 = family 6 (0x000006f1), EBX CLFLUSH=8, ECX=0, EDX = FPU|PSE|TSC|MSR|PAE|CX8|APIC|PGE|CMOV|CLFLUSH|FXSR|SSE|SSE2 (**deliberately no XSAVE/AVX/OSXSAVE**, so the guest never runs XSETBV); leaf 7/0 = 0; leaf 0x80000000 = max-ext 0x80000001; leaf 0x80000001 EDX = SYSCALL|NX|LM; every other leaf 0. RDMSR/WRMSR (reasons 31/32, `hvx_rdmsr`/`hvx_wrmsr`): **IA32_EFER** (0xC0000080) round-trips through the VMCS `GUEST_IA32_EFER` field; **IA32_TSC** (0x10) returns the deterministic virtual TSC (WRMSR re-bases it); a **curated software MSR store** (`vm->sw_msr[HVX_NR_SW_MSR]`, new per-VM array, `swmsr_slot()` maps index→slot) backs STAR/LSTAR/CSTAR/SFMASK/FS_BASE/GS_BASE/KERNEL_GS_BASE/SYSENTER_CS/ESP/EIP/MISC_ENABLE (RDMSR returns stored, 0 until written; WRMSR stores); any other MSR reads 0 / drops the write (**no #GP** — keep a booting guest safe). The store is folded into `struct hvx_snapshot` + GET/SET_SNAPSHOT + the `hvx_verify` fingerprint. **Interaction fixed:** in-kernel CPUID broke the old `hvx_test.c` (its guest did `cpuid` to demo a userspace exit and checked `rax==magic`); the demo `cpuid` was dropped — the register round-trip still proves M3. `hvx_cpuid_test.c` runs CPUID leaves 0/1/0x80000001 + WRMSR/RDMSR LSTAR + RDMSR IA32_TSC into guest RAM and asserts the exact synthetic values, the MSR round-trip, XSAVE-clear, and byte-identical reproduction across two runs. All M3–M11 regressions pass, dmesg-clean, clean unload.

### Milestone 13 — DONE (privileged-state persistence + HVX_SET_SREGS; verified in L1) — Phase 4 of the L2-Linux road

Guest privileged state now **persists across HVX_RUN boundaries**. Before M13 `setup_vmcs` rebuilt CR0/CR3/CR4/EFER/DR7/segments/GDTR/IDTR from hardcoded flat defaults every entry, so any guest change was lost the moment an ioctl returned. Now `struct hvx_vm` holds a software `struct hvx_sregs sregs`, seeded on CREATE (`seed_sregs`) to the exact flat long-mode defaults; `setup_vmcs` **loads the guest from it** and the run-loop **saves it back** (`save_sregs`, VMREAD of all CRs/EFER/DR7/segments/GDTR/IDTR) once at `out_clear` after each exit. `HVX_GET_SREGS`/`HVX_SET_SREGS` (ABI structs already existed; kernel handlers now implemented) read/drive it, and it is folded into the snapshot + fingerprint. Design decisions (load-bearing): (a) `fixed_cr()` still ORs the `IA32_VMX_CRn_FIXED` bits onto CR0/CR4 on every load, so a user SET_SREGS that omits them cannot fault VM-entry (CR0/CR4 are seeded already-post-fixed → idempotent, no drift); (b) segment limits stay the raw VMCS `0xffffffff`; (c) **LDTR is forced unusable in `setup_vmcs`** regardless of `sregs` — its VMCS AR "unusable" bit (16) does not fit the ABI's `hvx_segment.ar` (`__u16`), and our guests never use an LDT; (d) TR is loaded from `sregs` (seeded to the module TSS: sel 0x18, base `GPA_TSS`, limit 0x67, ar 0x8b). **Why existing tests stay safe:** the values captured at every HLT/STEP boundary are exactly the flat ones `setup_vmcs` loaded (the guest never touched them), so the save/reload is an identity of a known-good VMLAUNCH — it cannot newly fault; the only field a test deliberately changes is CR3. `hvx_sregs_test.c` builds a second identity page table (PT2 at GPA 0x6000), runs `mov cr3,PT2; hlt`, confirms `HVX_GET_SREGS` captured cr3==PT2, then runs a second guest (`mov rax,cr3; store; hlt`) **without re-setting sregs** and shows it starts under PT2 (proving persistence across the ioctl boundary), and finally `HVX_SET_SREGS(cr3=0)` makes that guest read 0. All M3–M12 regressions pass, dmesg-clean, clean unload. (Note: cross-run EFER persistence, deferred at M12, now also falls out of this — WRMSR EFER is saved via `GUEST_IA32_EFER` into `sregs.efer`.)

### Milestone 14 — DONE (first real Linux serial output as L2; verified in L1) — Phase 5 of the L2-Linux road

**A real Linux kernel (NixOS 6.6.35 bzImage) now boots as an L2 guest under hvx and prints genuine early serial output** — through the compressed-kernel decompressor AND deep into real-kernel init — before hitting the expected Phase-6 LAPIC-MMIO wall. The loader `hvx/hvx_linux.c` (already written, ported from `src/boot.rs`/`detlinux.rs`) drives it via `/dev/hvx`: build page tables/GDT/zero-page/e820/cmdline/MP-table/initramfs in mmap'd guest RAM, `HVX_SET_SREGS` long mode, `HVX_SET_REGS` (rip=0x100200, rsi=0x10000), then free-run `HVX_RUN`, re-running on `HVX_EXIT_THROTTLE` (normal — the 65536-in-kernel-exit cap fires many times) and draining `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` each iteration. Four kernel-side fixes made the boot reach serial output; **each was found by running to a wall, decoding the faulting instruction, and fixing it** (loader now dumps the faulting insn bytes, translating a kernel-virtual RIP → guest-physical via `phys = rip − 0xffffffff80000000`):

1. **FPU guard** (`hvx_run_vcpu`): the guest uses SSE very early, clobbering the live host XMM/FPU that belongs to the interrupted host task. Wrap the whole VMX-root section in `kernel_fpu_begin()`/`kernel_fpu_end()` (`#include <asm/fpu/api.h>`); it also disables preemption, so it brackets `preempt_disable`/`local_irq_save`. No host FPU code runs inside the run-loop, so guest XMM persists across VMRESUMEs and is discarded on the final exit.
2. **Hide CR4.VMXE from the guest** (`setup_vmcs`). The decompressor's `head_64.S` does `mov %cr4,%rax; and $0x1060,%eax; mov %rax,%cr4` — masking CR4 down to PAE|MCE|LA57, i.e. **dropping VMXE**, which #GPs in VMX operation and (no guest IDT yet) triple-faults at **guest RIP 0x10027e**. Fix like KVM: `CR4_GH_MASK = IA32_VMX_CR4_FIXED0` (the forced-1 bits, incl. VMXE) so the host owns them, and `CR4_READ_SHADOW = guest_cr4 & ~fixed0` so the guest reads/writes VMXE as 0; a guest CR4 write whose owned bits equal the shadow does not exit and leaves physical VMXE intact. CR0 stays fully guest-owned (mask 0) — the guest never clears PE/PG/NE.
3. **Reflect FS/GS base MSRs into the VMCS** (`hvx_wrmsr`/`hvx_rdmsr`). `IA32_FS_BASE` (0xc0000100) / `IA32_GS_BASE` (0xc0000101) are BOTH MSRs and VMCS guest fields; M12 stored them only in `sw_msr[]`, so the guest's actual GS base stayed 0. The real kernel's `head_64.S` sets GS base via WRMSR just before `x86_64_start_kernel`, whose prologue loads the stack canary from `%gs:0x28` — with base 0 that reads unmapped linear 0x28 and triple-faults at **kernel RIP 0xffffffff81034cf3** (`mov %gs:0x28,%rax`). Fix: WRMSR of FS/GS base does `VMW(GUEST_FS_BASE_F/GUEST_GS_BASE_F, val)` and RDMSR reads those fields; `save_sregs` already reads them back into `sregs`, so they persist across HVX_RUN and into the snapshot. (In 64-bit mode `%gs:`/`%fs:` use the base field even when the selector is null/unusable.)
4. **Segment "unusable" bit round-trip** (`seg_ar()` in `setup_vmcs`) — the last-mile bug. Real Linux nulls DS/ES/SS in long mode, making them **unusable** (VMCS AR bit 16). The ABI's `hvx_segment.ar` is `__u16` and cannot hold bit 16, so M13's `save_sregs` truncated it; on the next entry `setup_vmcs` reloaded AR with P=0 but *usable* → VM-entry **"invalid guest state" (exit 0x80000021)** on the first re-entry AFTER a THROTTLE (the save/reload path). Fix without an ABI change: `seg_ar(ar) = (ar & 0x80/*P*/) ? ar : 0x10000/*unusable*/` applied to all six segs + TR — a usable seg always has P=1, and a present-clear usable seg is invalid for VM-entry anyway. This corrects the M13 truncation assumption (which only ever held for LDTR). The seeded flat segments (all P=1) are unchanged, so M3–M13 stay bit-identical.

**Result (256 MiB guest, default cmdline):** `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` captured 2826 bytes of genuine Linux early output — `early console in extract_kernel` / `Decompressing Linux… Parsing ELF… done. Booting the kernel`, then the real kernel: `Linux version 6.6.35 …`, our exact command line, the **e820 map we built** (`[mem 0x0–0x9fbff]` + `[0x100000–0xfffffff]` = 256 MiB), `NX … active`, `found SMP MP-table at [mem 0x0009fc00-…]`, `RAMDISK: [mem 0x0ff3a000-0x0fffffff]` (our initramfs), `MPTABLE: OEM ID: HYDO  Product ID: MPTB`, `MPTABLE: APIC at: 0xFEE00000`. It then stops at the **expected Phase-6 wall**: `EPT-VIOLATION (reason 48) gpa=0xfee00020 qual=0x181(read) rip=0xffffffff81083496` — `apic_read(APIC_ID)` on the LAPIC MMIO page. All M3–M13 regressions pass, dmesg-clean, clean unload, **no L1 restarts** (a triple-fault is a clean recoverable VM-exit, not a wedge). Notes: `HVX_CONSOLE_SZ` is still 4096 (this boot used 2826; a longer boot will truncate the console — bump it in Phase 6); the UART emulation does not track the 16550 DLAB, so a couple of divisor-latch bytes may appear as junk before real text (harmless). Loader added to `deploy-hvx.sh`'s build list; run manually in L1: `sudo ./hvx_linux /tmp/bzImage /tmp/initramfs.cpio.gz`.

### Milestone 15 — DONE (real Linux boots to the interactive initramfs shell prompt as L2; verified in L1) — Phase 6 of the L2-Linux road

**A real Linux kernel now boots all the way to the busybox `~ #` shell prompt under hvx**, past the M14 LAPIC-MMIO wall and through the timer, initramfs, and userspace `/init`. The console captured the complete `/init` output — `hydo: initramfs reached — userspace is alive!`, the `uname` banner, `hydo-boot-marker: launching shell on ttyS0`, and finally the interactive prompt `~ #` (the shell then blocks waiting for serial input we do not yet provide — that is deferred P6.4, not a failure). No panic; the guest idle-ticks cleanly afterward. Approach (per `hvx/PHASE6_DESIGN.md`): **force x2APIC and emulate the entire Local APIC through the existing RDMSR/WRMSR trap** (MSRs 0x800–0x8FF) — no LAPIC MMIO, no shadow pages, no APICv. The changes, each found by running to a wall and decoding the fault:

1. **Force x2APIC + known TSC freq** (`hvx_cpuid`): CPUID.01H:ECX bit 21 (x2APIC) + bit 24 (TSC-Deadline); raise max-basic-leaf to 0x16 and add CPUID.15H (crystal 25 MHz × 64 ⁄ 1 = 1.6 GHz) + CPUID.16H so Linux takes the *known-TSC-frequency* path (no PIT calibration busy-wait — this also sidesteps the KVM-path RDTSC calibration wall). `IA32_APIC_BASE` (0x1B) returns `0xFEE00D00` (BSP|EXTD|EN) so `check_x2apic()` sees "enabled by BIOS". Add `x2apic_phys` to the loader cmdline.
2. **Present a (feature-less) KVM hypervisor** (`hvx_cpuid`): CPUID.01H:ECX bit 31 + leaf 0x40000000 = `"KVMKVMKVM"` + leaf 0x40000001 = 0. WITHOUT this, `enable_IR_x2apic()` finds no interrupt-remapping and — because `x86_init.hyper.x2apic_available()` is false on "bare metal" — **disables x2APIC and falls back to (unmapped) xAPIC MMIO → guest #PF → panic**. The KVM hook makes it keep x2APIC; the empty feature leaf means no kvm-clock/PV-EOI/etc. path is taken.
3. **LAPIC / IOAPIC / 8259 / 16550 device model** (`struct hvx_lapic`/`hvx_ioapic`/`hvx_uart`): x2APIC registers are read-as-written except ID/VERSION/PPR/LDR/CCR (computed read-only) and EOI/ICR/SELF-IPI (side effects). The **IOAPIC at 0xFEC00000** stays EPT-unmapped and its accesses trap as EPT-violations we decode in-kernel (skip prefixes/REX, confirm MOV `0x89`/`0x8b`, map ModRM.reg → `hvx_regs`; instruction bytes fetched via the `rip − 0xffffffff80000000` kernel-text shortcut). Redirection entries stay masked. PIC ports 0x20/0x21/0xA0/0xA1 store/return the IMR masks. The **16550** models DLAB (so divisor writes are not captured as console bytes), a carrier-present MSR (`0xb0` = DCD|DSR|CTS — without DCD the tty hangs up and the shell's stdin EOFs → init exits), and an IIR that reports THR-empty (`0x02`) while THRI is enabled so the serial ISR actually transmits.
4. **TSC-deadline timer → M8 injection** (run-loop): a write to `IA32_TSC_DEADLINE` (0x6E0) arms it; when the virtual TSC reaches the deadline the LVT-timer vector is injected via the existing instruction-timed path. In the free-run COUNTER mode used for bring-up, virtual time also creeps one `tsc_step` per exit while armed, and an idle **HLT jumps time straight to the deadline** (so a tickless idle does not stall). Two required injection fixes: **lifted `INTR_VECTOR_MAX` 0x20 → 0xFF** (Linux installs a 256-vector IDT; the timer vector is ~0xEC) while keeping the module's own IDT at 0x20 (`IDT_MAX_GATE`); and an **interruptibility gate** — inject only when `GUEST_RFLAGS.IF=1` and no STI/MOV-SS shadow, else set PROCBASED interrupt-window exiting and handle `EXIT_REASON_INTERRUPT_WINDOW` (reason 7).
5. **Load the guest's SYSCALL / KERNEL_GS_BASE MSRs into hardware around the run** (`hvx_run_vcpu`): `SYSCALL` and `swapgs` do NOT VM-exit and are NOT in the VMCS, so STAR/LSTAR/CSTAR/SFMASK/KERNEL_GS_BASE (kept in `sw_msr[]` since M12) must be written to the *physical* MSRs before entry and the host's restored after — otherwise the first userspace→kernel transition (init's first syscall/page-fault) **double-faults in `error_entry`**. `wrmsrl_safe` is used because a guest MSR (e.g. hvx_cpuid_test's scratch LSTAR) may be non-canonical and would #GP the host.
6. **Minimal LAPIC in-service + EOI** (`struct hvx_lapic.in_service`): at most one interrupt is injected until the guest writes the EOI MSR (0x80B), so we never deliver a second device vector nested inside a running handler (which corrupted Linux's IRQ state). `HVX_INJECT` resets it (the M8 module ISR never EOIs); it rides in the snapshot.

Also: `HVX_CONSOLE_SZ` 4096 → 65536 (a full boot log is ~6 KiB); `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` decoupled from the struct size in its `_IOC` number (64 KiB exceeds the 14-bit `_IOC` size field); the snapshot struct kzalloc'd in the ioctl handlers (frame-size); LAPIC/IOAPIC/PIC/UART-adjacent state folded into `struct hvx_snapshot` (P6.5). All M3–M13 regressions pass (insmod/rmmod rc=0), dmesg-clean, no L1 restarts. `hvx_cpuid_test.c` updated for the new CPUID bits/leaves. **Deferred:** P6.4 (serial *input* IRQ → interactive typing) and P6.6 (single-step ICOUNT determinism / `boot-verify` past the RDTSC wall).

### Milestone 16 — DONE (whole-boot determinism + branch-from-snapshot on real Linux; verified in L1) — Phase 7 of the L2-Linux road

**The M15 boot is now a rigorous, formal determinism result: hvx boots real Linux 6.6.35 to the initramfs shell so that (1) two boots from scratch are BIT-IDENTICAL in FULL state, and (2) branch-from-snapshot on the running OS reproduces the boot bit-for-bit.** New userspace harness `hvx/hvx_boot_verify.c` (models `src/verify.rs`/`src/state.rs`/`src/detlinux.rs`) boots the staged bzImage+initramfs in FAST free-run (`HVX_RUN`, COUNTER-mode virtual TSC), stops at a well-defined marker (run/drain `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` until it contains `hydo-boot-marker: launching shell on ttyS0`), and computes a canonical FNV-1a fingerprint over the FULL end state — the reconstructed console bytes + the `HVX_GET_SNAPSHOT` struct (meaningful fields, fixed order, no raw struct/padding) + all 256 MiB of guest RAM (via mmap). Result: two fresh boots and a mid-boot branch all fingerprint to the **same** value `1bdf266a0db52bf7`, and the two boot consoles are byte-for-byte equal (`cmp` empty). This proves determinism all the way THROUGH the RDTSC-calibration region (`native_calibrate_cpu_early`) that walled hydo under KVM — hvx owns RDTSC, so there is no wall there. The shared bzImage-loader setup was factored out of `hvx_linux.c` into `hvx/hvx_boot.h` (the byte-for-byte `boot.rs`/`detlinux.rs` recipe) so both clients boot the identical guest; `hvx_linux.c` now just `#include`s it + `main()`.

**Three determinism fixes were required (the key findings — each hunted down by diffing two boots' full state, then their consoles byte-for-byte):**
1. **Deterministic THROTTLE cadence** (`hvx_run_vcpu`). In free-run the guest only returns to userspace at a `HVX_EXIT_THROTTLE` boundary, so "stop when the console first shows marker X" actually stops at the first THROTTLE at/after X — and that boundary must be a deterministic function of the guest or the overshoot (hence RAM/regs at the stop) jitters. The single `guard` counter used to bump on external-interrupt and interrupt-window exits too, and those arrive at nondeterministic host-driven times. Split it: `guard` now counts ONLY retired (deterministic) exits and triggers THROTTLE at `HVX_MAX_INKERNEL_EXITS` (1<<16); a separate, larger `safety` cap (`HVX_SAFETY_CAP` = 1<<20) bounds total iterations against a pathological interrupt storm (host interrupts are already serviced INLINE via the `sti;nop;cli` window, so a large cap is safe and, during a normal boot, the retired cadence always fires first). THROTTLE boundaries now land at deterministic icounts.
2. **Deterministic virtual-TSC creep** (`hvx_run_vcpu`) — the dominant bug. In COUNTER mode `vm->tsc` crept `+= step` once per run-loop ITERATION while a LAPIC deadline was armed, at the loop top — which meant a nondeterministic external-interrupt iteration crept `vm->tsc` at a nondeterministic icount, so the deadline was crossed one instruction early/late and the LAPIC timer injected at a run-dependent instruction. That shifted jiffies/`sched_clock`, which showed up first as a differing printk timestamp (`[    9.977915]` vs `[    9.977587]`) and cascaded into RAM/rdtsc_count. Fix: the deadline CHECK-and-inject stays at the loop top, but the creep MUTATION moved into the RETIRED block (once per retired exit while a deadline is armed), so `vm->tsc` is a pure function of `icount` and external interrupts never move it. (The RDTSC handler keeps its own per-call `+= step` for the no-deadline tests; those never arm `tsc_deadline`, so the new creep is inert for M3–M13.)
3. **UART register latch in the snapshot** (`struct hvx_uart` → `struct hvx_snapshot`). M15 added the 16550 model (ier/lcr/mcr/fcr/scr/dll/dlm) to the VM but not to the snapshot, even though it persists across `HVX_RUN` and drives how the next guest UART access is decoded (DLAB, THRI). Added the seven bytes to the snapshot ABI + GET/SET handlers + the fingerprint — a genuine completeness fix so a branch taken mid-serial-programming reproduces subsequent UART behavior. (This is a correct addition to the deterministic device state; the two changes demonstrably required to make both boots identical for this workload were #1 and #2.)

Harness details worth keeping: the guest console is reconstructed in USERSPACE (the module's own console buffer is never reset by `HVX_SET_SNAPSHOT`, so the branch rewinds a userspace watermark and appends only newly-produced bytes); the branch mid-point marker is `Trying to unpack rootfs image as initramfs` (deep in kernel init, timer armed, initramfs about to unpack — a ~3 KB / heavy-execution continuation that stresses the snapshot). All M3–M13 regressions pass (insmod/rmmod rc=0), dmesg-clean, no L1 restarts. `hvx_boot_verify` added to `deploy-hvx.sh`'s build list; run manually in L1 (`sudo ./hvx_boot_verify /tmp/bzImage /tmp/initramfs.cpio.gz`).

**Scope honesty:** this is WHOLE-BOOT (free-run) determinism, not instruction-exact bisection. Single-stepping the ~10^8 boot instructions (one VM-exit each) is infeasible without a PMU fast instruction clock (the next milestone), and it is moot here because free-run is already fully deterministic — there is no divergence to bisect. **Next milestone (clearly scoped): port `src/pmu.rs`'s `step_insn_fast` — a guest-only PMU instruction counter (INST_RETIRED.ANY) with overflow/descheduling handling — to give hvx a FAST instruction clock.** That would (a) make single-step ICOUNT boots tractable, enabling instruction-exact divergence bisection à la `src/detlinux.rs`, and (b) let the boot run under `HVX_TSC_ICOUNT` (TSC = base + icount·step) so the virtual TSC is tied to true retired instructions rather than the COUNTER free-run approximation. Expected next walls once instruction-exact: the LAPIC-timer phase relationship and jitter-entropy.

### Milestone 17 — BLOCKED by hardware: the L0 CPU is HYBRID and KVM refuses a guest PMU on hybrid silicon — NOT started in-kernel

The PMU fast instruction clock (the M16 "next milestone") is **blocked at the hardware+kernel level on this box — it is NOT a config we can flip.** The L0 CPU is a **13th Gen Intel Core i7-13700HX (Raptor Lake HYBRID: P-cores + E-cores)**; `/sys/devices/` shows both a `cpu_core` and a `cpu_atom` PMU, and dmesg reports `Alderlake Hybrid events`. KVM 6.6.35 contains a hardcoded gate in `kvm_init_pmu_capability()` — `if (cpu_feature_enabled(X86_FEATURE_HYBRID_CPU)) enable_pmu = false;` — so it deliberately disables guest-PMU virtualization on hybrid parts (the gate exists because hybrid P-core/E-core counters are unreliable to virtualize). The mandatory capability probe — `hvx/hvx_pmu_probe.c`, run inside L1 — shows the nested L1 vCPU has **no architectural PMU at all**, so neither PMU mechanism (the VMCS load/save `IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL` controls, nor host-counter save/restore around VMRESUME) has anything to count with. No kernel change was made (`hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h` stay at the M16 module, byte-identical); per the milestone's own "capability-probe-first / do-not-force / do-not-risk-L1" discipline, I STOPPED at evidence instead of shipping an untestable fast-path.

**Proven not-a-config-toggle (2026-08-12):** the `kvm` module was successfully unloaded and reloaded with the flag — `rmmod kvm_intel` (rc=0), `rmmod kvm` (rc=0), `modprobe kvm enable_pmu=1` (rc=0), `modprobe kvm_intel` (rc=0) — and `/sys/module/kvm/parameters/enable_pmu` **still read `N`** afterward, with no kvm dmesg line. KVM accepted `enable_pmu=1` and forced it back to false at init because the CPU is hybrid. So no module parameter, kernel cmdline, or `modprobe.d` setting can enable it here.

**Exact evidence (all read inside L1):**
- **The VMX controls DO exist** (so the design is viable once a vPMU appears): with TRUE ctls in use (`IA32_VMX_BASIC` bit 55 = 1), VM-exit "load `IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL`" (exit-ctls **bit 12**) and VM-entry "load `IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL`" (entry-ctls **bit 13**) are both in the allowed-1 half (`IA32_VMX_TRUE_EXIT_CTLS=0x007fffff00036dfb`, `IA32_VMX_TRUE_ENTRY_CTLS=0x0000f3ff000011fb`). The VMX-preemption timer is also available (`IA32_VMX_MISC=0x20000165`, rate 5) if ever wanted as a coarse burst bound.
- **But there is no counter:** `CPUID.0AH = {eax=0,ebx=0,ecx=0,edx=0}` in L1 → PerfMon version 0, 0 fixed counters, 0 GP counters. Every architectural PMU MSR **#GPs on read** in L1: `IA32_FIXED_CTR0 (0x309)`, `IA32_FIXED_CTR1 (0x30a)`, `IA32_FIXED_CTR_CTRL (0x38d)`, `IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL (0x38f)`, `IA32_PERF_CAPABILITIES (0x345)` all return EIO via `/dev/cpu/0/msr`.
- **Root cause: hybrid CPU + KVM's hardcoded hybrid gate (see the header).** `/sys/module/kvm/parameters/enable_pmu = N` and stays `N` after a successful reload with `enable_pmu=1` (proof above) because `kvm_init_pmu_capability()` forces it off on `X86_FEATURE_HYBRID_CPU`. So KVM zeroes guest `CPUID.0AH` and faults the PMU MSRs regardless of QEMU's `pmu=on`. (L0 itself HAS a working PMU — `arch_perfmon` in `/proc/cpuinfo`, full-width counters, `nmi_watchdog=1` consuming one GP counter — this is purely KVM's guest-vPMU-on-hybrid gate.) Note: L0's `kvm` is **shared** with unrelated `qemu-system` TCG jobs (TCG does not use KVM, but coordinate before any reload).

**What was changed (harmless, and half the fix):** `hvx/run-l1.sh` now launches L1 with `-cpu host,+vmx,pmu=on` (was `-cpu host,+vmx`). `pmu=on` is the QEMU-side request for a guest vPMU; it is **necessary but not sufficient** — KVM ignores it while `enable_pmu=N`, so L1 is byte-for-byte the same environment (verified: **all M3–M13 regressions still pass**, insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg-clean, no L1 restart, under the `pmu=on` sandbox). Once the host enables the vPMU, `pmu=on` makes it live automatically. `hvx/hvx_pmu_probe.c` is kept as the reusable capability probe (msr-based, standalone; not wired into `deploy-hvx.sh` since it needs `/dev/cpu/msr`, not `/dev/hvx`).

**Options (pick one to proceed):**
1. **Move hvx development onto a NON-HYBRID host** (Xeon, or a pre-Alder-Lake Core, or any CPU without `X86_FEATURE_HYBRID_CPU`) where KVM exposes the guest vPMU. Then re-run `hvx_pmu_probe` (expect `SUPPORTED: YES`, CPUID.0AH version ≥ 2, ≥ 1 fixed counter) and implement the VMCS load/save-`PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL` guest-only clock. The VMX controls are already confirmed present, so the design is directly implementable there. **Caveat to validate even then:** nested KVM must correctly count L2-retired instructions into L1's virtual `IA32_FIXED_CTR0` across the module's own VM-entry/exit — nested vPMU has historically been incomplete, so the M5 201-instruction MTF ground-truth cross-check (`PMU-count == MTF-count == 201`) is the first thing to run, and could reveal a second wall (miscount under nesting).
2. **Patch host KVM to drop the hybrid gate and rebuild/reload it.** Needs L0 root, building `kvm.ko` for 6.6.35 on NixOS, and is UNSUPPORTED — the gate exists because hybrid vPMU counts are unreliable, i.e. this walks straight into the miscount caveat above. Not recommended.
3. **Declare the fast clock blocked on this platform and stay on the exact MTF clock (RECOMMENDED for this box).** The MTF instruction clock (M5) is already exact and deterministic — it is only slow (one VM-exit per instruction, ~10^8 exits for a boot). Instruction-exact divergence bisection on Linux stays out of reach until (1), but whole-boot free-run determinism (M16) does NOT need it and is unaffected.

Recommendation: **Option 3** on this box (the CPU is hybrid; there is no toggle), and **Option 1** — a non-hybrid host — whenever the goal is instruction-exact bisection. The `-cpu host,+vmx,pmu=on` change in `run-l1.sh` is harmless and becomes live automatically on a host that can grant the vPMU.

### Milestone 18 — DONE (serial input / interactive Linux shell — Phase 6.4; verified in L1)

**The real-Linux L2 shell that M15 booted (output-only) is now INTERACTIVE: input reaches the guest and echoes, and typed commands run.** M15/M16 reached the busybox `~ #` prompt but the shell then blocked reading `ttyS0` with no input path. M18 adds the UART receive side and wires COM1's IRQ through the M8 instruction-timed injection, so a real `echo hydo-interactive` typed (or scripted) at the prompt runs and its output appears. This is purely additive: with an **empty** input queue the whole RX path is inert, so the M16 whole-boot determinism fingerprint is **unchanged** (`1bdf266a0db52bf7`).

The four pieces (`hvx/hvx.c`, `hvx/hvx_abi.h`, `hvx/hvx_linux.c`):
1. **Input ring + `HVX_PUT_INPUT`.** `struct hvx_vm` gains a small ring buffer (`input_buf[4096]`, free-running `input_head`/`input_tail`, `count = tail - head`); a new ioctl `HVX_PUT_INPUT` (`struct hvx_input {len; buf[256]}`, ABI cmd 14) pushes up to 256 bytes/call from userspace. The ring is fed only from the ioctl path and drained only inside the run-loop; `HVX_RUN` holds the VM mutex for its whole duration, so push and pop never overlap and no locking is needed.
2. **16550 RX model in `hvx_pio`.** A guest `IN` from RBR (0x3f8, DLAB=0) now pops the next queued byte (returns 0 if empty), which clears the RX interrupt cause; **LSR bit 0 (Data Ready)** reflects queue-non-empty; **IIR** reports received-data-available (0x04, priority) when a byte is queued AND ERBFI (IER bit 0) is set, else falls back to the prior THR-empty (0x02) / none (0x01) logic. The TX/console path is untouched.
3. **COM1 IRQ via `redir[4]`.** The old level-triggered `uart_tx_irq` became `uart_irq`, which asserts IRQ4 when EITHER THRI (TX, IER bit 1) OR ERBFI+data-ready (RX, IER bit 0) holds — the routed vector is IOAPIC redirection entry 4 (ISA IRQ4 → COM1 per the MP table), delivered through the existing M8 path with the same interruptibility gate + interrupt-window exiting + LAPIC in-service/EOI as the TSC-deadline timer. **CRUCIAL gate (kept):** it asserts nothing until the guest has unmasked `redir[4]` with a real vector (≥ 0x20) — a spurious IRQ4 before the 8250 driver is ready corrupts Linux's IRQ state (the M15 in-service note). Because `uart_rx_pending` is false with an empty ring, `uart_irq` is byte-identical to the old `uart_tx_irq` on the no-input boot.
4. **Loader (`hvx_linux.c`).** Two input modes: **interactive** (stdin is a TTY → `cfmakeraw` + `O_NONBLOCK`; each loop iteration forwards available stdin bytes via `HVX_PUT_INPUT` while still draining `HVX_GET_CONSOLE`; terminal restored on exit) and **scripted** (env `HVX_INPUT="cmd\n"`, C-escapes honoured → once the `~ #` prompt appears in the console, the string is fed once and the loop watches for `HVX_EXPECT`, default `hydo-interactive`). The demo uses scripted because a live TTY over the double remote-login hop is unreliable; live typing is the identical `HVX_PUT_INPUT` code path. Feeding **after** the prompt avoids the 8250 startup RBR-flush eating a byte.

**Determinism / snapshot decision (load-bearing):** the input ring is **TRANSIENT host state, deliberately NOT in `struct hvx_snapshot` or the fingerprint** (exactly like the console buffer). Host input arrives at nondeterministic times, so folding it in would make it nondeterministic; keeping it out means (a) a no-input boot is bit-identical to M16, and (b) the RX path only ever perturbs execution when a human/script actually feeds bytes. The M16 fingerprint `1bdf266a0db52bf7` was re-verified unchanged (see below), so no baseline changed.

**Proof (all in L1, one clean run, zero L1 restarts):**
- **Unit test `hvx_input_test.c`** (added to `deploy-hvx.sh`): pushes `"PING\n"` via `HVX_PUT_INPUT`; a self-contained guest polls LSR.DR, reads RBR, and echoes each byte to the console (THR). Asserts the echoed console == `"PING\n"`. **PASS.**
- **End-to-end demo** (`hvx/run-input-demo.sh`, host-side): boots the NixOS 6.6.35 bzImage+initramfs via `hvx_linux` with `HVX_INPUT='echo hydo-interactive\n'`. Console shows the shell echo the typed command and run it:
  ```
  hydo-boot-marker: launching shell on ttyS0
  ~ # echo hydo-interactive
  hydo-interactive
  ~ #
  ```
  `hvx_linux rc=0`, "DEMO PASS: guest shell consumed the input and produced \"hydo-interactive\"" — the standalone `hydo-interactive` output line proves the shell parsed AND executed the fed command, not merely echoed it.
- **Regressions:** all M3–M13 tests PASS (`deploy-hvx.sh load`: insmod rc=0, every `hvx_*_test` PASS incl. the new `hvx_input_test`, rmmod rc=0, host dmesg clean). **`hvx_boot_verify` still fingerprints `1bdf266a0db52bf7`** — whole-boot `fp1==fp2` IDENTICAL and branch `fp_a==fp_b` IDENTICAL — so M16 determinism is fully preserved. dmesg-clean, clean unload, no L1 restarts.

**Deferred (unchanged):** P6.6 (single-step ICOUNT determinism / instruction-exact `boot-verify`) still needs the PMU fast clock, which is BLOCKED on this hybrid box (M17). Nothing here touches that.

### Milestone 19 — DONE (deterministic SMP / multi-vCPU; verified + stress-tested in L1)

**hvx now runs multiple vCPUs in ONE VM DETERMINISTICALLY.** You cannot run vCPUs in parallel on physical cores and stay deterministic — real concurrent memory interleaving is nondeterministic. Following the state of the art (rr, Hermit), hvx **serializes the vCPUs onto one execution context and interleaves them by a deterministic QUANTUM measured in the MTF instruction clock**: userspace runs vCPU0 for Q instructions (single-step), then vCPU1 for Q, round-robin. The interleaving becomes a pure function of the per-vCPU instruction counts, so it reproduces bit-for-bit; changing Q changes the interleaving in a still-reproducible way. Because MTF is exact but slow (one VM-exit per instruction), this milestone targets **small hand-written SMP guests, NOT a full Linux SMP boot** (see honest scope).

**Per-vCPU / shared-VM split (the refactor — the main risk, and it landed byte-clean):** `struct hvx_vm` was split into a shared VM plus an array of `struct hvx_vcpu` (`hvx.c`). **Per-vCPU (private, N≥1):** VMCS + VMXON pages, the register file, `struct hvx_sregs`, the deterministic virtual TSC (`tsc`/`step`/`aux`/`mode`/`rdtsc_count`), the instruction clock `icount`, the pending instruction-timed injection, the curated software MSR store (`sw_msr[]` — these are per-CPU MSRs: SYSCALL STAR/LSTAR/…, GS base), and its own x2APIC (`struct hvx_lapic`, ID = vCPU index). **Shared (per-VM):** guest RAM + EPT (one physical address space), the serial console, the serial input ring, and the IOAPIC / 8259 PIC / 16550 UART device model. Every `struct hvx_vcpu` carries a `->vm` back-pointer; the ~15 exit-handler/`setup_vmcs`/`save_sregs`/`hvx_run_vcpu` helpers now take a `struct hvx_vcpu *` and reach shared state through `->vm`. **Only ONE vCPU is ever in VMX-root at a time** (serialized on the pinned host CPU), so cross-vCPU writes (an IPI landing on another vCPU's `inject_*`) are race-free with no locking beyond the existing per-fd mutex.

**ABI (backward-compatible; `hvx_abi.h`):** `HVX_CREATE_VM`'s `struct hvx_config` gained `__u32 n_vcpus` (0 or 1 ⇒ 1 vCPU; cap `HVX_MAX_VCPUS`=8). A new ioctl `HVX_SET_VCPU` (cmd 15, `__u32` index) selects the **current vCPU** — the single-fd analogue of KVM's per-vCPU fd — and every per-vCPU ioctl (`SET/GET_REGS`, `SET/GET_TSC`, `SET/GET_SREGS`, `GET/SET_SNAPSHOT`, `INJECT`, `RUN`, `RUNC`) targets it (default 0). Userspace drives the schedule explicitly: `HVX_SET_VCPU(i)` then `HVX_RUNC(single_step, max_insns=Q)`, round-robin. **N=1 is byte-for-byte the old behavior** (a single-vCPU VM never calls `HVX_SET_VCPU`, so all pre-M19 clients operate on vCPU 0; `struct hvx_snapshot` is unchanged — GET/SET_SNAPSHOT capture vCPU 0's per-state + the shared device state exactly as before).

**IPI delivery (implemented):** a vCPU writing the x2APIC ICR (MSR 0x830) with a fixed/lowest-priority delivery mode now routes a deterministic instruction-timed injection to the TARGET vCPU(s) — physical destination (`dest` = target x2APIC ID = vCPU index) and the self / all / all-but-self shorthands are honored (`hvx_lapic_send_ipi`). The target's injection is scheduled at ITS current `icount` ("deliver on the target's next slice"), a reproducible point given the fixed round-robin, reusing the M8 injection path (interruptibility gate + LAPIC in-service). **N=1 self-IPI is preserved bit-identically:** a single-vCPU VM keeps the exact pre-M19 UP rule (a fixed/lowest-priority ICR write self-injects regardless of destination/shorthand), so the boot is unaffected. INIT/SIPI/NMI delivery modes are **not** modeled — there is no hardware AP bringup; the SMP test starts each vCPU directly at its entry point (honest scope).

**Proof — `hvx_smp_test.c` (2 vCPUs sharing RAM):** (1) **shared-counter race** — both vCPUs do K=20 NON-ATOMIC increments (`mov (%rbx),%eax; inc; mov %eax,(%rbx); dec; jnz; hlt`) of one shared counter; the load-modify-store straddles quantum boundaries, so the number of lost updates is a function of Q. Result: **Q=200 (serialized) → counter 40 = 2K; Q=1 (finest interleave) → counter 20 = K.** **(a) reproducible:** same Q run twice is byte-identical (fp `27ead8c880ef7820` for Q=200, `88ee51bd9f8e40e0` for Q=1, over both vCPUs' regs+icount + shared RAM). **(b) the quantum deterministically controls the result:** the two Q values give different (but each reproducible) fingerprints and the exact predicted counters (40 vs 20). (2) **cross-vCPU IPI** — vCPU0 writes the ICR to send vector 0x20 to APIC ID 1; vCPU1 runs an interruptible loop; **(c)** the module's ISR fires exactly once on vCPU1 (`handler_count=1`), vCPU1 still finishes its own work via IRETQ (`vcpu1_progress=20`), and the whole final state is reproducible (fp `d042bb01201c269d`). "MILESTONE 19 PASS".

**Regressions (all green, one clean run, ZERO L1 restarts):** every M3–M13 + M18 test passes via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean, no BUG/oops/taint). **The M16 whole-boot determinism fingerprint is PRESERVED: `hvx_boot_verify` still reports `1bdf266a0db52bf7`** — whole-boot `fp1==fp2` IDENTICAL and branch `fp_a==fp_b` IDENTICAL — and the M18 interactive shell demo still passes (`hydo-interactive` echoed and executed), confirming the single-vCPU real-Linux path is byte-for-byte unchanged. Clean unload. (One bug found and fixed during bring-up: an early `seed_vcpu` zeroed the just-allocated per-vCPU VMXON/VMCS pages → NULL write in `hvx_run_vcpu`; the fix is that `seed_vcpu` seeds only software state and never the page allocations.)

**Honest scope:** this is the deterministic multi-vCPU FOUNDATION proven on controlled guests — serialized execution + instruction-quantum round-robin + reproducible interleaving + cross-vCPU IPIs. Deterministic SMP boot of real Linux is NOT the target: MTF-serialized SMP boot is far too slow without the (M17-BLOCKED) PMU fast clock, and it needs full INIT/SIPI AP bringup plus per-CPU LAPIC-timer/spinlock correctness. AP-startup (INIT/SIPI) is intentionally unmodeled; device (UART) IRQs are routed to the BSP (vCPU 0) only, keeping device-IRQ targeting deterministic under SMP. Snapshot/branch on the SMP guest was left as the deferred bonus (per-vCPU snapshot works — GET/SET_SNAPSHOT are vCPU-indexed — but the multi-vCPU branch harness is not written).

### Milestone 20 — DONE (deterministic virtio-PCI block device / disk; verified + stress-tested in L1)

hvx now has a **deterministic virtio-blk block device** — a snapshot-able backing disk whose I/O completion fires at an EXACT instruction, delivered through the M8 injection path. This is the first real device (beyond the console/serial-input) and the PCI-bus infrastructure it rides on is reusable for future virtio-net / NVMe / real distro images (matching what bhyve does). Two design decisions drove the work: (1) the virtio-blk CORE (virtqueue protocol, request servicing, backing image, deterministic completion, snapshot) is **transport-agnostic**; PCI is just the discovery/transport layer on top; (2) the novel/risky determinism (instruction-timed completion) was **validated in isolation FIRST** (Phase A), then the PCI plumbing was layered on (Phase B), so a fingerprint divergence localizes to the completion logic vs. PCI enumeration.

**The virtio-blk core (`struct hvx_vblk` in `struct hvx_vm`, shared per-VM like the console/IOAPIC/UART).** One split virtqueue is serviced against an in-memory backing image (`HVX_SET_DISK`/`HVX_GET_DISK`, ≤ 1 MiB, `kvmalloc`'d, snapshot-able alongside guest RAM exactly like the mmap'd RAM — GET/SET around a snapshot rewinds the disk). `virtio_blk_process` (the deterministic core) runs synchronously at a kick: it reads `avail->idx`, walks each descriptor chain `[header][data…][status]` (via a page-wise gpa→host copy `hvx_guest_rw`), copies data between guest RAM and the disk (`VIRTIO_BLK_T_IN`/`_OUT` at `sector·512`), writes the status byte + the used-ring entry, advances the cursors, then raises the virtio ISR and **schedules the completion at `icount + HVX_VBLK_LATENCY` (16 insns)**. That completion becomes an injected interrupt at a run-loop **loop-top check** (mirroring the LAPIC-timer discipline exactly): the scheduling was set in a retired IO/MMIO exit, so `completion_due` is a pure function of the instruction clock and a host-interrupt iteration cannot shift the delivery instruction (the M16 creep-discipline). The device is **INERT until a disk is attached** (`disk == NULL`): PCI config returns "no device" (0xffffffff), the PIO/MMIO register windows return false, and no IRQ ever asserts — so a VM that never attaches a disk (the boot!) is byte-identical and the M16 fingerprint is preserved.

**Phase A — deterministic completion, ISOLATED from PCI (MUST-HAVE, `hvx_virtio_iso_test.c`).** The device is driven by the SIMPLEST possible kick: a bare **PIO doorbell** (a tiny test-only register block at ports 0x500–0x52f — queue GPAs / size / ready / completion-vector / NOTIFY / ISR / capacity), so the completion determinism is proven with zero PCI machinery. The completion is delivered as a **direct one-shot** (the `irq_vec` the guest programmed) via the M8 injection slot. A hand-written guest submits a WRITE (sector 5) **and** a READ (sector 2) in one kick, waits for the completion ISR, and HLTs. Proof: data round-trips through the image (write landed on disk sector 5; read pulled disk sector 2 into a guest buffer, both status OK), the completion IRQ fires exactly once at a reproducible instruction (icount 43), and **replay / round-trip / branch-from-snapshot all fingerprint-equal** (`7e586473f6e494d7`) over an FNV hash of regs + device state + guest RAM + the disk image (branch snapshots mid-flight — after the kick, before the completion fires — proving the pending completion + disk are captured). **Bug found & fixed:** the direct one-shot uses the module's built-in ISR, which never EOIs, so `lapic.in_service` stayed set after run #1 and the arming block's `in_service==0` gate blocked the completion on every subsequent run (replay hung). Fix: the direct completion is fire-and-forget (like `HVX_INJECT`), so it clears `in_service` when it arms — not gated on a prior EOI.

**Phase B — PCI transport ON TOP (TARGET, `hvx_virtio_pci_test.c`).** Standard **0xCF8/0xCFC PCI config space** (extended into `hvx_pio`) exposes ONE function: virtio vendor `0x1af4`, modern virtio-blk device `0x1042`, class 01/80, a 32-bit MMIO **BAR0** at `0xfe000000`, and the **modern virtio-PCI capability list** (common/notify/ISR/device cfg). BAR0 is EPT-unmapped, so guest accesses trap as EPT-violations decoded by a general MMIO MOV decoder (`hvx_virtio_mmio`: prefixes/REX/0x66, opcodes 0x88/0x89/0x8a/0x8b, sizes 1/2/4/8; the effective GPA comes from the VMCS). The **modern common-config** (device_status handshake, VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1 feature negotiation, queue_desc/driver/device, queue_enable) feeds the SAME core; a write to the notify region kicks it. The device's **INTx is routed through the existing M15 IOAPIC** (redirection entry `HVX_VBLK_GSI`=11 → vector → the M8 injection path), delivered by a level source `vblk_irq` gated on `in_service==0`; the guest's own ISR (overwriting `GPA_ISR`) acks the device by reading the virtio ISR register (read-to-clear) and EOIs the LAPIC. A hand-written guest **enumerates** config space (reads vendor:device = `0x10421af4`, BAR0 = `0xfe000000`), enables the device, programs IOAPIC redir[11], negotiates features, sets up the virtqueue, does the same write+read, and takes the completion INTx. Proof: enumeration + data round-trip + one completion (icount 112) + **replay / round-trip / branch all fingerprint-equal** (`3f95ef833747f3ec`). **Bug found & fixed:** the module's guest identity page tables only map the low **1 GiB**, but the IOAPIC (0xfec00000) and BAR (0xfe000000) live in the **4th GiB**, so a hand-written guest #PF'd on the first MMIO access and spun in the fault handler into a triple fault (real Linux avoids this by `ioremap`-ing MMIO). Fix: `HVX_SET_DISK` maps the 4th GiB `[3 GiB,4 GiB)` into the guest's page tables via a **1 GiB PDPT leaf** — but ONLY when a disk is attached, so the boot's pristine low-1-GiB tables (and the M16 fingerprint) are untouched.

**Regressions (all green, one clean run, ZERO L1 restarts on the final runs):** all M3–M13 + M18 + M19 tests pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean — no BUG/oops/taint), PLUS the new `hvx_virtio_iso_test` (Phase A) and `hvx_virtio_pci_test` (Phase B). **The M16 whole-boot determinism fingerprint is PRESERVED: `hvx_boot_verify` still reports `1bdf266a0db52bf7`** (whole-boot `fp1==fp2` IDENTICAL, branch `fp_a==fp_b` IDENTICAL) and the M18 interactive shell demo still runs (`hydo-interactive` echoed and executed) — the new device is fully inert when no disk is attached, so the boot is byte-for-byte unchanged. Clean unload. The snapshot ABI grew (device control state appended at the end of `struct hvx_snapshot`; the disk image rides via the separate `HVX_GET/SET_DISK` ioctls), which does not shift older field offsets — the boot fingerprint hashes only fields through `uart_dlm`, so it is unaffected.

**Honest scope / STRETCH deferred:** Phase A (deterministic completion, isolated) was the MUST-HAVE and Phase B (PCI transport, hand-written-guest-verified) the TARGET — both landed cleanly and deterministically. The STRETCH (booting **real Linux** with the virtio-blk disk attached and having `virtio_pci`+`virtio_blk` bind to it) was NOT attempted, and is a genuine follow-on milestone with concrete walls: (a) attaching the device changes the boot's execution (Linux would enumerate/probe it), so it needs its own boot config and would NOT share the no-disk fingerprint; (b) modern `virtio_pci` strongly prefers **MSI-X**, which is not implemented (only INTx-through-IOAPIC); (c) our hand-built MP table has no PCI interrupt-routing entries and the boot runs `acpi=off` (no `_PRT`), so Linux could not route the device's INTx to a GSI. Implementing MSI-X + PCI IRQ routing is the scoped next step for real-distro-image support. **[DONE in M21 via MSI-X.]**

### Milestone 21 — DONE (real Linux uses the virtio-blk disk over MSI-X, deterministically; verified in L1) — the M20 STRETCH

**A real Linux 6.6.35 kernel now enumerates the M20 virtio-blk PCI function, binds `virtio_pci` + `virtio_blk`, creates `/dev/vda`, and READS it — the completion interrupt delivered as an MSI-X directly to the LAPIC — and the whole disk boot is bit-for-bit deterministic.** Console proof: `pci 0000:00:03.0: [1af4:1042]`, `virtio_blk virtio0: [vda] 128 512-byte logical blocks`, then the initramfs `/init` prints the marker it read from sector 0: **`hydo-vblk-read: HYDO-VBLK-DISK-OK-M21-0123456789`**. This is MSI, not INTx, so it sidesteps M20's two walls (no MSI irqdomain need for `_PRT`/MP-table PCI routing under `acpi=off`).

**Part 1 — MSI-X (the state-of-the-art interrupt path; `hvx/hvx.c`, `hvx/hvx_abi.h`).** The virtio-blk PCI function now advertises an **MSI-X capability** at config offset `0x90` (chained onto the end of the virtio-pci cap list: DEVICE cap `next` 0x00→0x90), with the **MSI-X table** at BAR0+0x4000 and **PBA** at BAR0+0x5000 (`HVX_VBLK_BAR_SIZE` 0x4000→0x8000, still a power of two so BAR sizing works). The message control (enable bit15 / function-mask bit14 writable, table-size RO = `HVX_MSIX_ENTRIES-1`) round-trips through PCI config; the table entries `{msg_addr_lo, msg_addr_hi, msg_data, vector_ctrl}` and the common-cfg `msix_config`/`queue_msix_vector` fields are stored per-VM. **Delivery** (`vblk_msix_irq`): when a completion is pending, MSI-X is enabled + unmasked, and the queue has a valid table index, the vector is the entry's `msg_data & 0xff` and it is injected as a **direct, edge-triggered one-shot into the LAPIC** at the deterministic completion instruction — no INTx, no IOAPIC. INTx stays fully working (M20 `hvx_virtio_pci_test`), suppressed only while MSI-X is enabled (per the PCI spec); the run-loop arming block gained MSI-X as a source after INTx, clearing `irq_pending` on delivery (edge). MSI-X control state is folded into `struct hvx_snapshot` (`vblk_msix_control`/`vblk_config_msix`/`vblk_queue_msix`/`vblk_msix_tbl[]`) + GET/SET + the with-disk fingerprint.

**Part 2 — real-Linux integration + three walls found and fixed (each by booting to a wall and decoding it):**
1. **PCI didn't come up at all** — `PCI: Fatal: No config space access function found`. Linux's conf1 probe (`pci_sanity_check`) scans bus 0 and rejects the config mechanism unless it finds a **host-bridge / VGA / Intel-or-Compaq** device; our lone virtio function (vendor `0x1af4`, class `0x0180`) matched none. Fix: present a minimal **Intel host bridge at 00:00.0** (`hvx_pci_read_bridge`: `[8086:1237]`, class `0x060000`), visible only when a disk is attached. Now `pci 0000:00:00.0: [8086:1237]` + `pci 0000:00:03.0: [1af4:1042]` both enumerate and conf1 is accepted.
2. **virtio is built as modules** in this NixOS kernel (`CONFIG_VIRTIO_BLK=m`, `CONFIG_VIRTIO_PCI=m`), and the busybox initramfs has none. `build-linux.sh` now stages a **SEPARATE disk-enabled initramfs** (`build/initramfs-disk.cpio.gz`) that carries the decompressed `.ko`s (virtio, virtio_ring, virtio_pci_legacy_dev, virtio_pci_modern_dev, virtio_pci, virtio_blk) and whose `/init` `insmod`s them in dependency order, then reads `/dev/vda` (`dd if=/dev/vda bs=1 count=32`) and prints the marker. The ORIGINAL no-disk initramfs is left untouched (its boot must stay inert-device identical). `CONFIG_PCI_MSI=y`, so the guest kernel's MSI-X core works under `acpi=off` (MSI is APIC-based, not `_PRT`-based).
3. **The disk read hung** — after `dd` submits the read it idles waiting ONLY on the completion MSI, with **no LAPIC timer armed** (`tsc_deadline == 0`), so the M15 HLT handler (which only advanced the clock when the timer was armed) returned the HLT to userspace and the boot stopped (`guest HLT after 6 exits`). Fix (`hvx_run_vcpu` HLT handler): also honor a pending virtio-blk completion — when the guest idle-HLTs and `completion_armed`, jump the instruction clock forward to `completion_due` so the completion becomes due and the MSI wakes the guest. **This is inert for the no-disk boot** (`completion_armed` is always 0 with no disk, so the condition reduces to the original `tsc_deadline` and the body is unchanged).

Disk attach: `hvx_linux.c` attaches a disk named by the `HVX_DISK` env var, passing **`HVX_DISK_NO_IDMAP`** (new `struct hvx_disk.flags`) so the module does NOT poke its 4th-GiB identity map into the guest tables — a real OS builds its own page tables and `ioremap`s MMIO itself (the module's fixed `GPA_PDPT` slot is the OS's PML4). Also: the device now advertises a nonzero default queue size (`HVX_VBLK_QSIZE`=256, since modern virtio_pci reads `queue_size` before setting it) and the MMIO decode uses the OS-programmed BAR base (`vblk_bar_base`) in case Linux reassigns BAR0.

**Part 3 — determinism (TWO invariants):**
- **With-disk (NEW reproducible baseline `36583d533d7c989b`):** `hvx/hvx_disk_boot_verify.c` boots the disk-enabled image with the disk attached, stops at `hydo-disk-marker: vda read complete`, and fingerprints the FULL end state — console + snapshot (incl. the virtio-blk + MSI-X device state) + all 256 MiB RAM + the backing disk (`HVX_GET_DISK`). Two fresh disk boots AND a branch-from-snapshot **across the disk read** all fingerprint `36583d533d7c989b`. The M16 creep discipline holds: the completion instruction is `kick_icount + HVX_VBLK_LATENCY`, a pure function of the instruction clock, and the MSI vector/dest come from the guest-programmed (deterministic) MSI-X table.
- **No-disk (device inert):** the device is fully inert with no disk (`hvx_virtio_pio`/EPT-BAR/completion/`vblk_msix_irq` all early-return on `!disk`/`!completion_armed`/`!irq_pending`), so the no-disk boot is byte-identical to pre-M21 and stays fully deterministic (`fp1==fp2==branch`). **HONEST NOTE ON THE FINGERPRINT VALUE:** the pre-M21 no-disk value `1bdf266a0db52bf7` was tied to a **one-off, non-reproducible** `initramfs.cpio.gz` blob — `find | cpio | gzip` embeds each file's mtime + a gzip timestamp, so it was never byte-reproducible; that blob only persisted in L1's `/tmp` across sessions and was regenerated during this milestone (810263→810262 bytes), and since the initramfs image is loaded into hashed guest RAM, the value changed. The MODULE is provably inert for the no-disk path (code audit above + continued full determinism). To make this robust, **`build-linux.sh` is now byte-reproducible** (fixed mtimes + sorted names + `gzip -n`), so the no-disk baseline is a STABLE, re-verifiable **`640ece2f900fe5b3`** (and the with-disk `36583d533d7c989b`) — both regenerate identically. `1bdf266a0db52bf7` is not recoverable (its source blob is gone), but the inert-device guarantee it stood for is preserved and now reproducible.

**Regressions (all green, one clean run, ZERO L1 restarts):** all M3–M13 + M18 + M19 + **M20** tests pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean) — including `hvx_virtio_iso_test` **`7e586473f6e494d7`** (Phase A) and `hvx_virtio_pci_test` **`3f95ef833747f3ec`** (Phase B INTx), so the MSI-X additions did not disturb the INTx path (the host bridge sits at dev 0, the tests use dev 3). No-disk `hvx_boot_verify` = `640ece2f900fe5b3` (reproducible), with-disk `hvx_disk_boot_verify` = `36583d533d7c989b` — both whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL. Clean unload. The module load-log string was bumped from "milestone 19" to "milestone 21".

**Deferred / honest scope:** the disk `/init` proves the read RAW (`dd if=/dev/vda`), not via a mounted filesystem (an acceptable proof per the milestone). Writes through `/dev/vda` from real Linux, and a larger backing image (`HVX_DISK_MAX` is 1 MiB), are untested. The MSI-X destination is honored from `msg_addr` but device IRQs still target the BSP (vCPU 0), matching the M19 device-IRQ-on-BSP rule.

### Milestone 22 — DONE (deterministic disk fault injection; verified + stretch-verified on real Linux in L1)

hvx now perturbs the M20/M21 virtio-blk device from a **SEEDED, fully deterministic fault schedule** — the FoundationDB model: same seed → same faults → same result → any bug it surfaces replays bit-for-bit. **The determinism decision (the heart of the milestone):** every fault decision is a PURE FUNCTION of `(fault_seed, per-device request index)` — never wall-clock time or host state — computed with a self-contained **splitmix64 finalizer** keyed by the request index (`fault_roll(seed, k) = splitmix64(seed + (k+1)·GOLDEN)`); the completion-reorder permutation is keyed by `(fault_seed, per-kick index)`.
