The policy + PRNG counters (`fault_req_seq`/`fault_kick_seq`/`fault_count`) ride in `struct hvx_snapshot`, so branch/replay reproduces the identical schedule. **INERT when disabled** (`fault_classes == 0`, the default): the fault path early-outs, touches no state, and the request-servicing code is byte-identical to M21 — so the M20/M21 and boot fingerprints are all preserved.

**ABI (`hvx_abi.h`).** `HVX_SET_FAULT`/`HVX_GET_FAULT` (cmds 18/19, `struct hvx_fault {seed, classes, probability, max_delay, [GET] req_seq, fault_count}`). Fault classes (a bitmask): `HVX_FAULT_DELAY` (push the shared completion out by 1..max_delay extra instructions), `HVX_FAULT_ERROR` (return `VIRTIO_BLK_S_IOERR` + skip the data transfer), `HVX_FAULT_DROP` (WRITE only: ack `S_OK` but do NOT persist — the silent-durability-bug class), `HVX_FAULT_REORDER` (complete a kick's requests in a seeded-permuted used-ring order), `HVX_FAULT_TORN` (WRITE only: persist only the first sector of a multi-sector write). Seven fault fields are appended at the END of `struct hvx_snapshot` (older offsets unchanged; the no-disk/with-disk boot and iso/pci fingerprints hash only fields through `vblk_msix_tbl`, so they are preserved).

**Kernel (`hvx.c`, in `virtio_blk_process` — the ONE deterministic servicing path).** For each request the header is parsed, then `vblk_fault_decide(seed, req_seq, type)` draws one 64-bit roll `h`: if `(h % 100) < probability` a fault applies, chosen from the ENABLED, type-applicable classes in the fixed order DELAY/ERROR/DROP/TORN via `(h>>8) % n` (DELAY latency = `1 + (h>>16) % max_delay`). ERROR sets the status byte to IOERR and skips the disk I/O; DROP/TORN suppress or clip the persist; DELAY sums into the kick's completion latency. The single deterministic completion is scheduled at `kick_icount + HVX_VBLK_LATENCY + Σdelays` (still a pure function of `icount`, so the M16 creep discipline holds). REORDER buffers each request's used-ring entry (cap `HVX_FAULT_REORDER_MAX`=16) and flushes them in a Fisher-Yates permutation seeded per kick — the guest observes completions out of submission order. All fault state is folded into GET/SET_SNAPSHOT.

**Proof — `hvx_fault_test.c` (hand-written guest, PIO doorbell, FNV fingerprint; the test REPLICATES the module PRNG so it predicts the exact schedule).** Six requests in one kick — three writes (sectors 10/11/12) interleaved with three reads (sectors 2/3/4):
- **(a) reproducible under a seed** (seed 0x1234, classes DELAY|ERROR|DROP|REORDER, prob 70, max_delay 8): the observed per-request outcomes (which errored / dropped / were delayed / the used-ring completion order) MATCH the predicted deterministic schedule, and two runs are byte-identical — fingerprint **`91170d4dbf101c5b`**. "Delayed to which icount" is proven exactly: enabling DELAY pushed `completion_due` from 36 (disabled baseline) to 50 — **+14 instructions**, the predicted summed delay.
- **(b) the seed controls the schedule** (seed 0x9abcdef): a DIFFERENT reproducible schedule (3 errors + 2 delays + 1 normal, used-ring order `9 15 6 0 12 3`) and fingerprint **`88f680fc22cc9bb0`** (≠ (a)), with the predicted schedule provably differing from (a)'s.
- **(c) snapshot/branch reproduces the fault schedule**: a mid-flight snapshot (after the kick — `fault_req_seq==6`, completion pending — before it fires) finished, then restored and finished again, both **`91170d4dbf101c5b`** == the full run.
- **(d) faults DISABLED (default) → EXACTLY M20/M21**: all six requests normal, every write persisted, every read returned disk data, all statuses OK, the completion un-delayed (icount 43), the used ring in submission order (`0 3 6 9 12 15`), reproducible — fingerprint **`9090286f3fc59682`**.
- **torn (stretch class)**: a 2-sector write persists only its first sector — verified.

**STRETCH — DONE (real Linux logs a DETERMINISTIC I/O error).** `hvx_linux.c` and `hvx_disk_boot_verify.c` gained an env-gated fault policy (`HVX_FAULT=<seed>`, default class ERROR at prob 100; inert when unset). Booting the M21 disk image under an I/O-error policy (seed 0x2222), the real kernel logs the error deterministically — console: **`I/O error, dev vda, sector 0 op 0x0:(READ) ...`** and **`Buffer I/O error on dev vda, logical block 0, async page read`** (plus errors on sectors 8/16/24). Two fault-injected boots AND a branch-from-snapshot across the read all fingerprint to the SAME **NEW** value **`b49eb759b9d607aa`** (`hvx_disk_boot_verify HVX_FAULT=0x2222`), proving the injected faults are deterministic end-to-end into a real OS. `hvx/run-fault-demo.sh` is the host-side runner (scoped kills only). No driver-retry nondeterminism wall was hit — the errored reads are a pure function of the request index, so retries error identically.

**Regressions (all green, one clean run, ZERO L1 restarts):** all M3–M13 + M18 + M19 + M20 + M21 tests pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean — no BUG/oops/taint/stall) — including `hvx_virtio_iso_test` **`7e586473f6e494d7`** and `hvx_virtio_pci_test` **`3f95ef833747f3ec`** (the disabled fault path is byte-identical; these two values changed from the M20/M21 `dd2c…`/`dd1f…` by the page-table A/D-bit determinism fix below — NOT by any fault logic). The no-disk boot is preserved at **`640ece2f900fe5b3`** and the fault-free with-disk boot at **`36583d533d7c989b`** (both whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL, via `run-disk-demo.sh`). `hvx_fault_test` added to `deploy-hvx.sh`. **Honest scope:** the delay model is a single shared per-kick completion pushed out by the summed per-request delays (the current device raises ONE completion interrupt per kick), not N independent per-request completion IRQs; reorder is capped at 16 requests/kick (the buffer bound; overflow completes in-order). Torn writes clip at the first sector. Real-Linux WRITE fault classes (drop/torn through `/dev/vda`) were not exercised end-to-end — the disk `/init` only reads.

**Post-M22 determinism fix — page-table ACCESSED/DIRTY bits (found via a flaky `hvx_virtio_pci_test`).** A stress run surfaced a RARE, host-load-dependent nondeterminism: the FRESH `hvx_virtio_pci_test` run occasionally fingerprinted `3f95…` instead of `dd1f…`, while every snapshot-restored path stayed `dd1f…` (the round-trip/branch normalized it). Root cause (pre-existing since M20, NOT introduced by M22 — the disabled fault path is byte-identical): the M20 idmap maps the 4th GiB (virtio BAR `0xfe000000` + IOAPIC `0xfec00000`) via a single 1 GiB PS PTE at `pdpt[3]`, and that MMIO window is **EPT-unmapped**, so a guest MMIO access is a page walk that FAULTS at the EPT stage. Whether the CPU writes the **ACCESSED bit** into that guest PTE on such a *faulting* walk is implementation-specific and, under nested VMX, host-load-dependent — a 1-byte flip in the guest page tables (which the fingerprint hashes). The signature nailed it: the round-trip always normalized because `HVX_SET_DISK` re-pokes `pdpt[3]` (the ONLY guest-RAM byte it touches), and `hvx_virtio_iso_test` (which drives the device over **PIO**, never touching the 4th-GiB MMIO) never flipped. **Fix (`hvx.c`, `HVX_SET_DISK` idmap poke):** pre-set A|D in the PTE — `pdpt[3] = (3<<30) | 0x83 | 0x60` — so the walker finds them already set and never writes, making the PTE a pure function of the guest. (The low identity tables get A/D set by their own SUCCESSFUL walks, which is deterministic, so they need no fix; the boots pass `HVX_DISK_NO_IDMAP` / attach no disk, so the poke is skipped and `36583d…`/`640ece2f…` are untouched.) This is why `hvx_virtio_iso_test`, `hvx_virtio_pci_test`, and `hvx_fault_test` (all hand-written guests that use the idmap) fingerprint to NEW STABLE values. **Verified:** `hvx_virtio_pci_test` `fp_a`/`fp_b`/round-trip/branch all `3f95ef833747f3ec` across 40 back-to-back runs under concurrent kernel-build CPU load (was flaky before; `pdpt[3]` read back as `0xc00000e3` every run) — the fresh-vs-restored mismatch is gone.

### Milestone 23 — DONE (deterministic virtio-net PCI device; verified + stretch-verified on real Linux in L1)

hvx now has a **deterministic virtio-net network device** — a second virtio-PCI function alongside the M20/M21 virtio-blk one, reusing the same PCI + MMIO-BAR + MSI-X foundation. Scripted inbound frames are delivered into the guest at an EXACT instruction (MSI-X, M8 path) and outbound frames are captured host-side; both are folded into the snapshot so branch/replay reproduces network activity bit-for-bit. The engine owns the network device and drives its timing.

**The device (`struct hvx_vnet`/`struct hvx_vnet_queue` in `struct hvx_vm`, shared per-VM like the console/vblk).** A PCI function at **bus 0 / dev 4** (vendor `0x1af4`, modern virtio-net device `0x1041`, class 02/00), BAR0 at `0xfe020000` (distinct from vblk's `0xfe000000`), the same modern virtio-pci capability list + an **MSI-X** capability (`hvx_vnet_pci_read/write`, `hvx_vnet_bar_read/write`, `hvx_vnet_mmio` — a duplicate of the vblk MMIO MOV decoder so the vblk path stays byte-identical). **Two virtqueues:** receiveq (0) and transmitq (1). Feature negotiation is deliberately **MINIMAL**: only `VIRTIO_NET_F_MAC` (bit 5) + `VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1` — **no** checksum/GSO/TSO/GRO/mergeable-rx-bufs, and **no controlq**. The device config exposes the MAC (and a link-up status byte). `num_queues=2`; the virtio_net_hdr is the 12-byte `virtio_net_hdr_v1` (num_buffers=1 on RX; RX small-receive uses one writable descriptor, TX a `[hdr][frame]` chain). **INERT until attached** (`present==0`): PCI config returns "no device", the BAR/PIO windows early-out, no completion arms — so every prior fingerprint is preserved.

**Deterministic INBOUND (RX): `HVX_PUT_RX` + instruction-timed MSI-X.** Userspace pushes scripted Ethernet frames into a small per-device FIFO (`rx_buf[HVX_VNET_RX_SLOTS][…]`, free-running `rx_head`/`rx_tail`; modeled on the M18 serial input ring but — unlike serial input — the cursors ride in the snapshot, since RX is a deterministic scripted source). `virtio_net_rx_deliver` consumes a posted receive buffer, writes the 12-byte header + the frame + the used-ring entry, and **arms the completion at `kick_icount + HVX_VNET_LATENCY`** (a pure function of the instruction clock — M16 creep discipline), delivered as an **MSI-X** (`vnet_msix_irq`, the queue's message-data vector injected to the BSP at the deterministic completion instruction, edge-cleared on delivery). **OUTBOUND (TX): `HVX_GET_TX`.** `virtio_net_tx_process` (at the transmitq kick) skips the 12-byte header across the descriptor chain and captures the payload into a host-side buffer (rewound on a virtio device_status=0 reset so a replay re-captures identically). **ABI (`hvx_abi.h`):** `HVX_NET_ATTACH` (`struct hvx_net_config {mac, flags}`; `HVX_NET_NO_IDMAP` for a real OS, `HVX_NET_PACED` for the stretch), `HVX_PUT_RX` (`struct hvx_rx`), `HVX_GET_TX` (`struct hvx_tx`); all virtio-net device + queue + RX-FIFO-cursor + captured-TX state appended to `struct hvx_snapshot`.

**Determinism invariants (both proven):**
- **Device inert when unused — all prior fingerprints UNCHANGED:** `hvx_virtio_iso_test`=`7e586473f6e494d7`, `hvx_virtio_pci_test`=`3f95ef833747f3ec`, `hvx_fault_test` (M22 values `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`), no-disk boot `640ece2f900fe5b3`, with-disk boot `36583d533d7c989b` — all re-verified GREEN with the M23 module. Every RX/TX/PCI/MMIO path early-outs on `!present`.
- **With the net device, a NEW reproducible fingerprint:** the hand-written `hvx_net_test` (below) fingerprints `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee` across replay / round-trip / branch, and is STABLE across **20 back-to-back runs under concurrent CPU load** (the M22-class page-table A/D hazard is handled — `HVX_NET_ATTACH` pokes the SAME `pdpt[3]` 1 GiB idmap leaf as `HVX_SET_DISK` with A|D pre-set, so the EPT-unmapped virtio-net BAR window is a pure function of the guest; verified no fresh-vs-restored flip).

**Validation — `hvx_net_test.c` (hand-written guest, MSI-X, FNV fingerprint).** A hand-written guest enumerates the virtio-net PCI function (reads vendor:device `0x10411af4`, BAR0 `0xfe020000`), resets it, enables + programs MSI-X table entry 0 (vector 0x20 → LAPIC), negotiates VERSION_1+MAC and reads back the MAC (`52:54:00:12:34:56`), sets up the receiveq (bound to the MSI-X vector) + transmitq, receives a scripted 60-byte RX frame pushed via `HVX_PUT_RX` (delivered at the receiveq kick, taken as an **MSI-X interrupt at reproducible icount 162**), and posts a 60-byte TX frame captured via `HVX_GET_TX`. Asserts: RX frame arrived intact (12-byte hdr + payload), TX captured intact, MAC matches, the RX IRQ fired once at a reproducible instruction, two runs byte-identical, and snapshot/round-trip/branch reproduce — all `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`. "MILESTONE 23 PASS."

**STRETCH — DONE (real Linux binds virtio_net, brings up eth0, and answers a scripted ARP; deterministic).** `build-linux.sh` stages a third initramfs (`build/initramfs-net.cpio.gz`) carrying the `virtio_net` module stack (`virtio`, `virtio_ring`, the pci_*_dev libs, `virtio_pci`, `failover`, `net_failover`, `virtio_net`) + an `/init` that brings up **eth0 with a STATIC IP (10.0.0.2/24; no DHCP)**. `hvx_linux.c` attaches the net device (`HVX_NET_NO_IDMAP | HVX_NET_PACED`) and pushes a scripted **ARP request** (who-has 10.0.0.2 tell 10.0.0.1) before the boot. Real Linux 6.6.35 enumerates `pci 0000:00:04.0 [1af4:1041]`, binds `virtio_net`, creates `eth0` (our MAC), brings it up RUNNING, and **replies** — the loader captures **`ARP REPLY: 10.0.0.2 is-at 52:54:00:12:34:56`** on the transmitq (`hvx_linux rc=0`, "DEMO PASS"). `hvx_net_boot_verify.c` proves determinism: two fresh net boots AND a branch-from-snapshot across the ARP exchange all fingerprint the NEW baseline **`fd5663b60bf9412e`** (console + snapshot + 256 MiB RAM + captured TX). Host-side runner `hvx/run-net-demo.sh` (scoped kills only).

Two real-Linux walls found and fixed (each by booting to a wall and decoding it — this is why the stretch needed real work, not just the core):
1. **Do NOT advertise `VIRTIO_NET_F_STATUS`.** With STATUS, `virtnet_probe` starts the link carrier-OFF and only raises it on a **config-change interrupt** — which this device does not generate — so `eth0` never becomes RUNNING (`carrier:0`) and never answers ARP (confirmed via `ifconfig eth0`). WITHOUT STATUS, virtio_net assumes link-up (`netif_carrier_on` at probe → RUNNING). Advertise MAC + VERSION_1 only.
2. **PACED RX delivery (`HVX_NET_PACED`).** A real driver posts a **deep** receive ring and kicks the receiveq **ONCE at open**, so naive kick-triggered delivery places every scripted frame in that first NAPI burst — **before** the interface finishes coming up (NETDEV_UP / the local address becoming `RTN_LOCAL`), so `arp_process` receives the (byte-perfect, verified via a kernel-side dump) ARP but drops it without replying (empty neigh table even with `arp_accept=1`; `rx_packets` counts them). Fix: when paced, the run-loop **trickles one pending frame per `HVX_VNET_RX_PACE` (200000) instructions** against the already-posted buffers — a deterministic icount cadence — so a LATER frame arrives after the interface is fully up and gets answered. The hand-written test (single frame, kick-triggered, paced OFF) is byte-identical.

**Environmental fix bundled here — `build-linux.sh` busybox PIN (found while re-verifying the boots).** The staged initramfs bakes in a static busybox chosen by `ls /nix/store/*busybox-static*/bin/busybox | head -1`; the shared box's nix store gained additional `busybox-static-1.37.0` builds since the M21 baseline, and a newly-added store hash now sorts first, so `head -1` picked a DIFFERENT busybox → different initramfs bytes (loaded into hashed guest RAM) → the boot fingerprints changed to `e3907b04…`/`3c58ff16…` even though the **kernel was unchanged and the module is provably inert** (iso/pci unchanged, boots still deterministic). `build-linux.sh` now PINS the exact known-good busybox store path (`ahw5fkq0…-1.37.0`, with a GC fallback), which **restores `640ece2f900fe5b3` / `36583d533d7c989b` exactly** (re-verified). This is the same class of environmental blob-dependency the M21 note documents; the pin makes the boots re-reproducible.

**Regressions (all green, synchronous, one clean run):** all M3–M13 + M18 + M19 + M20 + M21 + M22 tests pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean) — including `hvx_virtio_iso_test` **`7e586473f6e494d7`** and `hvx_virtio_pci_test` **`3f95ef833747f3ec`** (unchanged) plus the new `hvx_net_test` **`0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`**. No-disk boot **`640ece2f900fe5b3`** and with-disk boot **`36583d533d7c989b`** (whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL, `run-disk-demo.sh`) with the busybox pin. With-net boot **`fd5663b60bf9412e`** (two boots + branch, `hvx_net_boot_verify`). 20/20 net-MMIO stress stable under CPU load. dmesg-clean, clean unload, no L1 restarts.

**Honest scope:** the hand-written core delivers RX via a per-kick completion and captures TX synchronously; the device raises ONE completion per kick (RX and TX have independent per-queue completions). Real-Linux RX uses PACED delivery (a deterministic trickle) to sidestep the open-time NAPI-burst-vs-interface-up race — a device-timing model, not a change to Linux. The stretch proves inbound ARP → reply; TCP/UDP flows, multi-frame RX bursts into one NAPI poll, and mergeable/GSO offloads are deliberately out of scope.

### Milestone 24 — DONE (deterministic network fault injection; verified + stretch-verified on real Linux in L1)

hvx now perturbs **packets** on the virtio-net device from a SEEDED schedule — symmetric to the M22 disk faults — so any bug the perturbation surfaces replays bit-for-bit. EVERY fault decision is a PURE FUNCTION of a seeded PRNG keyed by **direction + per-direction packet index** (and the per-kick index for reorder), never wall-clock or host state: same seed → same fault schedule → same result → reproducible. The net-fault policy + PRNG counters ride in the snapshot, so branch/replay reproduces the identical faults. INERT (early-out, no state touched) when `nfault_classes == 0` (the default) → byte-identical to M23.

**Design (reuses the M22 machinery).** `struct hvx_vnet` gained `nfault_seed`/`nfault_classes`/`nfault_probability`/`nfault_max_delay` + per-direction PRNG counters (`nfault_rx_seq`/`nfault_tx_seq`, `nfault_rx_kick_seq`/`nfault_tx_kick_seq`) + `nfault_count`. `vnet_fault_decide` reuses the M22 splitmix64 finalizer (`fault_mix`) via `net_fault_roll(seed, dir, index) = fault_mix((seed ^ dir_salt) + (index+1)*GOLDEN)` with `dir_salt = 'R'<<48` (RX) / `'T'<<48` (TX) so the two directions' schedules are independent. For each packet, if `(h % 100) < probability` a fault applies, chosen from the enabled applicable classes in the FIXED order **DROP, DELAY (RX only), CORRUPT, DUPLICATE** by `((h>>8) % n)`; REORDER is a separate per-kick class (Fisher-Yates over the used-ring entries, keyed by `net_reorder_roll(seed, dir, kick)` — the M22 pattern with the same `0xD15EA5E5C0FFEE11` salt). The five classes: **DROP** (RX: consumed from the FIFO but not delivered / TX: not captured — but the guest's transmit descriptor is still acked); **DELAY** (RX only: the batch completion is pushed out `1..max_delay` extra instructions — `completion_due += extra`, a pure function of icount, M16 creep discipline); **CORRUPT** (flip one byte at `(h>>16) % frame_len` with XOR value `(u8)((h>>32)|1)` — always nonzero; RX corrupts in place in the already-consumed FIFO slot, TX corrupts in the captured `tx_buf`); **DUPLICATE** (deliver into a second RX descriptor / capture the TX payload twice); **REORDER** (permute the used-ring completion order of a multi-packet kick — like the disk). `HVX_SET_NET_FAULT`/`HVX_GET_NET_FAULT` (`struct hvx_net_fault`, ioctls 23/24) drive it; all fault fields are appended to `struct hvx_snapshot` (older offsets unchanged → all prior fingerprints preserved) and to the `hvx_net_fault` fingerprint.

**RX batching (the one behavior change, made byte-safe).** M23's receiveq delivered ONE frame per kick (the `break`, needed for the paced real-OS trickle). To let the fault engine see a multi-packet batch (DROP/REORDER/DUPLICATE), `virtio_net_rx_deliver(vcpu, max_consume)` now drains the whole batch at a **non-paced** kick (`max_consume=0`) and stays one-per-call for the **paced** path and the paced-boot kick (`max_consume=1`). A single pushed frame (the M23 hand-written test) delivers exactly one either way, and the paced boot is unchanged — so **`hvx_net_test`=`0e2b7acf2f4f94ee` and the net boot are byte-identical**. RX/TX now buffer their used-ring entries and flush them (permuted for REORDER), exactly like `virtio_blk_process`. (The RX corrupt-in-place + TX capture-into-`tx_buf` avoid any large stack buffer in the IRQs-off/FPU-disabled run-loop section — 0 frame-size warnings.)

**Validation — `hvx_net_fault_test.c` (hand-written guest, FNV fingerprint; the test REPLICATES the module PRNG so it predicts the exact schedule).** A hand-written guest enumerates the virtio-net function, sets up the receiveq (12 receive buffers, MSI-X-bound) + transmitq (6 `[hdr][frame]` chains), kicks both once; the host pushes 6 scripted RX frames + captures 6 TX frames, all fault-filtered:
- **(a) reproducible under a seed** (S1=`0x1234`, classes `DROP|DELAY|CORRUPT|DUPLICATE|REORDER`, prob 70): the observed outcomes match the predicted schedule — RX used-ring order `3 0 1 2 4` (5 delivered copies incl. a duplicate, corruption byte-exact), TX used-ring order `0 4 10 6 2 8` (8 captured frames incl. duplicates), completion armed at a reproducible icount — and two runs fingerprint identically **`883a00eb6a14a6f0`** (over regs + net device state + RX-FIFO count + captured TX + fault/PRNG state + all guest RAM). DELAY is shown by the completion_due: off=162 vs on=186 = **+24 instructions exactly as predicted**.
- **(b) the seed controls the schedule** (S2=`0x9abcdef`): a DIFFERENT but reproducible schedule (RX `1 3 2 0 4`, TX `10 2 0 4 8 6`) and fingerprint **`37490fb78da33064`** (≠ S1), schedule provably differs.
- **(c) snapshot/branch** at the armed midpoint (RX/TX processed, completion pending) reproduces the finish bit-for-bit: `fp_orig == fp_branch == fp_a1 == 883a00eb6a14a6f0`.
- **(d) faults DISABLED (default) == M23:** all 6 RX frames delivered normally (used ring `0 1 2 3 4 5`), all 6 TX captured, reproducible **`40659aabb208aaa0`**. "MILESTONE 24 PASS."

**STRETCH — DONE (a seeded DROP deterministically changes real Linux's behavior).** `hvx_linux.c` reads env `HVX_NET_FAULT=<seed>` (+ `HVX_NET_FAULT_CLASSES`/`_PROB`/`_MAXDELAY`; default class DROP at prob 100, inert when unset) and installs the policy before the net boot. Host-side runner `hvx/run-net-fault-demo.sh` (scoped kills only) boots the NixOS 6.6.35 net image three times: the **baseline** (no fault) binds `virtio_net`, brings up `eth0` (10.0.0.2), and **answers the scripted ARP** — `ARP REPLY found` (as M23, `hvx_linux rc=0`; baseline fingerprint `fd5663b60bf9412e`); the two **DROP** boots (`seed=0xd24 classes=DROP prob=100`) bring up the identical `eth0` but the seeded DROP suppresses every scripted ARP copy, so **NO ARP reply is captured** — `baseline ARP-reply-lines=1 drop1=0 drop2=0` → "M24 STRETCH PASS: seeded DROP deterministically suppressed the ARP reply." The behavior change comes from the seed alone and is reproducible across both DROP boots. (A canonical NEW with-net-fault boot fingerprint via `hvx_net_boot_verify` is not added: that harness stops on the ARP-reply marker, which the DROP boot never reaches — the deterministic evidence is the reproducible reply-suppression; the rigorous full-state fingerprint proof lives in the hand-written `hvx_net_fault_test`.)

**Regressions (all green, synchronous, one clean run):** all M3–M13 + M18 + M19 + M20 + M21 + M22 + M23 tests pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean) with **UNCHANGED** fingerprints — `hvx_virtio_iso_test`=`7e586473f6e494d7`, `hvx_virtio_pci_test`=`3f95ef833747f3ec`, `hvx_net_test`=`0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, `hvx_fault_test`=`91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`. No-disk boot **`640ece2f900fe5b3`** and with-disk boot **`36583d533d7c989b`** (whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL). The net-fault BAR window reuses the M23 `pdpt[3]` A/D-preset idmap (no new EPT-faulting MMIO window). dmesg-clean, clean unload, **no L1 restarts**.

**Honest scope:** faults act at the device layer (RX FIFO → receive descriptors, transmitq → capture buffer), keyed by packet index. DELAY is RX-only (it perturbs the receiveq completion timing); TX DELAY / on-wire latency modeling and payload-aware corruption (checksums/flows) are out of scope. The rigorous reproducibility proof is the hand-written test; the real-Linux stretch proves the deterministic *behavior change* (reply suppression), not a new full-boot fingerprint.

### Milestone 25 — DONE (FLAGSHIP: a real durability bug found by a seeded disk fault, reproduced bit-for-bit; verified in L1)

**This is the payoff of the whole "be your own hypervisor" path.** For the first time, 24 milestones of deterministic machinery (real-Linux boot, virtio-blk disk, the M22 seeded disk-fault engine, snapshot/branch, the canonical fingerprint) do what deterministic simulation testing promises: **find a real durability bug in a real application through seeded fault injection, then reproduce it perfectly.** It is almost entirely userspace + build assembly — **the kernel module is UNCHANGED from M24** (only the banner/`MODULE_DESCRIPTION`/load-log milestone strings bumped to 25, which hash no guest state), so **every prior fingerprint is preserved**.

**The SUT — `hvx/hydokv.c`** (a ~230-line raw-block, `O_DIRECT`, single-outstanding-I/O key/value store, statically linked with musl) with ONE deliberate, documented, realistic durability bug: **it ACKS a write as committed the moment `pwrite()` returns success — recording it in its in-memory index — BEFORE any durability barrier**, batching the barrier (`fdatasync`) only every `BATCH`=8 puts. An acked put in the current, not-yet-barriered batch sits in a power-loss window. This is the FoundationDB/Jepsen "unsynchronized write lost on reboot" class. The bug is HONEST: hydokv is provably correct when nothing is lost (F1), and the fault models a real hardware behavior.

**The load-bearing correctness point — the read must bypass the page cache.** A dropped *physical* write is invisible to a cached read (Linux still holds the value the guest wrote). hydokv does ALL I/O with `O_DIRECT` (every read is a real device read) and additionally **close+reopens `/dev/vda`** ("crash / recover") before the oracle reads — so the oracle checks the true on-device state. Without this, the durability check would be fake. The workload writes `K`=48 records (key `i` → sector `1+i`, value a pure function of `i`, no timestamps/RNG), records each acked put in a reference model, then the oracle asserts every committed key reads back with its committed value (invariant = READ-COMMITTED-DURABILITY). It prints `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=48 durable` or `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION key=<k> expected=<v> got=<g>`, then the stop marker `hydo-flagship-marker: oracle complete`.

**The fault ↔ real-world mapping:** the M22 `HVX_FAULT_DROP` class acks `VIRTIO_BLK_S_OK` to the guest (so `pwrite()` succeeds and the app believes the write landed) but does NOT persist the sector — precisely "an un-synced write lost on power failure." hydokv knows nothing about faults; the fault is injected BELOW it by the hypervisor.

**The four checks (the same four `hydo verify` runs, now on a REAL bug in REAL software)** via `hvx/hvx_bug_verify.c` (full-state fingerprint = console incl. the verdict line + snapshot + 256 MiB RAM + disk image via `HVX_GET_DISK`; models `hvx_disk_boot_verify.c`):
- **(F1) NO FAULT — the app is correct AND deterministic:** `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=48 durable`; two fresh boots + a branch-from-snapshot are bit-identical in FULL state → **new OK baseline `62ae248b01bf3f70`**.
- **(F2) SEEDED DROP (seed `0xB5EED`, prob 25%) — THE demonstration:** a genuinely-committed key is LOST — `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION key=7 expected=0xa5a5a5a55384660b got=0x0000000000000000` (14 of 48 committed keys vanished). The failing run **replays** bit-for-bit (two fault-injected boots identical) AND **branch-from-snapshots** bit-for-bit (rewind to before hydokv runs, restore, finish → identical failing state, so the fault schedule + PRNG counters + disk are all captured) → **new VIOLATION baseline `7ade5f37d16a347c`**.
- **(F3) SEED-SENSITIVE:** a different seed (`0x1CE5EED`) loses a DIFFERENT key — `VIOLATION key=0` — with a different, reproducible fingerprint **`832cc63372b1458d`** (≠ F2), proving the fault is really consumed (the determinism is real, not the mere absence of variation).
- **(F4=does-not-fire-without-the-fault)** IS F1's OK verdict — proving the fault is what surfaces the latent bug, not a broken app.

"hvx_bug_verify: MILESTONE 25 PASS." **F0** (`hvx/hvx_bug_f0_test.c`, in `deploy-hvx.sh`): a HAND-WRITTEN guest (no Linux, ms) proves the same loop at zero boot cost — a "committed" record written then DROP-faulted, detected as lost via a device read-back, reproducible + branch-identical (`1083e400458728ff`); a permanent fast regression.

**Stretch F4 (unmodified real SUT — SQLite) — DEFERRED with evidence, NOT faked.** The design's own follow-on. The building blocks are present on the build machine (the pinned busybox has `mkfs.ext2`/`mkfs.vfat`/`mount`; `ext4`/`ext2`/`vfat` are kernel modules under the module tree; a static sqlite `3.45.3` is in the nix store at `/nix/store/wr9kbm5zbfqk424bhphdc8s2nb4gxcdm-sqlite-static-…`). The genuine wall is a MULTI-STEP effort beyond the must-have loop: (a) an unmodified SQLite needs a real **filesystem**, and `mkfs` stamps a UUID + superblock timestamps and a mounted rw fs writes inode/superblock times — these must be pinned/baked to keep the byte-for-byte fingerprint stable (in principle deterministic under hvx since M16 makes even the clock/CRNG bit-identical, but it is unproven and adds real risk); (b) SQLite DB + fs exceed the 1 MiB `HVX_DISK_MAX` (needs the design's noted one-constant, fingerprint-neutral bump); (c) the store sqlite path is a **library** output — a static `sqlite3` CLI must be obtained/built reproducibly; (d) the honest durability window is `PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL`. Each is surmountable; together they are a stretch, and `hydokv` (F0–F3) is the must-have that proves the loop. Recorded so a follow-up can pick it up directly.

**Regressions (all green, synchronous, one clean run):** all M3–M24 tests + F0 pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean). **Preserved fingerprints UNCHANGED** (recomputed this session): no-disk boot **`640ece2f900fe5b3`**, with-disk boot **`36583d533d7c989b`** (both whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL); `hvx_fault_test`/`hvx_net_fault_test`/etc. all pass unchanged. dmesg-clean, clean unload (rmmod rc=0), **no L1 restarts**.

**Honest scope:** WHOLE-BOOT (free-run) determinism, as M16 (single-step bisection is BLOCKED/moot, M17). The bug is a real class demonstrated on a purpose-built store (`hydokv`); F4 extends it to an unmodified off-the-shelf tool and is deferred. The DROP fault surfaces the durability bug; `HVX_FAULT_TORN` (a corrupt rather than missing record) and a seed sweep are cheap adds on the same harness.

### Milestone 26 — DONE (flagship on UNMODIFIED SQLite; verified in L1) — finishes M25's deferred F4

**The M25 loop, now on a REAL, off-the-shelf application on a REAL filesystem.** M25 proved find-a-bug-by-seeded-fault-then-reproduce-it on a purpose-built store (`hydokv`); the credibility risk it named was "you wrote a buggy app on purpose." M26 removes it: the SUT is an **UNMODIFIED, statically-linked upstream `sqlite3` 3.45.3 CLI** (`pkgsStatic.sqlite`, nobody wrote it for this demo) running on a **real ext2 filesystem** staged as `/dev/vda`. A seeded virtio-blk fault makes a genuinely-committed SQLite row vanish, and the failure replays + branches bit-for-bit and is seed-sensitive.

**SUT + workload/oracle (`build-linux.sh` §2g → `build/initramfs-sql.cpio.gz` + `build/sql-disk.img`).** A new initramfs carries the static `sqlite3`, the virtio-blk stack, and the **ext2 module stack** (`mbcache`, `ext2`); its `/init` mounts `/dev/vda`, runs **64 committed INSERTs under `PRAGMA synchronous=FULL`** (sqlite fsyncs on every commit — the CORRECT durable config, NOT `synchronous=OFF`; keys 0..63, value a pure function of the key, a fixed ~2 KB pad so each row spans roughly its own 4 KiB page → a dropped physical write reliably loses a committed row's page), then `sync` + `echo 3 > /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches` so a fresh read hits the **DEVICE** (a dropped write is invisible to a cached read — the load-bearing correctness point, same as M25's O_DIRECT), then an oracle (a fresh `sqlite3` connection) re-reads every committed row and prints `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=64 durable` / `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION key=<k> expected=<v> got=<g>` + the stop marker `hydo-sqlite-marker: oracle complete`. **Byte-reproducibility** (the whole determinism story): the ext2 image is built with a **pinned UUID + `libfaketime`-frozen timestamps + pinned `hash_seed`** (`faketime … mke2fs -t ext2 -b 1024 -U … -E hash_seed=…`, with the `mke2fs`/`libfaketime` store paths themselves PINNED; this e2fsprogs build IGNORES `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, so faketime freezes every superblock/inode time — proven byte-identical across rebuilds, see the fs-image byte-reproducibility hardening note below), and `sqlite3` is a pinned nix-store path — the exact discipline as the pinned busybox/musl toolchain. (The DB is created at runtime by `/init`, so only the empty fs image is staged.)

**HONESTY — two fault models, framed exactly (this is the point of using a real tool):**
- **`HVX_FAULT_DROP` = a "LYING DISK"** (the existing M22 class): it acks a write — even one sqlite `fsync`'d — but silently fails to persist the sector. This is NOT normal power loss; it is a device that **VIOLATES the durability contract** (a real, documented class: disks/controllers that "lie about fsync", the FoundationDB/Jepsen "unsynchronized-write" bug). Under it, correctly-configured SQLite loses a committed row — **not a SQLite bug**, but proof that SQLite's durability is only as good as the disk's honesty, found and reproduced deterministically. This is the demonstration.
- **`HVX_FAULT_DROP_UNFLUSHED` = a FAITHFUL POWER-LOSS model** (NEW this milestone): a write rolled for this class is held in a per-device **volatile write cache** (not persisted; reads are served from it — read-your-writes) and is lost ONLY if never FLUSHed; a virtio-blk **FLUSH** (sqlite's fsync — the device advertises `VIRTIO_BLK_F_FLUSH` **only when this class is armed**, so every prior fingerprint is preserved) commits the cache to stable media, making those writes durable and **un-droppable**. It therefore CANNOT lose a properly-barriered write. **The contrast, shown on the identical SUT with the same seed:** the LYING disk loses the fsync'd committed row; the FAITHFUL fault does NOT — isolating the flush barrier and proving the loss is the disk violating durability, not SQLite. This is the "SHOW the FULL-vs-relaxed contrast" the design asked for, done cleanly (no crash-trigger needed: sqlite's per-commit fsync's FLUSH commits the cache before the oracle reads).

**Kernel change (`hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h`) — additive + inert-by-default, so ALL prior fingerprints hold.** (1) `HVX_DISK_MAX` 1 MiB → **64 MiB** (a one-constant EINVAL bound; `HVX_SET_DISK` `kvmalloc`s exactly `dk.len` and the fingerprint hashes `disk_size` bytes, so it is fingerprint-neutral — verified). (2) New fault class `HVX_FAULT_DROP_UNFLUSHED` (bit 5), appended LAST in `vblk_fault_decide`'s candidate order so it never shifts a pre-M26 selection. (3) A volatile-write-cache in `struct hvx_vblk` (`unsync[]` metadata + a lazily-`kvmalloc`'d 2 MiB data buffer, allocated in `HVX_SET_FAULT` **outside** the IRQs-off run-loop, freed with the VM): `virtio_blk_process` stashes a `DROP_UNFLUSH` write, overlays held bytes on reads, invalidates on a superseding persist, and commits the cache on a `VIRTIO_BLK_T_FLUSH`; cleared on `HVX_SET_DISK`/device reset. Every helper early-outs when `unsync_n == 0`, so with the class disabled the servicing path is byte-identical to M25. (4) `VIRTIO_BLK_F_FLUSH` advertised only when the class is armed. The volatile cache is **transient device state** (empty at flush/pre-SUT boundaries where snapshots are taken — the M7/M8 "reproducible boundary" guarantee), so it is deliberately NOT in `struct hvx_snapshot`; the branch harness snapshots BEFORE sqlite runs (cache empty) and replays the whole workload deterministically.

**The four checks (`hvx/hvx_sqlite_verify.c`, modeled on `hvx_bug_verify.c`; full-state fingerprint = console incl. the verdict + snapshot + 256 MiB RAM + 16 MiB disk):**
- **(F1) NO FAULT — correct + deterministic:** `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=64 durable`; two fresh boots + a branch-from-snapshot are bit-identical → **new OK baseline `1c072036545a7e87`**.
- **(F2) SEEDED LYING-DISK DROP (seed `0x5017E0`, prob 5%) — THE demonstration:** a committed row is LOST — `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION key=15 expected=100015 got=MISSING` (2 of 64 rows lost; DB still readable). The failing run **replays** bit-for-bit (two boots identical) AND **branch-from-snapshots** bit-for-bit → **new VIOLATION baseline `7a4ab31fddabe4cc`**.
- **(F3) SEED-SENSITIVE:** a different seed (`0x5017E1`) loses a DIFFERENT row — `VIOLATION key=31` — with a different, reproducible fingerprint **`dc04dbe3b49624df`** (≠ F2), proving the fault is really consumed.
- **(CONTRAST) FLUSH-AWARE DROP, same seed as F2:** `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=64 durable` → the fsync'd committed row **SURVIVES** the faithful power-loss fault (**`2d96a0c788c1fbba`**), isolating the flush barrier. "hvx_sqlite_verify: MILESTONE 26 PASS."

**Regressions (all green, one clean run, verified this session):** all M3–M25 tests + F0 pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean — no BUG/oops/taint-from-hvx/stall). **The `HVX_DISK_MAX` bump + flush-aware additions are FINGERPRINT-NEUTRAL — every preserved fingerprint is UNCHANGED (recomputed):** `hvx_virtio_iso_test`=`7e586473f6e494d7`, `hvx_virtio_pci_test`=`3f95ef833747f3ec`, `hvx_fault_test`=`91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682` (the M22 engine I extended still hashes identically), `hvx_net_test`=`0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, `hvx_net_fault_test`=`883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`; no-disk boot **`640ece2f900fe5b3`**, with-disk boot **`36583d533d7c989b`** (both whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL), M25 hydokv **`62ae248b01bf3f70`**/**`7ade5f37d16a347c`**/**`832cc63372b1458d`** (MILESTONE 25 PASS re-run). Clean unload (rmmod rc=0), **no L1 restarts** (L1 pid stable). (The L1 `tainted` word carries a stale bit-7/WARN from L1's own 20 h history + the expected OOT/UNSIGNED module bits; a fresh insmod+run cycle produces an empty WARN/BUG/trace dmesg scan, so hvx itself is clean.)

**Honest scope / notes:** WHOLE-BOOT (free-run) determinism, as M16/M25. The demonstrated contrast is **lying-disk vs. flush-aware** on the same `synchronous=FULL` SUT (the cleanest, no-crash-trigger isolation of the FLUSH barrier); the `synchronous=NORMAL/OFF` "documented relaxed window loses a row even under a faithful fault" variant needs an explicit crash-point (discard the un-flushed cache before the oracle reads) and is a cheap follow-on on this same harness. The DROP outcome is tuned (prob 5%) to lose a NAMED row with the DB still readable rather than fully corrupting it (a higher prob does the latter — also a valid, more dramatic durability violation). The volatile cache is not yet in the snapshot (transient; empty at snapshot boundaries here — capture it if a future harness snapshots mid-un-flushed-window).

### Milestone 27 — DONE (flagship on real Redis: documented AOF durability window) — Rung 2 of the loopback-first stack; verified in L1

**The M25/M26 find-a-bug-then-reproduce loop, now on a real *network* service driven by a co-located client over loopback, reproducing Redis's OWN documented durability tradeoff.** The SUT is an **UNMODIFIED, statically-linked upstream `redis-server` + `redis-cli` 7.2.4** (`pkgsStatic.redis`, pinned store path) plus its client, **co-located in ONE L2 guest talking over the in-kernel `127.0.0.1` LOOPBACK** — the "loopback-first" thesis in miniature: no virtio-net, the client↔server path is an in-kernel memcpy driven by the deterministic clock, so it is deterministic for free. Persistence is the **AOF on a real ext2 fs on `/dev/vda`** (reusing M26's fs+disk work). The result reproduces Redis's famous `appendfsync` knob under a faithful power loss: **`appendfsync=always` SURVIVES, `appendfsync=everysec` LOSES the last acked writes** — the documented AOF window — bit-for-bit.

**The crash model — a two-boot power loss, NO new kernel machinery for the crash.** The backing disk has a **volatile write cache** (the M26 flush-aware class `HVX_FAULT_DROP_UNFLUSHED` armed at prob 100: a write is durable only after a virtio-blk FLUSH, which Linux issues on `fsync` because the device advertises `F_FLUSH` when the class is armed). The "power loss" is modeled entirely in userspace by the existing snapshot/disk machinery: **boot1 (WORKLOAD, fault armed):** bring up `lo`, mount ext2, start redis-server, drive `NKEYS=48` committed `SET`s over `127.0.0.1` via redis-cli (each `OK` = acked), then **`SIGSTOP` redis** to freeze the durability state at the just-acked instant (before any periodic fsync). `HVX_GET_DISK` then returns the **DURABLE image = only the FLUSH-committed bytes** (the un-flushed volatile cache is NOT in it — exactly what survives a power loss; `HVX_GET_DISK` already excludes it and `HVX_SET_DISK` clears it, so no kernel change was needed for the crash). **boot2 (ORACLE, a FRESH VM, no fault):** `HVX_SET_DISK` the durable image (RAM gone + volatile cache gone = a true reboot), redis-server **AOF-replays** from it, and an oracle re-reads every acked key. Because FLUSH is the sole persistence gate, the outcome is a pure function of the fsync POLICY, robust to writeback timing: `always` fsyncs (= FLUSH = durable) BEFORE the client's `OK` → SURVIVES; `everysec` batches the fsync ~1/s so the just-acked writes sit un-flushed → LOST.

**Three real-service-nondeterminism walls found and fixed (each a deterministic-hypervisor-specific issue, not a redis bug):**
1. **Deterministic CRNG seed (`hvx_seedrng.c`, new).** A deterministic hypervisor exposes NO hardware entropy — RDRAND/RDSEED are unadvertised (they would be nondeterministic) and the virtual TSC advances by a fixed step per exit, so it carries NO jitter. Linux's CRNG therefore never initialises: redis's blocking `getrandom()` (hash seed) hangs at startup while the kernel SPINS in `try_to_generate_entropy` reading the jitter-free TSC (observed: >130 M RDTSC exits, redis never prints its banner). Fix: a tiny static helper credits a FIXED block of entropy via the `RNDADDENTROPY` ioctl on `/dev/urandom` → `random: crng init done` → `getrandom()` returns at once. The seed is constant and the whole boot is deterministic (M16), so the CRNG stream stays byte-reproducible. Run once from `/init` before redis; staged only in the redis initramfs (no shared-loader change).
2. **Deterministic idle heartbeat (`HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT`, gated).** A co-located loopback service blocks in `epoll_wait` for loopback data whose delivery needs a **softirq/tick**; when the guest HALTs fully tickless (`tsc_deadline==0`, no pending device completion — decoded as `pv_native_safe_halt` at the idle RIP) a deterministic machine has nothing to wake it (no host interrupts, no re-armed timer) → the run stalls (the M21-class idle-HLT, but with nothing armed to jump to). Fix: when the guest idle-HLTs unarmed AND the VM opted in, the run-loop advances the virtual clock by a fixed step and injects the guest's LAPIC-timer vector — a **synthetic periodic tick** that runs Linux's timer ISR + softirqs (delivering the loopback frame) + the scheduler (`HVX_IDLE_HEARTBEAT_TSC`, in `hvx_run_vcpu`). **OFF by default** — the plain no-disk boot ALSO idles unarmed, so an ungated heartbeat moved its fingerprint (`640ece2f900fe5b3`→`792d852bad6c705f`); gating it behind `struct hvx_config.flags` (the old `_pad`, default 0) restores byte-identical behavior everywhere except the redis path. The harness sets the flag; `hvx_linux` sets it from env `HVX_IDLE_HEARTBEAT`.
3. **`redis-cli -t` unsupported.** This build rejects `-t <timeout>` ("Unrecognized option"), so the readiness poll's every `ping` failed; dropped the flag.

**Kernel change (`hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h`) — additive + inert-by-default, so ALL prior fingerprints hold.** `struct hvx_config._pad` → `flags` (+ `HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT` bit; zero-default = off); `struct hvx_vm.idle_hb`; `HVX_IDLE_HEARTBEAT_TSC` + the gated unarmed-idle-HLT branch in the run-loop's HLT handler. Every path is skipped when the flag is off, so a VM that does not opt in is byte-identical to M26.

**The four checks (`hvx/hvx_redis_verify.c`, modeled on `hvx_sqlite_verify.c`; full-state fingerprint = console incl. the verdict + snapshot + 256 MiB RAM + 16 MiB disk; two-boot crash-recover):**
- **(SANITY) everysec, NO fault, NO crash:** `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=48 durable` — proves the unmodified redis + loopback client↔server + AOF work and are correct absent the power loss (`8fb413b379a1fade`).
- **(F1) DURABLE CONFIG (`always`) under the volatile-cache power loss → OK:** every acked write was fsync'd (= FLUSHed = durable) before the client's OK, so it SURVIVES the crash; two full crash-recover runs are bit-identical → **new OK baseline `ca15ec5acd7b282a`**.
- **(F2) DOCUMENTED WINDOW (`everysec`) under the SAME power loss → VIOLATION:** a committed key is LOST — `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION key=1 expected=100001 got=MISSING`; the failing run **replays** bit-for-bit AND **branch-from-snapshots** bit-for-bit → **new VIOLATION baseline `fc2db18cac961b51`**. F1-vs-F2 IS the `always`-vs-`everysec` contrast, isolating exactly the AOF fsync window. (The workload durable-disk is proven deterministic: `HVX_GET_DISK` hashes to the same `f8cc48adc9f2c35e` across all three produce-durable calls.)
- **(F3) SEED-SENSITIVE:** a seeded LYING-DISK DROP (prob 8% on `always`) corrupts the AOF differently per seed — seed `0x5ED15A0` → `4af35d75be853246`, seed `0x5ED15A1` → `9d0b5a91e734712b` (≠), each reproducible, both `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION` — proving the fault is really consumed by the seed.

"hvx_redis_verify: MILESTONE 27 PASS."

**Branch honesty (the one subtlety):** the F2 branch reproduces the snapshot **round-trip bit-for-bit in FULL state** (`fp_a==fp_b`, the M7 property: restore-then-continue equals continue) AND the **durable outcome** — the oracle-verdict console + the persisted AOF — is byte-identical to the straight run (`dfp` matches; the console + disk fingerprint components were confirmed identical). What is NOT asserted is that a branch's full-256-MiB endpoint equals a *separate* straight run's: the synthetic idle-heartbeat advances jiffies/scheduler during the POST-marker idle and the harness stops at a throttle boundary, so those two endpoints differ only in post-marker idle bookkeeping — a stop-point artifact under the heartbeat, not a divergence (M16/M26 achieve full-endpoint equality only because, without the heartbeat, their post-marker idle is RAM-stable).

**RAM/scale outcome:** NO `MAX_GPAGES` bump was needed — 256 MiB is ample (redis-server 3.4 MB + redis-cli 1.4 MB static, a 48-key dataset, AOF on the 16 MiB disk). `MAX_GPAGES` stays 65536. The disk stays the M26 64 MiB single `kvmalloc`. So no scale-up risk was incurred; the GB-scale page-array refactor is deferred to Rung 3 as planned.

**Regressions (all green, verified this session):** all M3–M26 hand-written tests + F0 pass via `deploy-hvx.sh load` (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean). **Preserved fingerprints UNCHANGED (recomputed):** `hvx_virtio_iso_test`=`7e586473f6e494d7`, `hvx_virtio_pci_test`=`3f95ef833747f3ec`, `hvx_fault_test`=`91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, `hvx_net_test`=`0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, `hvx_net_fault_test`=`883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, `hvx_bug_f0_test`=`1083e400458728ff`; **no-disk boot `640ece2f900fe5b3`, with-disk boot `36583d533d7c989b`** (both whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL — the `flags`+heartbeat gate is provably inert). **The idle heartbeat is OFF by default, so the extra RAM/paths are fingerprint-neutral.** The M26 **sqlite** harness re-run **PASSED all four checks**, but its NUMERIC baselines had drifted from the original M26 values — which the M27 pass correctly diagnosed as environmental **fs-image drift**: the host `mke2fs`/e2fsprogs binary that stamps the byte-reproducible `sql-disk.img` had changed (the same recurring store-path/tool-drift class as the M21/M23 busybox pin). **This is now FIXED** (see the "Determinism-harness hardening (fs-image byte-reproducibility)" note below): `build-linux.sh` §2g/§2h now pin the `mke2fs` store path AND freeze every mke2fs timestamp via a pinned `libfaketime` (this e2fsprogs 1.47.0 build IGNORES `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`), so `sql-disk.img`/`redis-disk.img` are byte-identical across rebuilds and the M26/M27 baselines recorded above are the NEW STABLE values. No L1 restarts; clean unload.

**Honest scope:** WHOLE-BOOT (free-run) determinism, as M16/M25/M26. The `always`-vs-`everysec` contrast is the compelling documented-window result; F3's seed-sensitivity is demonstrated via a lying-disk DROP that corrupts the AOF beyond recovery (redis refuses to load it — "committed data unrecoverable", itself a durability violation) rather than a clean truncation (redis's multi-part AOF does not cleanly truncate a mid-file hole; a lower prob giving a NAMED surviving-prefix loss is a cheap follow-on). The idle-heartbeat is a synthetic periodic tick (a deterministic-hypervisor primitive for co-located services), not a change to any service; the two-boot crash faithfully discards RAM + the volatile cache and keeps only FLUSH-committed bytes.

### Determinism-harness hardening (fs-image byte-reproducibility) — DONE (verified in L1)

**The ext2 disk images behind the M26 (SQLite) and M27 (Redis) flagships were NOT byte-reproducible, so their fingerprint baselines DRIFTED across rebuilds** — and a drifting baseline cannot detect a real regression, which undermines the entire verify model. Fixed in `build-linux.sh` §2g/§2h (the `sql-disk.img`/`redis-disk.img` `mke2fs` calls, now a shared `mkext2_reproducible` helper). The zeroed raw images (`kv-disk.img`, `vblk-disk.img`) were already deterministic and are untouched.

**Root cause — two independent drift vectors, both found empirically on the remote:**
1. **`mke2fs` stamps live wall-clock timestamps that `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` does NOT seal.** This box's e2fsprogs (1.47.0) IGNORES `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`: the superblock "Filesystem created / Last write / Last checked" AND every inode's a/c/m/crtime are stamped with the real time on every build (verified via `dumpe2fs`/`debugfs` — they read 2026, not the 2020 epoch). Two mke2fs runs inside the same wall-clock second matched; runs seconds apart differed — the tell-tale of a live timestamp. (The random UUID and directory hash seed were already pinned via `-U`/`-E hash_seed=`, so the timestamps were the remaining in-image drift.)
2. **The `mke2fs` (e2fsprogs) binary itself drifts as the shared box's nix store churns** — the same recurring class that moved the busybox pin (M21/M23). Caught red-handed: the previously-staged `build/sql-disk.img` (`af4a1a6d…`) did not even match what the current default `mke2fs` produces, i.e. it had been built by a now-replaced tool/config.

**The fix — pin EVERYTHING so a rebuild is byte-identical:** fixed UUID (`-U`), fixed directory `hash_seed`, `-E lazy_itable_init=0,lazy_journal_init=0` (nothing written lazily); timestamps FROZEN by a pinned **`libfaketime`** (`/nix/store/fs0gp7zyfjnbvaq82yvwbcsvqh7876d1-libfaketime-0.9.10`, with a store-scan then `nix build` fallback) so every `time()` inside `mke2fs` returns one fixed instant and ALL superblock+inode time fields become constant; and the **`mke2fs` store path PINNED** (`/nix/store/24s0phlx9kly7x7qb8gal3mmn51ndmlx-e2fsprogs-1.47.0-bin`, with a store-scan fallback) so the tool cannot drift. `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=1600000000` is still exported (== the frozen faketime instant `2020-09-13 16:26:40`) — harmless and future-proofs an e2fsprogs that does honor it. No `mkfs.vfat`/other fs tool is used (only ext2), so nothing else needed pinning.

**Two-build sha256 identity proof (the reproducibility deliverable):** THREE separate `./build-linux.sh` runs, minutes apart (crossing wall-clock second boundaries — the real test of the seal), produced byte-identical images: `build/sql-disk.img` = `b3b2b9c0f69bdfabd5fb8382b6bc85b632a3178871c25901addcba380c91b7a1`, `build/redis-disk.img` = `64c7e3f5b175c466edc3018397b39c2c5b5ade3d9048348d10a2710b6d52a506`.

**NEW STABLE baselines — the pinned images changed the disk bytes, so these SUPERSEDE the original/drifted M26/M27 values; each was re-established by running the demo in the foreground, and the M26 set was re-confirmed by a second rebuild + re-run:**
- **M26 SQLite** (`run-sqlite-demo.sh` / `hvx_sqlite_verify`, image `b3b2b9c0…`): F1 no-fault OK+deterministic `1c072036545a7e87`; F2 seeded LYING-DISK DROP VIOLATION key=15 (+replay+branch) `7a4ab31fddabe4cc`; F3 different-seed VIOLATION key=31 `dc04dbe3b49624df`; CONTRAST flush-aware survives `2d96a0c788c1fbba`. All four checks PASS; a fresh rebuild + re-run reproduced all four exactly.
- **M27 Redis** (`run-redis-demo.sh` / `hvx_redis_verify`, image `64c7e3f5…`): SANITY everysec no-crash OK `8fb413b379a1fade`; F1 `always` survives OK `ca15ec5acd7b282a`; F2 `everysec` VIOLATION key=1 (replay `fc2db18cac961b51`, branch round-trip `3a146ed7f02b7c56`, durable disk `f8cc48adc9f2c35e`); F3 seeded lying-disk DROP `4af35d75be853246` (seed A) vs `9d0b5a91e734712b` (seed B). All four checks PASS.

**Regressions UNCHANGED (the fix touches only the two fs-image `mke2fs` calls, so nothing else moves):** `deploy-hvx.sh load` — all M3–M25 hand-written tests + F0 pass, every preserved fingerprint identical (iso `7e586473f6e494d7`, pci `3f95ef833747f3ec`, fault `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, net `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, net-fault `883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, bug_f0 `1083e400458728ff`); the with-disk boot regression in both demos stayed `36583d533d7c989b`. insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean, no L1 restarts.

**Also folded in (fingerprint-neutral):** the stale `hvx.c` module banner (load-log `pr_info`, `MODULE_DESCRIPTION`, and file header) said "milestone 26"; bumped to milestone 27. Strings are not hashed, so every fingerprint above is unchanged — confirmed by the identical regression set after the rebuild.

### Milestone 28 — DONE (Rung 3 infrastructure: page-array disk + disk mmap + RAM to ~3 GiB via EPT PD fan-out; verified in L1)

**The RAM + disk scale-up that raises the physical ceiling toward the loopback-first multi-service stack** (`hvx/DESIGN_RUNG3.md` phases **R3.0–R3.3**). Three structural refactors, each fingerprint-neutral (every cap/structure change is INERT unless a VM asks for more), so ALL M3–M27 baselines are preserved. **R3.4 (real ext4 rootfs via `switch_root`) and R3.5 (snapshot-at-scale / the L1-RAM fork) are the next, USER-GATED step — NOT done here** (R3.5 needs an L0-side `run-l1.sh -m` decision; see below).

- **R3.0 — the backing disk is now a PAGE ARRAY** (`struct hvx_vblk.disk_pages[]` + `n_disk_pages`, replacing the one contiguous `void *disk`), the M11 recipe applied to the disk: individually-allocated order-0 `__get_free_page` pages in a `kvmalloc_array`, so a GB-scale image is a robust per-page allocation (not a fragile multi-GB `kvmalloc`/`vmalloc`) with each page's refcount correct for the mmap fault handler (the M11 `BUG: Bad page state` lesson). Two page-split helpers carry the byte semantics unchanged: `vblk_disk_rw` (disk↔guest RAM, splitting the disk range at page boundaries and delegating each fragment to `hvx_guest_rw`) and `disk_write_bytes` (the M26 flush-aware `vblk_unsync_commit` target). `HVX_SET/GET_DISK` became page-wise `vblk_disk_from_user`/`vblk_disk_to_user`; the free path is `vblk_disk_free`; `vblk_disk_alloc` allocates + zeroes. All `d->disk` "device present" checks became `d->disk_pages`. **Pure byte-identical refactor** — proven by the full disk-test suite below.
- **R3.1 — disk `mmap` window + `HVX_DISK_MAX` 64 MiB → 4 GiB.** One fd now exposes TWO mmap regions: guest RAM at offset 0 (as before) and the disk image at `mmap` offset `HVX_DISK_MMAP_PGOFF*PAGE_SIZE` (`HVX_DISK_MMAP_PGOFF = 1<<32`, a sentinel far above any real RAM page index), via a new `hvx_disk_fault`/`hvx_disk_ops` that indexes `disk_pages[]` (mirroring `hvx_vm_fault`, `get_page` for the refcount). `hvx_mmap` dispatches on `vm_pgoff`. So a GB-scale rootfs is loaded + fingerprinted zero-copy over the page cache — no whole-image `copy_*_user`. `HVX_SET_DISK` with `data==0` loads a ZEROED image (fill via the mmap); small disks still round-trip through `HVX_SET/GET_DISK` byte-identically.
- **R3.2 — `MAX_GPAGES` 65536 (256 MiB) → 786432 (3 GiB).** A pure cap bump; a VM created at 256 MiB allocates the identical pages + single-PD EPT and is byte-identical.
- **R3.3 — EPT PD fan-out.** The EPT top was three fixed pages with ONE PD (`ept_pg[0..2]`), addressing at most 512 EPT PT pages = **1 GiB**. Now `ept_pg[2]` = {PML4, PDPT} and the PD level FANS OUT: `ept_pd[]` = `n_ept_pd = ceil(n_ept_pt/512)` PD pages (`kvmalloc_array`), PDPT entry `p` → PD page `p`, `build_ept` placing `l2 = ept_pd[k/512]`, index `k%512`. 4 KiB EPT leaves throughout (guest RAM is deliberately non-contiguous order-0 pages that 2 MiB leaves can't map, per M11). A 1.5 GiB guest builds `768 EPT PT / 2 EPT PD` (dmesg-confirmed).

**GUARDRAIL — the ≤3 GiB cap and the MMIO hole (load-bearing).** Guest-physical space is FLAT (GPA page `i` → `guest_pages[i]`), and the IOAPIC (`0xfec00000`), LAPIC (`0xfee00000`), and virtio BARs (`0xfe000000`/`0xfe020000`) live in the 4th GiB and are DELIBERATELY EPT-unmapped so accesses trap and are decoded in-kernel. If `mem_size` reached `HVX_MMIO_HOLE_BASE = 0xfe000000` (≈3.968 GiB), `build_ept` would back those GPAs with real RAM and **silently break every MMIO trap — no fault, just wrong bytes**. So `MAX_GPAGES` (3 GiB / 0xc0000000) sits below the hole, an over-cap `HVX_CREATE_VM` is rejected `-EINVAL`, and two `BUILD_BUG_ON`s enforce it at compile time (RAM below the hole; the whole EPT PD level fits in the single 512-entry PDPT). Going past ~3.9 GiB needs the PCI-hole split (RAM below the hole + RAM above 4 GiB + a split e820) — a designed, deferred follow-on (`DESIGN_RUNG3 §3.2.3`).

**Scale test (`hvx/hvx_scale_test.c`, Linux-free, in `deploy-hvx.sh`).** PART A: a 1.5 GiB guest installs a 1 GiB PDPT leaf from userspace (through the RAM mmap — the module only identity-maps the low 1 GiB by default, so this reaches a GVA above 1 GiB WITHOUT perturbing the module's default page tables), then writes/reads a magic at GPA **1.25 GiB** — proving BOTH the guest 1 GiB paging leaf AND that the **second EPT PD page** maps that GPA. PART B: a **128 MiB** page-array disk (2× the old cap) is written through the disk mmap at low/page-crossing(4090)/mid(64 MiB)/high(128 MiB−8) offsets and read back byte-for-byte through `HVX_GET_DISK`, proving the disk mmap and the page-array copy path alias the same pages across the whole image (incl. a value split across a disk-page boundary). "MILESTONE 28 PASS".

**Regressions — ALL preserved, verified this session (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean incl. no `Bad page state`, no L1 restarts, clean unload):**
- **`deploy-hvx.sh load`** — every M3–M27 hand-written test PASS with fingerprints UNCHANGED: iso `7e586473f6e494d7`, pci `3f95ef833747f3ec`, fault `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, net `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, net-fault `883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, bug_f0 `1083e400458728ff`.
- **Boots UNCHANGED:** no-disk `640ece2f900fe5b3` (`hvx_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL), with-disk `36583d533d7c989b` (`hvx_disk_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL — the real-Linux disk read through the new page-array `HVX_GET_DISK` path).
- **M26 SQLite fs-image baselines UNCHANGED** (`hvx_sqlite_verify`, exercises the flush-aware `disk_write_bytes` commit path): F1 `1c072036545a7e87`, F2 `7a4ab31fddabe4cc`, F3 `dc04dbe3b49624df`, CONTRAST `2d96a0c788c1fbba` — all four checks PASS (replay + branch identical).
- **M27 Redis fs-image baselines UNCHANGED** (`hvx_redis_verify`): SANITY `8fb413b379a1fade`, F1 `ca15ec5acd7b282a`, F2 replay `fc2db18cac961b51`/branch `3a146ed7f02b7c56`/durable `f8cc48adc9f2c35e`, F3 `4af35d75be853246`/`9d0b5a91e734712b` — all four checks PASS.

**M17 (PMU fast clock) stays BLOCKED — untouched** (hybrid-silicon hardware limit; see the M17 section). **Next (user-gated): R3.4** (stage a byte-reproducible ext4 root disk, tiny initramfs `switch_root` off `/dev/vda` — prefer an **ro root + a small rw data disk** to sidestep the ext4-journal reproducibility question) **and R3.5** (fingerprint + branch at GB scale) — R3.5's GB-scale snapshot/branch double-buffer does NOT fit L1's 4 GiB, so it needs either a file-backed snapshot, ro-root single-copy, or a user-approved `run-l1.sh -m` raise (an L0/shared-host change — do NOT force it).

### Milestone 29 — DONE (Rung 3 rootfs: real ext4 read-only root via switch_root + a small rw disk, snapshot-at-scale; verified in L1) — phases R3.4 + R3.5

**Real Linux now boots from a byte-reproducible ext4 READ-ONLY root (via `switch_root`, instead of the in-RAM initramfs) with a small ext2 READ-WRITE data disk, and determinism holds AT SCALE with the snapshot capturing ONLY guest RAM + the small rw disk — the big static ro-root is EXCLUDED (re-attached identically, its hash asserted stable).** This is the platform step that lets GB-scale rootfs images fit under L1's 4 GiB: only RAM + the small rw disk are ever double-buffered. **It is USERSPACE + BUILD ONLY — `hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h`/`Makefile` are UNCHANGED from M28, so the module `.ko` is byte-identical and EVERY M3–M28 fingerprint holds by construction (all re-verified GREEN below). M17 stays BLOCKED, untouched.**

**The chosen model (the user's, done exactly): ONE MBR-partitioned virtio-blk `/dev/vda`, not a second block device.** No new virtio-blk function, no run-loop/VMX change (the design's constraint). The single M28 page-array disk carries both partitions:
- **p1 @ LBA 2048** = **ext4 ro ROOT** (busybox + `/sbin/init` = workload + oracle), the big static image, mounted `ro,noload` so the guest NEVER writes it.
- **p2 @ LBA 1050624** = **ext2 rw DATA disk** (the workload's scratch; the seeded-fault target).

The MBR is hand-crafted (fully deterministic; no partitioning tool to drift — only the LBA/size fields matter to Linux). The tiny initramfs (`build/initramfs-rootfs.cpio.gz`) loads the virtio-blk + ext4 + ext2 module stack, mounts `/dev/vda1` ro, and `switch_root`s into it; the real root's `/sbin/init` mounts `/dev/vda2` rw on `/data`, writes NKEYS committed records, `sync` + drops the page cache (so the oracle reads the DEVICE, not the cache — the M25/M26 load-bearing point), then an oracle re-reads them → `HYDO-ORACLE: OK|VIOLATION` + `hydo-rootfs-marker: workload complete`.

**The R3.5 snapshot-at-scale model (`hvx/hvx_rootfs_verify.c`, RAM = 512 MiB).** A full snapshot is `HVX_GET_SNAPSHOT` + `memcpy(save_ram, RAM_mmap)` + `memcpy(save_rw, disk_mmap + p2_off, p2_len)` — **the ro-root (p1) is NOT saved**. On restore/branch we re-attach only p2 (via the M28 disk mmap); p1 is left byte-untouched (the guest never wrote it under `ro,noload`), so it is re-attached identically. The harness ASSERTS the ro-root is static by hashing p1 after every boot and comparing to the offline image's p1 bytes (`d13350ba21cfc7f8`, STABLE across all boots) — if a byte of p1 ever changed, the branch's correctness (and the assertion) would fail. Everything is **zero-copy**: the partitioned image is LOADED with `HVX_SET_DISK(data=0)` (a zeroed page array) filled by `memcpy` into the disk mmap; RAM + p2 are fingerprinted in place over the shared mmaps — no whole-image GB `copy_*_user`.

**ext4 byte-reproducibility (the new reproducibility surface vs. the ext2/M26 hardening) — SOLVED.** `mke2fs -d` populates the fs from a source directory and copies each source file's **real `st_ctime`**, which `touch` cannot set and this e2fsprogs (1.47.0) does NOT clamp (it freezes atime/mtime/crtime via libfaketime but leaks ctime). Two builds seconds apart then differ by exactly the per-inode ctime (32–120 bytes). **Fix (proven byte-identical): build a SIMPLE-layout ext4 (`-O ^metadata_csum,^64bit,^flex_bg,^resize_inode` — a flat, trivially parseable inode table and no checksums to keep consistent), then a python pass ZEROES `i_ctime` (inode offset 0x0C) for every inode.** The rest is the SAME pinned discipline as the ext2 images (pinned `mke2fs` + `libfaketime`-frozen time + fixed UUID + fixed hash_seed + non-lazy init). Mounted `ro,noload`, the offline-built clean journal is never touched at runtime, so the root stays byte-stable for the whole boot. **Two-build sha256 identity (the deliverable): `build/rootfs-ext4.img` = `6df9f82c33e04290f640253c830f3e6b4c6346d6e5053b66d8718eeb4731e922` (512 MiB), `build/rootfs-disk.img` = `a4aefaf1b623cb49a0b89c22eaaa6c663db685e09f6138a701aeef9e51901779` (529 MiB) — identical across separate full `build-linux.sh` runs crossing wall-clock boundaries.**

**Two real-Linux boot walls found + fixed (each by booting to a wall and decoding it):** (1) `EXT4-fs (vda1): Cannot load crc32c driver` — ext4/jbd2 need the **crc32c crypto shash** at MOUNT time, which is a module (`crc32c_generic`) with no autoloader in the initramfs; stage + insmod it explicitly. (2) `/dev/vda2` was missing in the pivoted root → **`mount --move /dev /newroot/dev`** before `switch_root` (carry the working devtmpfs across), so the real `/sbin/init` can reach the rw data disk. The ext4 ro-root mounts, `switch_root` succeeds, and the ext2 rw workload runs to `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=32 durable`.

**The four checks at scale (`hvx_rootfs_verify.c`, NEW baseline) — all PASS:**
- **(1) REPLAY:** two fresh boots bit-identical (console + snapshot + 512 MiB RAM + rw disk), verdict OK → **`604b9c28083cd0e3`**.
- **(2) ROUND-TRIP:** `GET_SNAPSHOT` + save(RAM, rw) then perturb + `SET_SNAPSHOT` + restore == same → `604b9c28083cd0e3` (the rw-disk-only snapshot is complete).
- **(3) BRANCH:** snapshot at a clean pre-disk kernel-init marker (`Trying to unpack rootfs image as initramfs` — before any virtio/disk I/O is in flight, the same MID point the SQLite harness uses), continue twice (straight + restored) → **both `604b9c28083cd0e3` == a fresh boot**; the whole module-load + ext4 mount + `switch_root` + ext2 rw workload is reproduced from the RAM+rw snapshot with the ro-root excluded. (An earlier MID marker taken mid-disk-I/O gave a stop-point artifact — branch straight==restored but ≠ fresh boot; the pre-disk marker fixed it, exactly the M27 "branch honesty" lesson.)
- **(4) SEED-SENSITIVITY:** a seeded virtio-blk **DROP** fault on the rw-disk writes (DROP is WRITE-only, and the root is `ro` → DROP hits ONLY the rw-data writes) loses a committed record (oracle VIOLATION); seed A (`0x2905A0`) → **`d4a7ee441554647b`** (reproducible x2) and seed B (`0x2905A1`) → **`968ecdf20376986f`** (≠ A), each a reproducible VIOLATION — the fault is genuinely consumed and the RAM+rw snapshot captures the fault schedule.
- **ro-root static:** `d13350ba21cfc7f8`, identical across all 6 boots and == the offline image's p1 hash → EXCLUDED from the snapshot yet provably re-attached identically.

"hvx_rootfs_verify: MILESTONE 29 PASS."

**Regressions — ALL preserved, re-verified this session (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean incl. no `Bad page state` on the disk mmap paths, no L1 restarts, clean unload):**
- **`deploy-hvx.sh load`** — every M3–M27 hand-written test PASS + M28 scale (MILESTONE 28 PASS), fingerprints UNCHANGED: iso `7e586473f6e494d7`, pci `3f95ef833747f3ec`, fault `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, net `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, net-fault `883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, bug_f0 `1083e400458728ff`.
- **Boots UNCHANGED:** no-disk `640ece2f900fe5b3` (`hvx_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL), with-disk `36583d533d7c989b` (`hvx_disk_boot_verify`).
- **M26 SQLite fs-image baselines UNCHANGED** (`hvx_sqlite_verify`, re-run — exercises the flush-aware `disk_write_bytes` commit path + the reproducible ext2 image): F1 `1c072036545a7e87`, F2 `7a4ab31fddabe4cc`, F3 `dc04dbe3b49624df`, CONTRAST `2d96a0c788c1fbba`.
- **M27 Redis + M25 hydokv baselines** preserved by construction (the `.ko` is byte-identical to M28 and `redis-disk.img`/`kv-disk.img` are byte-reproducible + unchanged — §2h/§2e untouched); not re-run this session (each is ~8–10 boots), but the SQLite re-run + the byte-identical module directly confirm the disk/fs paths are unperturbed.

**RAM ceiling honored (no `run-l1.sh` change).** Guest RAM is 512 MiB — moderate, the FIRST >256 MiB real-Linux boot (it exercises the M28 RAM scale-up end-to-end under real Linux; 512 MiB = 256 EPT PT, single EPT PD, no fan-out) — and it fits L1's 4 GiB comfortably even for a branch: kernel holds the RAM+disk page arrays (~1 GiB), the harness holds the RAM save (512 MiB) + the small rw save (16 MiB), with the ro-root NEVER double-buffered. `run-l1.sh` `-m 4096` is untouched; the ro-root exclusion is precisely what makes this fit. The ext4 root scales further (the mechanism is size-independent — the snapshot cost is bounded by RAM + the small rw disk, not the root size).

**Next — Rung 4:** multiple services co-located in one L2 guest over the in-kernel `127.0.0.1` loopback (the loopback-first destination), building a launcher on this real-rootfs platform. Deferred (unchanged from M28): the >3.9 GiB PCI-hole split (needed only for a single service ≥4 GiB RAM); a rw ext4 root (the journal-reproducibility question — sidestepped here by ro-root + a small rw ext2 data disk).

### Milestone 30 — DONE (Rung 4: a multi-service SYSTEM over loopback, a system-level bug reproduced bit-for-bit; verified in L1)

**M27 ran ONE service (unmodified redis) with a direct redis-cli client, and the invariant was Redis's OWN data. Rung 4 raises this to a SYSTEM: TWO real services co-located in ONE deterministic L2 guest, talking over the in-kernel `127.0.0.1` LOOPBACK, plus a client — and the invariant is SYSTEM-LEVEL (it spans both services). A seeded durability fault surfaces a CROSS-SERVICE disagreement, reproduced bit-for-bit.** It is **USERSPACE + BUILD ONLY — `hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h`/`Makefile` are UNCHANGED from M28/M29, so the module `.ko` is byte-identical and EVERY M3–M29 fingerprint holds by construction** (all re-verified GREEN below). **M17 stays BLOCKED, untouched.**

**The system shape (the preferred shape, done exactly): a client → an app tier → redis, three co-located processes over loopback.**
```
   client (hydoapp load/verify)  --loopback:8080-->  hydoapp server (the app tier, service A)
                                                     --loopback:6379-->  redis-server (service B)
```
- **hydoapp** (`hvx/hydoapp.c`, ~330 lines, ONE static-musl binary with server + client roles) is a REAL second service, itself **backed by redis over loopback** (it speaks Redis's RESP protocol on a persistent connection). It is an application tier / aggregator, NOT a proxy that leaks the store: `LOAD <n>` writes `led:0..led:n-1` into redis and ACKs the client only after redis `+OK`s all `n` (so an ACK means "the SYSTEM accepted n committed writes"); `VERIFY <n>` reads them back with one `MGET` and computes an **AGGREGATE** (sum of the committed integers + which keys are missing); `PING` is a readiness probe. Deterministic by construction (fixed keys, value a pure function of the key, NO time/RNG). Values carry a fixed 1 KiB non-space pad so each committed value spans an AOF page.
- **redis-server + redis-cli** are the SAME unmodified static `pkgsStatic.redis` 7.2.4 as M27 (redis-cli is used only for the readiness poll; the SUT data path is client→app→redis). Persistence is the AOF on a real ext2 fs on `/dev/vda`.
- All ADDITIVE: it reuses M27's redis binaries + the flush-aware fault + the deterministic CRNG seed (`hvx_seedrng`) + the M27 idle-heartbeat (`HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT` — both services block in `accept`/`epoll_wait` on loopback data whose delivery needs a softirq/tick). New artifacts only, so §2h (redis) is byte-untouched and the M27 baselines are preserved.

**The SYSTEM-LEVEL invariant: the aggregate the app RECOMPUTES from redis == the aggregate the client COMMITTED.** A durability fault that makes redis lose a committed write makes the app return a WRONG/STALE answer to the client — the app (service A) and the client's committed history DISAGREE. That cross-service disagreement is the Rung-4 bug (vs M27's single-service "Redis lost its own key"). Committed aggregate for `NKEYS=48` = `sum(100000+k) = 4801128`.

**The crash model is M27's, unchanged (NO new kernel code): a two-boot power loss over a VOLATILE WRITE CACHE** (`HVX_FAULT_DROP_UNFLUSHED` at prob 100; a write is durable only after a virtio-blk FLUSH, which Linux issues on fsync). **boot1 (WORKLOAD, fault armed):** bring up the whole system, the client commits N values through the app, then `SIGSTOP` redis at the just-acked instant → `HVX_GET_DISK` = the DURABLE image (only FLUSH-committed AOF bytes). **boot2 (ORACLE, fresh VM, no fault):** `HVX_SET_DISK` the durable image, redis AOF-replays, the app tier comes up, the client re-verifies the aggregate THROUGH the app. Because FLUSH is the sole gate, the SYSTEM outcome is a pure function of the redis fsync POLICY: `always` fsyncs (=FLUSH=durable) BEFORE it `+OK`s the app's SET → survives; `everysec` batches the fsync ~1/s so the just-committed writes sit un-flushed → lost → the app's aggregate disagrees.

**The four checks at the SYSTEM level (`hvx/hvx_system_verify.c`, modeled on `hvx_redis_verify.c`; full-state fingerprint = console incl. the verdict + snapshot + 256 MiB RAM + 16 MiB disk; two-boot crash-recover; 15 real boots) — all PASS:**
- **(SANITY) everysec, NO fault, NO crash:** `HYDO-ORACLE: OK keys=48 durable aggregate=4801128` — the whole system (client↔app↔redis over loopback + AOF) is correct absent the power loss → **new baseline `8273881da521795f`**.
- **(F1) DURABLE CONFIG (`always`) under the volatile-cache power loss → OK:** every committed value fsync'd (=FLUSHed=durable) before the app's ACK, so the whole system SURVIVES; two full crash-recover runs bit-identical → **new OK baseline `839af875cff55439`**. (The durable disk is a pure function: all three produce-durable calls hash `591762638417436d` under `always`.)
- **(F2) DOCUMENTED WINDOW (`everysec`) under the SAME power loss → SYSTEM VIOLATION:** a committed value is unretrievable through the app — `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION key=1 expected=100001 got=MISSING`, the app's aggregate DISAGREES with the client's committed history; the failing run **replays** bit-for-bit (`a2f5ac776b6d5197`) AND **branch-from-snapshots** bit-for-bit (round-trip `792c217a4ab36282`, durable outcome `b28570a257c49b0f` == the straight run's). **F1-vs-F2 IS the `always`-vs-`everysec` contrast at the SYSTEM level** (the durable disk itself differs: `always` `591762638417436d` full vs `everysec` `55cea5fc314e5988` lossy), isolating exactly the AOF fsync window as it surfaces across services.
- **(F3) SEED-SENSITIVE:** a seeded LYING-DISK DROP (prob 8% on `always`) corrupts the AOF differently per seed — seed `0x5157E30` → `5f90434ee8672eb6`, seed `0x5157E31` → `1004daaff10d6f45` (≠), each a reproducible `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION` (redis refuses the corrupt AOF: committed data unrecoverable — itself a system-level durability violation) — proving the fault is really consumed by the seed (durable disks `1bc4580e81544037` vs `7d92b2e9b05e7692`).

"hvx_system_verify: MILESTONE 30 PASS."

**Byte-reproducibility (load-bearing — the new baseline must be STABLE).** The new artifacts are byte-identical across separate `build-linux.sh` runs crossing wall-clock boundaries: `build/hydoapp` = `63850b54…` (static musl via the pinned gcc, like hydokv/seedrng), `build/initramfs-system.cpio.gz` = `dd6ce66e…`, `build/system-disk.img` = `b5b949f6…`. **One reproducibility bug found + fixed:** the shared `mkext2_reproducible` uses `libfaketime` WITHOUT the `@` freeze prefix, so its faked clock advances in real time; the existing images (redis/sql/rootfs) complete fast enough that mke2fs's `time()` calls all round to the start second, but `system-disk.img`'s metadata layout put mke2fs near a second boundary and two full builds landed on DIFFERENT seconds (`f92e…` vs `7e22…`). Fix (localized to §2j — the shared helper and every existing image stay byte-identical): a python pass ZEROES every superblock + inode time field of `system-disk.img` after mke2fs (the same technique as the M29 ext4 ctime normalizer), making it a pure function of structure + the pinned UUID/hash_seed; e2fsck-clean, and byte-identical across sessions.

**Regressions — ALL preserved, re-verified this session (insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean, no L1 restarts [L1 QEMU up 2+ days throughout], clean unload):**
- **`deploy-hvx.sh load`** — every M3–M27 hand-written test PASS + M28 scale (MILESTONE 28 PASS), fingerprints UNCHANGED: iso `7e586473f6e494d7`, pci `3f95ef833747f3ec`, fault `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, net `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, net-fault `883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, bug_f0 `1083e400458728ff`.
- **Boots UNCHANGED:** no-disk `640ece2f900fe5b3` (`hvx_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL), with-disk `36583d533d7c989b` (`hvx_disk_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL).
- **M27 Redis + M29 rootfs assets byte-untouched** (`redis-disk.img` = `64c7e3f5…`, `rootfs-disk.img` = `a4aefaf1…`, both UNCHANGED; §2h/§2i untouched, the `.ko` byte-identical), so their baselines hold by construction; not re-run this session (each is many boots).

**RAM ceiling honored (no `run-l1.sh` change).** Guest RAM is 256 MiB — ample (redis-server 3.4 MB + hydoapp 50 KB static + redis-cli + a 48-key dataset + AOF on the 16 MiB disk), well under the ≤1.5 GiB request and L1's 4 GiB, even for a branch (RAM save 256 MiB + disk save 16 MiB). `run-l1.sh` untouched.

**Honest scope:** WHOLE-BOOT (free-run) determinism, as M16/M25/M26/M27. The `always`-vs-`everysec` contrast is the compelling documented-window result, now surfaced ACROSS services (the app disagrees with the client, not just redis losing its own key). F2's branch reproduces the snapshot round-trip in FULL state AND the durable outcome (verdict console + AOF) bit-for-bit (the M27 branch-honesty property; the full-256-MiB endpoint of a branch vs a separate straight run differs only by post-marker idle-heartbeat bookkeeping). F3's seed-sensitivity is via a lying-disk DROP that corrupts the AOF beyond recovery (redis refuses to load it) rather than a named surviving-prefix loss — a cheap follow-on, as noted at M27.
