**Next — Rung 5 (JVM / Kafka):** run a JVM-based service (Kafka in single-process KRaft mode) deterministically — the single hardest nondeterminism surface (JIT/GC background threads, `System.nanoTime`, `SecureRandom`, identity hash codes), needing Rung 3's scale-up for the JRE. See `hvx/ROADMAP.md` §Rung 5.

### Milestone 31 — DONE (Part A: deterministic JVM) / WALL (Part B: Kafka) — Rung 5: run a real OpenJDK JRE deterministically; verified in L1

**PART A (the milestone) — a REAL, unmodified upstream OpenJDK 17 JRE runs a real multi-threaded Java workload DETERMINISTICALLY under hvx: two boots from scratch reach a BIT-IDENTICAL full state (guest RAM + rw disk + console) `4b8ffacde50f1169`, and round-trip + branch-from-snapshot reproduce it.** The JVM is the largest nondeterminism surface in the whole project; this proves hvx tames it. The SUT is the pinned static-nix `openjdk-minimal-jre-17.0.7+7` (a 595 MiB glibc-2.38 closure, **unmodified**) staged onto the M29 ext4 READ-ONLY root, running `hvx/HydoWork.java` — a real program that DELIBERATELY exercises every JVM nondeterminism source the ROADMAP names, then writes 32 committed key records to a small ext2 rw data disk (the M25/M26/M29 durability-fault target). Built + booted exactly on the M29 rootfs model: the big JRE lives on the ro-root, which is **EXCLUDED from the snapshot** (the R3.5 point — a hundreds-of-MiB JRE does NOT bloat the snapshot; only guest RAM + the small rw disk are captured, the ro-root hash-asserted stable `15564d928ede886d`). The four checks (`hvx/hvx_jvm_verify.c`, modeled on `hvx_rootfs_verify.c`) all PASS: **REPLAY** two fresh JVM boots identical `4b8ffacde50f1169`; **ROUND-TRIP** identical; **BRANCH** snapshot at a pre-initramfs kernel marker → continue twice → both `4b8ffacde50f1169` (the WHOLE JVM boot + run reproduced from the RAM+rw snapshot, ro-root excluded); **SEED-SENSITIVITY** a seeded virtio-blk DROP on the rw-disk writes loses a committed record differently per seed (A=`0dff66ffe1a4f19b`, B=`0630ba2b9f5cec79`, ≠, each reproducible, both oracle VIOLATION). "hvx_jvm_verify: MILESTONE 31 PASS."

**The per-source JVM-nondeterminism map (each exercised by HydoWork + PRINTED to the console, which is IN the fingerprint — so an untamed source would DIVERGE the two-boot fingerprint and be caught; the bit-identical `4b8ffacde50f1169` proves ALL are tamed):**
- **`System.nanoTime()` / `currentTimeMillis()`** → the deterministic virtual TSC (M4–M6/M16): the vDSO clock reads the fixed-step TSC at a deterministic instruction, so both calls return bit-identical values across boots. Deterministic **already**, proven by the fingerprint.
- **`SecureRandom` (default provider reads `/dev/urandom`) + `getrandom`** → the **M27 CRNG seeder** (`hvx_seedrng`, run once by `/init` before java). Without it the JVM's `SecureRandom`/`getrandom` would spin/hang on the jitter-free deterministic TSC exactly like Redis did (M27). With it the CRNG stream is a fixed function of the seeded entropy → the SecureRandom bytes (`secrand=…` in the console) are bit-identical across boots.
- **JIT compilation** → **TAMED by `-Xint`** (interpreter-only: no C1/C2 background compiler threads, no code-cache mutation, no invocation-counter/timer-driven compile decisions). This is the ROADMAP's recommended first-landing choice; it removes the single riskiest thread/timing surface entirely.
- **GC** → **`-XX:+UseSerialGC`** (single-threaded stop-the-world collector: no concurrent/parallel background GC threads) + a **FIXED heap** (`-Xms64m -Xmx64m`, so the collection cadence is a pure function of the deterministic allocation stream). GC genuinely RUNS: the workload churns ~160 MB of garbage (4 threads × 500k × ~80 B) through a 64 MB heap, so completing `rc=0` PROVES the collector ran (the minimal JRE omits the management beans, so `getCollectionCount()` reads 0 — hence completion, not the bean, is the proof).
- **Many threads on the SINGLE deterministic vCPU** → deterministic under free-run (M16/M27/M30 proved multi-thread/multi-process free-run determinism; the deterministic timer/idle-heartbeat drives scheduling at deterministic instruction counts). HydoWork PROVES it aggressively: 4 worker threads (plus the JVM's own VM/GC/reference-handler/finalizer/signal-dispatcher threads) contend on an **ORDER-DEPENDENT** locked accumulator (`state = state*M + x`, non-commutative), so the final `state=…` is a fingerprint of the EXACT lock-acquisition interleaving — its bit-identical reproduction across boots is a direct scheduler-determinism proof.
- **Identity hash codes (`System.identityHashCode`)** → deterministic under the no-ASLR deterministic allocator + deterministic HotSpot hash seeding; the `idhash=…` values reproduce bit-for-bit (needed NO pinning — the default `-XX:hashCode` scheme is deterministic under hvx).
- **hsperfdata (a hidden source)** → **`-XX:-UsePerfData`** disables the memory-mapped `/tmp/hsperfdata_*` instrumentation file the JVM otherwise updates continuously with live counters/timestamps. Plus `-XX:-UseContainerSupport` (skip cgroup probing), `-XX:ActiveProcessorCount=1`, `-Xshare:off` (no CDS-archive mmap), pinned locale/tz (`-Duser.timezone=UTC` etc.), `-Djava.security.egd=file:/dev/urandom`.

**The ONE module change (`hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h`) — additive + OPT-IN + inert-by-default, so ALL prior fingerprints hold by construction (the M27 gating discipline).** A distro glibc-2.38 binary aborts at load with **`libc.so.6: CPU ISA level is lower than required`**: the M12 synthetic CPUID omits **MMX** (an x86-64-**baseline** requirement) and the whole nixpkgs userland is built for ≥ x86-64-v2. New flag **`HVX_VM_X86_64_V2`** (`struct hvx_config.flags` bit 1, the same field as M27's `HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT`; → `vm->cpu_v2`) makes `hvx_cpuid` OR in MMX (leaf 1 EDX) + SSE3/SSSE3/CX16/SSE4.1/SSE4.2/POPCNT (leaf 1 ECX) + LAHF/SAHF (leaf 0x80000001 ECX). All **deterministic integer/SSE** features — **NO AVX/XSAVE/OSXSAVE** (the guest still never runs XSETBV), and the L1 host CPU supports them natively so any v2 instruction the guest runs executes correctly. **Default off → the kernel records the exact M12 feature flags in RAM → every prior boot fingerprint is byte-identical** (an ungated CPUID bump WOULD move every boot fingerprint; the gate is why M3–M30 are preserved). The JVM harness sets `HVX_VM_X86_64_V2 | HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT`; `hvx_linux` sets it from env `HVX_CPU_V2` (+ `HVX_IDLE_HEARTBEAT`). This was the load wall — found by booting to it and decoding `java exited rc=127` → the glibc ISA message.

**Speed + scale (the honest operating numbers).** Free-run JVM boot is FAST: **~5 s per boot** (kernel boot + the `-Xint` JVM run to the oracle), so the whole 4-check verify (8 boots + snapshot/branch) is **~40 s** wall. (`-Xint` costs ~5.5× vs JIT on this workload — 1178 ms vs 214 ms native — acceptable for HydoWork; it is the crux of the Part-B wall below.) Guest RAM **640 MiB**; the ext4 ro-root is **720 MiB** (holds the 598 MiB JRE closure + busybox + `hvx_seedrng` + `/app/HydoWork.class`), the ext2 rw data disk 16 MiB, the MBR-partitioned `jvm-disk.img` 737 MiB (sha256 `3169d6146ce25a67…`). The snapshot double-buffer (RAM + the small rw disk, ro-root excluded) fits L1's 4 GiB comfortably — exactly the M29 model paying off for a large image.

**PART B (STRETCH) — Kafka: a documented WALL, with evidence.** Apache Kafka 3.5.0 (KRaft, single-process) IS available (pinned `apache-kafka-2.13-3.5.0`, 103 MiB / 105 jars), but a **fingerprint-proven deterministic Kafka is not achievable within the standing constraints this session**, for four concretely-evidenced reasons: (1) **`-Xint` startup speed.** Part A established `-Xint` is required to make the JVM deterministic (kill JIT/compiler threads). Measured `-Xint` penalty is ~5.5× even for HydoWork's ONE class; Kafka loads orders of magnitude more (the Scala 2.13 runtime + thousands of Kafka classes) and does KRaft storage-format + Raft-quorum + socket-server + log-manager init, so a broker "started" under `-Xint` on a single free-run vCPU is many minutes PER boot — and the determinism verify needs ~8 boots. (2) **Verify memory envelope.** The determinism check needs the snapshot **double-buffer** (guest RAM + `ram_save`); with Kafka's larger heap (≥256 MiB → ~1 GiB guest RAM) plus a ~850 MiB kafka ro-root disk (kernel page-array + the harness's userspace copy), it presses hard against L1's **fixed 3.4 GiB** available — and `run-l1.sh` must NOT be touched. (3) **Coordination determinism surface.** KRaft's Raft election/heartbeat timers, request purgatory, quota managers, and background log flush/clean threads massively expand the nondeterminism surface vs HydoWork; a bit-identical proof would itself be a milestone-sized taming effort. (4) **Bring-up machinery.** The nix Kafka package assembles its classpath via bash/coreutils wrapper scripts (both `kafka.Kafka` and `kafka.tools.StorageTool` throw `ClassNotFoundException` under a plain `-cp libs/*`), so even a single boot needs that extra closure + a hand-assembled classpath + a KRaft config + a storage-format step. Per the milestone's explicit guidance ("a well-evidenced wall is a good outcome; Part A is the milestone"), Kafka is reported as the wall, not forced/faked. Clear path for a future rung: investigate whether JIT is deterministic under hvx (to drop `-Xint` for speed), and a file-backed snapshot (to fit the verify double-buffer without an L1 RAM raise).

**Regressions — ALL preserved (the module change is fingerprint-neutral by the opt-in gate; re-verified this session; insmod/rmmod rc=0, dmesg clean, no L1 restarts [host up 2 days], clean unload):**
- **`deploy-hvx.sh load`** — every M3–M27 hand-written test PASS + M28 scale (MILESTONE 28 PASS), fingerprints UNCHANGED: iso `7e586473f6e494d7`, pci `3f95ef833747f3ec`, fault `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, net `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, net-fault `883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, bug_f0 `1083e400458728ff`.
- **Boots UNCHANGED:** no-disk `640ece2f900fe5b3` (`hvx_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL), with-disk `36583d533d7c989b` (`hvx_disk_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL) — both create VMs with default flags (`cpu_v2=0`), so the CPUID and every hashed byte are identical to M16/M21.
- **M17 (PMU fast clock) stays BLOCKED — untouched.**

**Honest scope:** WHOLE-BOOT (free-run) determinism, as M16/M25–M30. The two seed-sensitivity boots both lose `key=0` (the first-written record, most often caught at prob 20 %) but with DIFFERENT reproducible fingerprints — genuinely seed-consumed; a named "different key per seed" would be a cheap tune (lower prob), as at M27/M30. `-Xint` + SerialGC + fixed heap is the deterministic-friendly config (the ROADMAP's recommended first landing); whether the JIT is deterministic under hvx (which would restore speed) is left open, and is exactly what a Kafka-scale service would need.

**Next — Rung 6 (the destination):** the full gRPC + Kafka + Redis + MySQL stack co-located over `127.0.0.1` loopback, with disk/clock fault injection on any service and a cross-service oracle. Kafka (this rung's Part B wall) + MySQL are the remaining heavyweight services to tame; the JVM-determinism recipe here (v2 CPUID + `-Xint`/SerialGC/fixed-heap + CRNG + idle heartbeat, ro-root-excluded snapshot) is the reusable foundation. See `hvx/ROADMAP.md` §Rung 6.

### Milestone 32 — Kafka unblock (JIT-determinism finding + file-backed snapshot [+ Kafka native recipe]) — verified in L1

Resolved the two things that walled Kafka at M31 — the `-Xint` speed penalty and the RAM-bound snapshot. **USERSPACE + BUILD ONLY: `hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h`/`Makefile` are UNCHANGED from M31, so the `.ko` is byte-identical and EVERY M3–M31 fingerprint holds by construction (all re-verified GREEN below). M17 stays BLOCKED, untouched.**

**PART 1 — the JIT IS bit-for-bit DETERMINISTIC under hvx (the prize; the GOOD direction).** M31 ran the JVM deterministically only with `-Xint` (interpreter; ~5.5× slower; the #1 Kafka blocker). M32 makes the HotSpot **JIT selectable** via a new kernel-cmdline token `hydo_jit=<config>` in the ro-root `/sbin/init` (`build-linux.sh` §2k; default/unknown → `int` = the M31 `-Xint`), then re-runs the M31 four checks per config. **Result: ALL five configs reproduce bit-for-bit — two fresh boots (REPLAY) AND branch-from-snapshot (BRANCH) are IDENTICAL — INCLUDING the ones with BACKGROUND compiler threads (`c1`, `tiered`).** The reason is exactly the hvx thesis: the whole vCPU is deterministic (the M27 idle-heartbeat scheduler fires at deterministic instruction counts), so the compiler threads race *deterministically* and the compilation decisions/order are a pure function of the instruction stream — no compiler-thread nondeterminism survives. Per-config baselines on the JIT-selectable `build/jvm-disk.img` (each replay==branch IDENTICAL, oracle OK):
- `int` (`-Xint`) = `8744ce0aa8977316`
- `c1` (`-XX:TieredStopAtLevel=1`, background C1) = `36d2be4980583ffc`
- **`c1sync` (`-XX:TieredStopAtLevel=1 -XX:CICompilerCount=1 -XX:-BackgroundCompilation`, ONE synchronous C1) = `393fc95c9dc02118` — FULL four-check PASS** (replay/round-trip/branch all `393fc95c9dc02118`; SEED-SENSITIVITY A=`36d2b59caeacd380` / B=`9ff0bf5cfcd9a4e4`, each a reproducible VIOLATION; ro-root `75836ca5e65d2600` stable)
- `tiered` (default C1+C2, background) = `0019feb57d72c15d`
- `tieredsync` (`-XX:-BackgroundCompilation`, C1+C2 synchronous) = `bb852900661c1b32`

Evidence detail (the harness now prints the workload's observed-values line per boot): across configs `sum`/`idhash`/`control` are IDENTICAL (order-independent sources), while `state=` (the ORDER-DEPENDENT locked accumulator) DIFFERS per config yet is per-config-reproducible — i.e. enabling the JIT genuinely *shifts the thread interleaving* (compilation changes execution timing → different lock-acquisition order), and hvx still reproduces each config's interleaving exactly. `secrand` also differs per config (the /dev/urandom read position depends on the JVM startup path) but is per-config-reproducible. **RECOMMENDED config: `c1sync`** — deterministic (full four-check PASS) with the fewest moving parts (C1-only, one synchronous compiler) and the fastest observed boot. **Speedup:** boot-to-oracle-marker wall `int` 3.93 s vs `c1sync` 3.18 s (c1 3.67, tiered 3.69, tieredsync 3.64) — the java portion drops ~2.5–3× (the ~2.7 s kernel-boot overhead dominates this tiny HydoWork workload, so the total-boot delta understates the java win; a class-heavy service like Kafka gains far more, see Part 3). This directly kills Kafka blocker #1.

**PART 2 — FILE-BACKED snapshot (fit a bigger guest without raising L1 RAM).** The verify harness double-buffered the snapshot (guest RAM + the small rw disk) in a `malloc(mem_size)` — 640 MiB for the JVM, ~1 GiB for Kafka — pressing L1's fixed ~3.4 GiB. `hvx_jvm_verify.c` now writes/reads the save to a **FILE on a real disk-backed path** (`struct savebuf`: `pwrite`/`pread` + `fdatasync` + `posix_fadvise(DONTNEED)`; default dir `/var/tmp/hvx-snap`, which is **ext4 on `/dev/vda1` in L1 — NOT tmpfs**, verified with `df -T`; `HVX_SNAP_MALLOC=1` restores the old in-RAM buffer for an A/B). **No module change — the module already exposes RAM+disk via mmap.** The FINGERPRINT IS UNCHANGED: the M31 verify still passes at **`4b8ffacde50f1169`** (replay/round-trip/branch IDENTICAL; SEED A=`0dff66ffe1a4f19b` / B=`0630ba2b9f5cec79`; ro-root `15564d928ede886d`) via the file-backed snapshot, on the pristine M31 disk (backed up as `build/jvm-disk-m31.img`, sha256 `3169d614…`). **RAM proof (A/B, same mode=roundtrip):** Maximum RSS **2.72 GiB (malloc) → 2.08 GiB (file)**, a ~656 MiB drop = the 640 MiB guest-RAM save no longer sits in anonymous process RAM → a larger (Kafka-scale ~1 GiB) guest snapshot now fits under L1's ceiling. The snapshot files are `unlink`ed-after-`open` (auto-cleanup on close/exit) so nothing lingers.

**PART 3 (STRETCH) — Kafka: 3 of the 4 M31 blockers RESOLVED/de-risked; the native bring-up recipe is validated; the deterministic-guest build (blocker #3) remains the milestone-sized work, NOT attempted this session.**
- **#1 `-Xint` speed → RESOLVED by Part 1** (JIT is deterministic, so `-Xint` is droppable). Concrete Kafka-scale evidence: a NATIVE single-broker KRaft startup (headless JDK, `-Xmx512m -XX:+UseSerialGC`) reaches "Kafka Server started" in **1014 ms with the JIT vs 2018 ms under `-Xint` (~2×)** — and steady-state (compute-bound) would gain much more.
- **#2 verify memory envelope → RESOLVED by Part 2** (file-backed snapshot removes the ~1 GiB Kafka RAM double-buffer).
- **#4 classpath bring-up → SOLVED.** M31's `ClassNotFoundException` was an UNQUOTED `-cp libs/*` (the shell expanded the glob so java saw only the first jar). The QUOTED java classpath wildcard **`-cp "$KP/libs/*"`** runs `kafka.Kafka` (`--version` → `3.5.0`) and a native single-broker KRaft comes up cleanly. Recipe (validated natively, loopback + a temp dir, scoped cleanup): storage-format = `java -cp "$KP/libs/*" kafka.tools.StorageTool random-uuid` then `… format -t <id> -c <kraft.properties>`; broker = `java … kafka.Kafka <kraft.properties>`; a minimal single-node KRaft `process.roles=broker,controller` config with `*.replication.factor=1`. Kafka pin: `/nix/store/prk07apdgqqjcqyxr5fqww1y8bxpw7z9-apache-kafka-2.13-3.5.0` (105 jars, 103 MiB). **NEW finding (a real requirement):** the guest must use the **HEADLESS JDK** (`openjdk-headless-17.0.7+7`), **NOT the M31 minimal JRE** — Kafka references `java.lang.management.ManagementFactory` at static-init and the minimal JRE OMITS the `java.management` module (`NoClassDefFoundError`). So a Kafka ro-root swaps the JRE closure for the (larger) headless-JDK closure.
- **#3 coordination determinism at Kafka scale under hvx → REMAINS the open, milestone-sized question** (KRaft Raft-election/heartbeat timers, request purgatory, quota + log flush/clean background threads). It needs a Kafka guest built (a `build-linux.sh` §2l: headless-JDK closure + kafka libs + KRaft config + a build-time storage-format on the ro-root/rw-disk) + a produce/consume client + a durability oracle + the four determinism checks (with a seeded disk fault on the log segments losing an acked message). **Encouraging prior:** Part 1 showed hvx makes even background compiler threads bit-deterministic by the same deterministic-scheduler mechanism that would govern Kafka's background threads — so a bit-identical Kafka is plausible, but proving it is the next rung's build, not this session's.

**Regressions — ALL preserved, re-verified this session (module byte-identical to M31; insmod/rmmod rc=0; dmesg clean; no L1 restarts [host up 2d+]; clean unload; `run-l1.sh` UNTOUCHED):**
- **`deploy-hvx.sh load`** — every M3–M27 hand-written test PASS + M28 scale (MILESTONE 28 PASS): iso `7e586473f6e494d7`, pci `3f95ef833747f3ec`, fault `91170d4dbf101c5b`/`88f680fc22cc9bb0`/`9090286f3fc59682`, net `0e2b7acf2f4f94ee`, net-fault `883a00eb6a14a6f0`/`37490fb78da33064`/`40659aabb208aaa0`, bug_f0 `1083e400458728ff`; cpuid M12 exact.
- **Boots UNCHANGED:** no-disk `640ece2f900fe5b3` (`hvx_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL), with-disk `36583d533d7c989b` (`hvx_disk_boot_verify`, whole-boot + branch IDENTICAL).
- **Byte-reproducibility of the rebuild:** modifying `build-linux.sh` §2k (JIT-selectable `/sbin/init`) + a full `build-linux.sh` run changed **ONLY** the JVM assets (`jvm-ext4.img`/`jvm-data.img`/`jvm-disk.img`); every other artifact (`initramfs*.cpio.gz`, `vblk-disk.img`, redis/sqlite/rootfs/system/kv images, `bzImage`) is byte-identical → the boot/regression baselines are structurally protected. (Observed latent issue, unrelated to this task: `jvm-data.img` is not byte-stable across wall-clock-separated builds — the same `libfaketime`-advance jitter M30 fixed for `system-disk.img`, not present-fixed for §2k; it does NOT affect same-disk determinism, which is what the verify tests.)

**Honest scope:** Parts 1+2 are the milestone and are fully verified. Part 3 advanced 3 of 4 Kafka blockers to RESOLVED and validated the native KRaft recipe end-to-end, but a fingerprint-proven deterministic Kafka (blocker #3 + the guest build) is deferred to Rung 6 — the JIT-determinism result makes it plausible, not proven.

### Milestone 33 — DONE (bare metal + real Kafka flagship + PMU branch clock; verified on the dedicated box, 2026-10-13)

**hvx now runs directly on L0 of a dedicated, throwaway bare-metal machine (no nesting), UNMODIFIED Apache Kafka 3.5.0 passes the four determinism checks, and the M17 PMU clock exists.** This removes the three hardware walls (nested exit tax, no PMU, hybrid CPU) and closes the M31/M32 Kafka wall.

**The bare-metal machine.** Xeon E3-1270 v6 (non-hybrid), 32 GB, Ubuntu 6.8, passwordless sudo, tree at `~/hydo`. hvx is `insmod`ed on the host itself there, so the "never load a module on the host" rule applies ONLY to the shared build machine. A wedge on the new bare-metal machine needs a reboot that only the user can do. Workflow: `rsync` of `hvx/` to `~/hydo/hvx/` on the bare-metal machine, then `make`, `sudo rmmod kvm_intel kvm`, `sudo insmod hvx.ko`, run tests by hand (`deploy-hvx.sh` is for the nested L1 only). Roughly 17× faster than nested. Every hand-written and boot fingerprint is bit-identical to the build machine, so determinism is host-independent.

**Five hvx bugs found and fixed on the way (all fingerprint-neutral; all were latent on the old box too):**
1. **Guest x87/SSE state was never saved.** Each return from `HVX_RUN` replaced the guest's live XMM/x87 registers with host leftovers. Now a per-vCPU 512-byte FXSAVE page (`fx_page`) is loaded before entry and saved at `out_clear`, and rides in `struct hvx_snapshot.fx`.
2. **Stale RIP on asynchronous exit paths — the "Kafka kernel-stack corruption".** `regs.rip/rsp/rflags` were refreshed only on the synchronous exit path, so a path that left the loop from an external-interrupt / interrupt-window exit re-entered the guest at an old kernel address with current registers (user registers inside `finish_task_switch`). They are now refreshed on EVERY exit.
3. **A guest spinning without VM exits froze the host for ~17 minutes** (`HVX_SAFETY_CAP` = 2^20 host ticks with preemption off: soft lockups, RCU stall, remote logins dead). After `HVX_PREEMPT_STREAK` (2000) consecutive non-retired exits the run loop now leaves VMX-root and `hvx_run_resched` re-enters after `cond_resched()`, carrying `guard`/`stepped` so no THROTTLE boundary moves; five exit-free re-entries return the new `HVX_EXIT_STALL`.
4. **Host NMIs were delivered into the guest** (NMI-exiting was off; the host lockup watchdog's NMI showed up as "Dazed and confused" in the guest). NMI-exiting is set and the NMI is handed to the host with `int $2`.
5. **An idle HLT with an interrupt already latched was returned to userspace**, stopping a healthy guest. It is now stepped over.

**Root cause of the Kafka hang.** The exit-count virtual TSC looks unstable to Linux's clocksource watchdog, which falls back to `refined-jiffies`. A thread that polls the clock (the JIT-compiled JVM) then makes no VM exits, so virtual time freezes and the guest livelocks; fix 2 above is what turned that hang into corruption. Two independent cures, both verified: the guest cmdline `tsc=reliable` (every clock read becomes an RDTSC exit), and the PMU branch clock below.

**PMU branch clock (`HVX_VM_PMU_CLOCK`, `struct hvx_config.flags` bit 2; closes M17).** GP counter 0 counts retired CONDITIONAL BRANCHES (event 0xC4/0x01, the event rr uses) in the guest only: the VMCS loads `IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL` = PMC0 on entry and 0 on exit, so no host branch is counted. Every `period` branches (default 2^20) the virtual TSC advances by `tick_tsc` (default 3,200,000 = 2 ms) and `icount` by one, at an EXACT instruction: the counter overflows `HVX_PMU_MARGIN` (256) branches early, the overflow interrupt (LVTPC re-pointed from NMI to the host's no-op vector 0xf2 for the run) forces an exit, and Monitor-Trap-Flag single-steps land on the target branch. The tick is processed at the loop TOP, before the LAPIC-deadline check, so a deadline it crosses injects on that same entry (processed after the check it injected at the next, host-timed exit — found as a real nondeterminism and fixed). `pmu_total`/`pmu_next` ride in the snapshot. `HVX_SET_PMU`/`HVX_GET_PMU` (ioctls 40/41, `struct hvx_pmu`) set the period and read statistics. `HVX_CREATE_VM` returns `ENODEV` when the host has no usable PMU (nested, hybrid). Off by default, so every prior fingerprint holds.

**Evidence for the clock** — `hvx/hvx_pmu_test.c` (SKIPs on a host without a PMU; added to `deploy-hvx.sh`): (A) a 200,000,000-branch loop reads exactly 200,000,000 in three runs; (B) with period 1,000,003 it takes exactly 199 ticks, zero overshoots, identical landing hash `86c4afcd0a799450`, `icount` and TSC in four runs although the number of landing steps differs; (C) stopping at tick 100 leaves the loop counter at exactly N − 100·period, and snapshot → finish equals restore → finish. On real Linux: the no-disk boot counts exactly 179,676,642 guest branches in every run with ticks disabled, and with ticks enabled two boots and a branch are identical (`32dc0d9f993358bf`; `HVX_PMU_CLOCK=1 ./hvx_boot_verify`).

**Kafka flagship (`hvx/hvx_kafka_verify.c`, guest built by `build-kafka.sh` / `build-linux.sh` §2l).** Unmodified single-broker Kafka 3.5.0 (KRaft) plus a producer/consumer over `127.0.0.1`, headless OpenJDK 17 with the `c1sync` JIT, 1024 MiB guest; ext4 read-only root (p1, excluded from the snapshot) and the log on **journaled ext4** (p2). The log disk was ext2 at first: under the power-cut fault an un-journaled filesystem is itself left inconsistent (`ext2_new_inode: inode number already in use`), so the durable config failed for a filesystem reason. `mode=all` runs in about three minutes and passes: SANITY OK; F1 `flush.messages=1` SURVIVES the volatile-cache power cut, replay identical; F2 default flush LOSES an acked message (`VIOLATION key=0`), replay and branch-from-snapshot identical; F3 two lying-disk seeds give different reproducible violations. Baselines, each reproduced in two full runs (SANITY / F1 / F2 / F3-A / F3-B):
- PMU clock, no `tsc=reliable`: `bed9661d57d5891b` / `9e6cba64455b85d8` / `c6060886f0e46dad` / `138c32c30895a50b` / `7bfa42daa560025b`
- PMU clock + `tsc=reliable`: `2820d094b0d45442` / `efef0a162f5045b2` / `252bae233562d6a6` / `17c136bca30ebc03` / `718c504e294c31d7`
- exit-count clock + `tsc=reliable` (`HVX_PMU_CLOCK=0`; the only mode that works nested): `e3ad76c716c836b0` / `a51c2e7ed1f38e11` / `71196fbd540a7675` / `a7d7668605cce416` / `c41dd22143e8c515`

The harness turns the PMU clock on by default and falls back on `ENODEV`. `HVX_CONSOLE_OUT=<file>` dumps the whole guest console of the last boot.

**SUPERSEDED NUMBERS: every Kafka fingerprint in this section, and the PMU test's `icount`, were replaced later the same day -- see Milestone 34 for the current baselines.**

**Honest scope.** These Kafka baselines are tied to the `kafka-disk.img` now on the new box: its p2 was rebuilt there with the distro `mke2fs` 1.47.0 and the mount line in the image's `/sbin/init` was patched in place (`ext2` → `ext4`). `build-linux.sh` §2l carries the same change, but the authoritative rebuild with the pinned nix `mke2fs` has NOT been run, so a rebuilt image may need re-baselining. The PMU clock is a tick source layered on the exit-count clock, not yet `HVX_TSC_ICOUNT` driven by real instructions; instruction-exact bisection of a Linux boot is still future work. Without `tsc=reliable` or the PMU clock, a heavy guest can still livelock. The five-fix module's Redis/SQLite/rootfs/system/JVM flagship baselines were not re-run on the new box (their images were not copied); the boot, virtio, fault, net and SMP fingerprints were.

### Milestone 34 — DONE (MySQL flagship + pinned Kafka image + seven more hvx fixes; verified on the bare-metal machine, 2026-10-13)

**UNMODIFIED MySQL 8.4.10 (InnoDB) passes the four determinism checks, the Kafka image is now the pinned reproducible build, and every older flagship was re-run on bare metal with the final module.** Getting MySQL through exposed seven more hvx defects; one of them (lost interrupts) changed several long-standing baselines, listed below.

**MySQL flagship (`build-mysql.sh`, `hvx/hvx_mysql_verify.c`).** Pinned nix `mysql-8.4.10` closure (778 MiB) on an ext4 read-only root (p1, 1280 MiB, excluded from the snapshot), data directory on journaled ext4 (p2, 256 MiB, the fault target), stock `mysql` client over TCP `127.0.0.1:3306`; the generic `initramfs-kafka.cpio.gz` does the `switch_root`. `/sbin/init` runs `mysqld --initialize-insecure` at boot (deterministic; nothing random is baked at build time), creates a schema and syncs it, then commits 30 single-row autocommit transactions. The knob under test is `innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit`: `hydo_flush=on` -> 1, `off` -> 2. Result of `mode=all` (about 3 minutes): SANITY OK; F1 (=1) SURVIVES the volatile-cache power cut; F2 (=2) LOSES acked rows (`VIOLATION key=3`), replay + branch-from-snapshot identical; F3 two lying-disk seeds give different reproducible violations. "MILESTONE 34 PASS", twice per configuration with identical fingerprints (SANITY / F1 / F2 / F3-A / F3-B):
- PMU clock (default): `4b53d4de51608212` / `ca368dd3ac89bbfe` / `f720da0ec84a1d26` / `c3d35d58e10f81da` / `b584b848d38764ca`
- exit clock + `tsc=reliable` (`HVX_PMU_CLOCK=0`): `cd0794d21e593721` / `15d4e014962b2f13` / `1e544ac9c96de589` / `25f8ca9c99ecb7b2` / `0898e9b8242895c3`
- image: `mysql-disk.img` sha256 `fcad31ab…`. The harness sets `HVX_VM_PAUSE_EXIT` (below); MySQL does not complete without it.

**Seven hvx fixes (all in `hvx/hvx.c`):**
1. **Lost interrupts — a single pending slot (the important one).** hvx kept ONE pending one-shot interrupt (`inject_vector`). When the guest sent itself an IPI while a timer tick was still waiting for interrupts to be re-enabled, the tick was overwritten. In periodic-timer mode (jiffies clocksource) Linux never re-arms after a lost tick, so the guest's clock stopped and MySQL spun forever. There is now an overflow pending set (`vcpu->irr`, `hvx_oneshot_post`/`hvx_oneshot_refill`, in `struct hvx_snapshot.irr`). **This changes guest behaviour wherever a collision used to happen, so these baselines MOVED:** no-disk boot `640ece2f900fe5b3` -> **`7a5559028ffbf52c`**; rootfs `604b9c28083cd0e3` -> **`77909a097a8eee08`** (seeds `fec1ed3459dafb40` / `ea2ce9d0b566b0d4`); Redis and the two-service system (all values, below). UNCHANGED: with-disk boot `36583d533d7c989b`, hydokv `62ae248b01bf3f70` / `7ade5f37d16a347c` / `832cc63372b1458d`, SQLite `1c072036545a7e87` / `7a4ab31fddabe4cc` / `dc04dbe3b49624df`, and every hand-written test (iso, pci, fault, net, net-fault, smp, bug_f0).
2. **Host freeze, second form.** The morning's bound only caught a guest with NO retired exits. A guest that keeps retiring a few exits while each PMU tick costs millions of single-steps held the CPU for 15 minutes. The run loop now leaves VMX-root whenever the host scheduler wants the CPU (`need_resched()`) at any non-retired exit, including tick-landing steps, and `HVX_EXIT_STALL` is time-based (`HVX_STALL_SECS` = 10 s without a retired exit, a retired branch, or RCX moving).
3. **Unusable segments were recorded with history-dependent leftovers.** A null DS/ES/FS/GS has no defined limit/attribute bits; the CPU leaves `0x000fffff`/0 after the guest's own null load and `0xffffffff`/G|DB after a VMCS rebuild. With re-entries now at host-timed points this flipped the fingerprint of otherwise byte-identical runs (9 of 40 boots). `seg_canon()` records one fixed form.
4. **Guest CR2 is now carried across entries** (`vcpu->cr2`); it is not a VMCS field and the host overwrites it.
5. **Interrupted event deliveries are re-injected** (IDT-vectoring information). Never observed to trigger (`reinjected=0` in the exit histogram), kept because it is the correct handling.
6. **PMU clock: REP string instructions.** One landing step per REP iteration with no branch retired; after 32 branch-free steps the landing now "hops" (runs free until the next branch) while at least 128 branches remain. A tick also advances `icount` by 2048 (was 1: a disk completion, due 16 units after its kick, took 16 ticks when the rest of the guest was spinning) and weighs 256 on the THROTTLE cadence.
7. **Volatile write cache served stale data.** When the cache was full (256 entries) a write fell back to "persist now" without invalidating older held bytes for the same range, so a later read overlaid the stale bytes and a later FLUSH committed them over the newer data.

**`HVX_VM_PAUSE_EXIT` (flags bit 3, opt-in).** Every guest `PAUSE` becomes a VM exit counted on the instruction clock, so spin-waits (kernel `cpu_relax`, InnoDB `ut_delay`, JVM `SpinPause`) move virtual time instead of freezing it. Off by default because it changes the exit stream of any guest that spins.

**Kafka on the PINNED image.** `build-kafka.sh` on the build machine produces `kafka-disk.img` sha256 `b7cb107e…` in three consecutive builds (one earlier build, the first after the recipe change, differed; cause not found). Final baselines, PMU clock, no `tsc=reliable` (the default): SANITY `9a1eb88261205ad4`, F1 `aa08e2001218def4`, F2 `2139e0b304db4440`, F3 `1bccf554f9bcc563` / `100a581d385313c6` — "MILESTONE 33 PASS". **In the two `tsc=reliable` configurations Kafka `mode=all` currently FAILS on expectations, not on determinism** (every replay is identical): with the PMU clock, F3 seed B corrupts the ext4 filesystem itself (`mount ... Data consistency error`), which the harness does not count as a violation; with the exit clock, F2 (default flush) happens to lose nothing, and the branch's durable outcome differs from the straight run (not investigated).

**Older flagships on bare metal with the final module — all PASS:** Redis SANITY `634c64b7c583b6df`, F1 `40e98d280d28a492`, F2 `789580e15b179115` (branch `47e08c006268d187`, durable `9655b06b18eb15d3`), F3 `2ec726ec7c0e50a7` / `c95cb2090b8db26c`. System SANITY `e95beaac20a0f91e`, F1 `bd0308f054ca0122`, F2 `fc7d9925ff0997db` (branch `8820c50a6b0b91f5`, durable `044d0fe3eb19175d`), F3 `aed9728c9633817f` / `5c8ec447620131cd`. JVM (`c1sync`, image rebuilt since M32, ro-root `6252ea45f0999a19`) `025b276a0215a7a6`. Each runs in under a minute.

**Honest scope / open items.** (a) One MySQL crash-test run out of 33 produced a different durable-disk hash; it was the first run after a module reload and did not recur in 24 more runs or in the four full verifies. Not explained. (b) The Kafka `tsc=reliable` expectation failures above. (c) With the PMU clock on, host performance counters are off while a guest runs. (d) The no-disk boot under the PMU clock (`32dc0d9f993358bf` before fix 1) was not re-measured after it. (e) `deploy-hvx.sh` and the `run-*-demo.sh` scripts still target the nested build machine setup; on the new box things are run by hand. (f) `HVX_PROGRESS=1` in the Kafka/MySQL harnesses prints live clock statistics and rewrites `HVX_CONSOLE_OUT` every four returns.

### Milestone 35 — DONE (Rung 6: four real services behind gRPC, one cross-service oracle; Kafka verify green everywhere; bare-metal regression script; 2026-10-14)

Done as three parallel workstreams on the bare-metal machine. Module reloads there are serialized by a lock: VM runs go through `~/bin/hvx-run` (shared flock on `~/.hvx-module.lock`), reloads through `~/bin/hvx-reload` (exclusive). **No module change in this milestone** — `hvx.ko` is the Milestone 34 build.

**The stack flagship (`build-stack.sh`, `hvx/stack/{main.go,go.mod,go.sum}`, `hvx/hvx_stack_verify.c`).** One guest runs UNMODIFIED MySQL 8.4.10, Kafka 3.5.0 (KRaft, `c1sync` JIT) and Redis 7.2.4, plus `hydostack`: a static Go binary (grpc v1.68.1, pinned by go.sum, no protoc — hand-written service over the standard wrapper types) with three roles. `server`: each gRPC `Place(id)` does a MySQL autocommit INSERT, a Kafka produce (acks=all) to topic `orders`, and a Redis SET, and only then ACKs. `place 30`: the client. `oracle 30`: after recovery every acked order must be in MySQL with the right amount and in the Kafka topic; Redis (a cache) may miss a key but must never hold a wrong value. Read-only ext4 root 1792 MiB; one 512 MiB journaled-ext4 data disk holds MySQL's datadir, Kafka's logs and Redis's AOF; guest RAM 1024 MiB; flags `X86_64_V2 | IDLE_HEARTBEAT | PAUSE_EXIT`, PMU clock. Provisioning (MySQL init, Kafka format, schema, topic) is synced before the orders so only the orders sit in the power-cut window. `hydo_flush=on` = every store durable before the ACK; `off` relaxes only MySQL (`innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit=2`). Images byte-identical across two builds: `stack-disk.img` `9c284fd1…`. `mode=all` (about 5 minutes) — "MILESTONE 35 PASS", reproduced three times (twice by the workstream, once independently):
- SANITY `f1459d3309447a4c`: 30/30 orders in MySQL, Kafka and Redis.
- F1 all durable + power cut: all 30 survive in every store, replay identical, `0d607062a553f198`.
- F2 MySQL relaxed + power cut: **a cross-service disagreement** — `VIOLATION mysql order=13 expected=100013 got=MISSING (kafka has it: true)`; replay and branch identical `c31c27d912988e0a` (durable `ed613bde9e04d468`).
- F3 lying-disk seeds: A `90bfcd1bfc1fe739` (Redis cannot recover), B `c07d58644c218cf9` (data disk unmountable).

**Kafka `mode=all` now passes in all three clock configurations (`build-linux.sh` §2l + `hvx/hvx_kafka_verify.c`).** The M34 "branch mismatch" was a harness bug, not a determinism bug: the branch check also required the F2 verdict to be a violation; every fingerprint had matched. F2 sometimes lost nothing because the kernel's background writeback and the ext4 journal commit flushed data before the cut, at a time that depends on the clock configuration; the guest now mounts the data disk with `commit=86400` and disables background writeback (`dirty_writeback_centisecs=0`), so only Kafka's own fsync makes data durable. A real Kafka finding along the way: `kafka-storage format` writes `bootstrap.checkpoint` (tmp + rename) WITHOUT fsync, so a power cut after formatting leaves a broker that can never start; the guest syncs once after formatting. A failed data-disk mount in the oracle phase now reports `HYDO-ORACLE: VIOLATION data disk unrecoverable`. The Kafka image flipped between two outputs across builds (one-second faked-clock slip in inode crtime/extra times and backup-superblock times); a Kafka-only time-zeroing pass fixes it: four builds give `kafka-disk.img` `ba27661b84dd1e8c…` (also shipped). Baselines, each twice (SANITY / F1 / F2 / F3-A / F3-B):
- PMU clock (default): `baae8a071a878505` / `ab40506e09bd7d9b` / `19a591dfde5f04cb` / `d9211628c9dc354f` / `d005d3e6403f454a` (re-verified independently)
- PMU + `tsc=reliable`: `d89824dcbb623835` / `c1d4deaa36374287` / `1fbb1c880b952131` / `27ca1bf8e1e2738b` / `108b91fb03774eb0`
- exit clock + `tsc=reliable`: `f45e1f33ed65a642` / `0f517bdd21a2573e` / `419c5713670c4b4e` / `9e5ca684755c43e5` / `722eb3b4abdfe69e`

**`hvx/deploy-metal.sh` — the regression runner for the bare-metal machine (run from the Mac).** Syncs the userspace sources (never `hvx.c`/`hvx_abi.h` unless `load`), compiles on the bare-metal machine against the bare-metal machine's `hvx_abi.h`, runs the 22 hand-written tests + both boot verifies through `hvx-run`, and checks each against the current baseline: "deploy-metal: ALL PASS (24 checks)" (verified). `load` also rebuilds and reloads the module; `flagships` also runs every real-service verify. Neither option has been run yet.

**MySQL flake watch:** 32 more `f1` runs (64 crash-recover cycles) were all identical (`9dd038fce2729719`, `ca368dd3ac89bbfe`). The single divergent run from M34 (the first run after a module reload) did not recur; the reload case is untested.

**Open:** (a) the shared `mkext4_rootfs_reproducible` helper zeroes only `i_ctime`, not `i_crtime`/extra times or backup-superblock times, so the MySQL root image (`build-mysql.sh`) can differ when a build crosses a wall-clock second — fixed only for Kafka (§2l) and the stack (`build-stack.sh`); fixing it for MySQL means rebuilding its image and re-baselining. (b) Guest RAM above 1 GiB is avoided because of the known EPT fan-out bug (not fixed). (c) Of the two candidate causes of the Kafka image flip, only the clock slip was shown; the directory-order change was applied as well, unproven.

### Milestone 36 — DONE (service crashes + network faults on the stack; RAM up to 3 GiB verified; MySQL image reproducible; 2026-10-14)

Three parallel workstreams again (module lock: `~/bin/hvx-run` / `~/bin/hvx-reload`). **No functional module change**: `hvx/hvx.c` gained one comment. Every headline result below was re-run independently.

**Stack faults (`build-stack.sh`, `hvx/stack/main.go`, `hvx/hvx_stack_verify.c` new `fault` mode, driven by `HVX_STACK_FAULT`).** The mode runs a scenario twice plus a branch from a snapshot taken after provisioning, before any fault; all must be bit-identical. (1) Single-service crash, `hydo_kill=<mysql|kafka|redis>@K`: the guest places K orders, SIGKILLs the service, places 3 more while it is down, restarts it, places the rest — no host timing involved. (2) Network faults, `hydo_net=<svc>:<netem args>`: `tc netem` on `lo` filtered to that service's port (iproute2 6.8.0 + `sch_prio`/`sch_netem`/`cls_u32` on the ro-root); netem's randomness and timers are the guest kernel's, which hvx makes deterministic. Invariant: every ACKed order is in MySQL (right amount) and Kafka; Redis may miss a key but never hold a wrong value; non-ACKed orders carry no guarantee (counted, not violations). All scenarios REPRODUCED (two runs + branch identical): kill kafka@10 `f56e33d5d6b69556` (re-verified), kill kafka@20 `1fe38e1b4edacb4b`, kill mysql@10 `b49f71cac010355f`, kill redis@10 `c2a2190c94ab43f6`, net mysql delay+40% loss `aec19e0008d67c96`, net redis duplicate+reorder `836751f21bfe18a2`, net kafka 1s+60% loss all-durable `9ab19f03e7ac1ce1` (0/30 ACKed), same with MySQL relaxed `8813456c6ef76cb1` (25/30). **Findings:** the front door returns errors for orders that are in fact fully applied (a retrying client would double-place); the non-idempotent Kafka producer writes duplicate records under packet loss; the same network fault ends differently depending on the durability setting. New base baselines (front-door binary + root changed; image `stack-disk.img` `4c12b3e2…`, two builds identical), 1024 MiB: SANITY `66169b0e7ad1ce4d`, F1 `0acb21280b5f55e6`, F2 `9a1187e128446896` (`VIOLATION mysql order=15 … kafka has it: true`, durable `0d1be4abd1a9ac02`), F3 `83def5a7304a8609` / `29c69e6ef2f9a04f`.

**Guest RAM above 1 GiB — works; the old "corruption" did not reproduce.** `hvx/hvx_bigram_test.c` (new) tags every page of a 3 GiB guest (784,388 pages) and checks them from guest and host: zero mismatches (re-verified). Real-Linux boots two-boots-plus-branch identical at 1536 (`b65c6e7bddfc670a`), 2048 (`fbd8b58eb9c569c7`) and 3072 MiB (`2eaa86e3247e730f`); a 3 GiB guest round-trips a 1.4 GB tmpfs file; MySQL sanity passes at 1536 and 2048 MiB (re-verified, `24ed0525d0872d23` at 2048). The stack passes `mode=all` at 2048 MiB twice: SANITY `4d7122148a79d207`, F1 `85816eb25e5ae3b7`, F2 `e1c9184f7513bd87`, F3 `c94b2b2f127f8844` / `6c2cbae4a03f1c49`. Most likely the earlier corruption was one of the M33/M34 bugs (lost FPU state, stale RIP, lost interrupts); not proven which. **Latent, deliberately left:** in the module-built default guest page tables, the page mapping guest-virtual 512 MiB–1 GiB shares a page with the built-in ISR, which overwrites its upper half; only hand-written test guests use those tables (real Linux builds its own), and fixing it would move every hand-written fingerprint — documented in a comment at `build_guest_page_tables`.

**MySQL image reproducibility (`build-mysql.sh`).** The shared helper zeroed `i_ctime` only; `mke2fs -d` also stamps `i_crtime` from the advancing faked clock (a byte diff showed all 1736 differing bytes were `i_crtime`). A new `zero_ext_times` pass zeroes superblock times in the primary and all backup superblocks and every inode's ctime/dtime/extra/crtime (plus atime/mtime on the data disk). Three builds in three different wall-clock seconds are byte-identical: `mysql-disk.img` `404bf87d10ec5422…`; e2fsck clean. New MySQL baselines (two runs each, re-verified): PMU clock SANITY `e73f52739a634165`, F1 `8be5987266ccb7aa`, F2 `c2ef1c3a1bfd1b86` (`VIOLATION key=3`), F3 `5b5905ac30da7da9` / `8749bd93d306cb05`; exit clock + `tsc=reliable` `2bfdb7a4ab96731e` / `057ddf3507e02db7` / `a87daf6aee32d56a` / `3e6dda69a9814405` / `17a5fa3bba6388bc`. These SUPERSEDE the Milestone 34 MySQL values.

**MySQL reload flake — closed.** Ten cycles of reload-then-run (holding the exclusive lock across both, so each run really was the first after a reload) were all identical (durable `3afdb6f3362627ae`, `8be5987266ccb7aa`). Combined with the M35 watch: one divergent workload boot in about 165, never reproduced.

**Regressions:** `hvx/deploy-metal.sh` ALL PASS (24 checks) after the module reload; boot baselines unchanged (`7a5559028ffbf52c`, `36583d533d7c989b`).

**Open:** the front door's non-atomic ACK and the non-idempotent producer are findings about the test application, not hvx — fixing them (idempotent producer, outbox pattern) would make a good "the oracle now passes" follow-up. `hvx_bigram_test` is not yet in `deploy-metal.sh`.

### Milestone 37 — DONE (the stack's front door made retry-safe and exactly-once; faults now go clean; 2026-10-14)

Fixes the two M36 findings in the test application (no hvx change). **Kafka producer is idempotent:** switched from segmentio/kafka-go (cannot do idempotent writes) to franz-go v1.17.1 (idempotent by default: producer id + sequence numbers, acks=all), so a batch re-sent after a lost response is discarded by the broker; segmentio stays for topic creation and the oracle's reads. **`Place(id)` is retry-safe:** MySQL upsert keyed by id plus a `published` flag; Kafka produce skipped if already published, and after an earlier produce of unknown outcome the server scans the topic for the key first; Redis SET; ACK only after all three. The client's new `retry <n> <ackedfile>` mode re-places every un-ACKed order with the same id (≤ 8 attempts); the guest runs it after a crashed service restarts and, for network faults, once under the fault and again after the fault is removed. A transactional outbox was considered and not used (needs a relay + outbox recovery; same exactly-once result here with less machinery). **Oracle tightened:** every ACKed order exactly once in MySQL (right amount, published) and in Kafka, never wrong in Redis; an un-ACKed order found in Kafka is now a VIOLATION; a Kafka duplicate is a VIOLATION. Files: `hvx/stack/{main.go,go.mod,go.sum}`, `build-stack.sh`. Image `stack-disk.img` `b3f1f81a…` (two builds identical).

**Every fault scenario now ends 30/30 exactly-once** (each two runs + branch identical): kill kafka@10 `9d081bcd4c55ec42` (re-verified independently), kill kafka@20 `324dc7b9c803f36f`, kill mysql@10 `c0e543530f3f54ad`, kill redis@10 `97f5cf840b725154`, net mysql delay+40% loss `852ac1d6fdb1a149`, net redis duplicate+reorder `1f0479b4fbd0de3a`, net kafka 1s+60% loss all-durable `da11eaa0068be123` (27/30 first pass, 30/30 after heal), same with MySQL relaxed `1f54211a422ee468`. New base baselines (`mode=all`, two runs, "MILESTONE 35 PASS"): SANITY `0c795949eb321cd7`, F1 `30b74bcb99d24489`, F2 `380d8ec0a7979ad9` (VIOLATION — MySQL's documented `trx_commit=2` window, expected; durable `3cc7be73cb485f1c`), F3 `048b7e0ad3dab0f2` / `286dbeb0f74507a4`.

**Remaining (a liveness limit, not a safety bug):** while the Kafka link stays degraded past the client's retry budget, an order can sit in Kafka with the client not yet ACKed — reproduced as `VIOLATION order=21 is in kafka but the client was never ACKed` when the heal step is skipped; a same-id retry after the fault clears ACKs it without a duplicate.

**Regression script:** `hvx/deploy-metal.sh` now includes `hvx_bigram_test` (3 GiB, every page); "ALL PASS (25 checks)".

### Milestone 38 — DONE (automatic fault explorer + always/sometimes assertions; first bug found without a hand-picked scenario; 2026-10-14)

The project is now a git repository (branch `main`; guest images under `build/` are not tracked — the build scripts reproduce them). Two parallel workstreams; no module change.

**Assertions (`hvx/ASSERTIONS.md`, `hvx/stack/hydoassert/{hydoassert.go,hydo_assert.sh}`, `hvx/hydo_assert_table.h`, `hvx/hydo_assert_report.c`).** Property kinds: `always`, `always_or_unreachable`, `sometimes`, `reachable`, `unreachable`, numeric comparisons via details. Wire format on the guest console: `HYDO-ASSERT-DECL {"t","id","msg"}` (catalog, at start, so never-evaluated properties are reported), `HYDO-ASSERT {"t","id","ok","msg","details"}`, and a lifecycle line `HYDO-SETUP-COMPLETE` (no guest emits it yet). Verdicts: an `always` that fails or is never evaluated FAILS; an `unreachable` that is hit FAILS; a `sometimes`/`reachable` never satisfied is NOT EXERCISED. The Go SDK writes straight to `/dev/ttyS0`, deduplicates (each assertion/outcome once per process), deterministic (declaration order, sorted keys, no clocks). The stack front door, client and oracle now state 17 properties (7 correctness, 10 coverage); `HYDO-ORACLE` is still printed (first violation in id order). `hydo_assert_report <consoles…>` prints the property table and exits 1 on a violation. Verified independently on F2: only `order.in_mysql` fails (order 9 missing from MySQL while Kafka has it).

**Explorer (`hvx/hvx_explore.c`, `hvx/explore/{explore.py,stack-explore.sh,stack-src*/}`, hooks in `build-stack.sh` for `STACK_OUT=explore*`).** A schedule is a pure function of a 64-bit seed (splitmix64): service kill (which, before which order, for how long), netem on one service's port for a window of orders, a power cut before order C, durability knobs (MySQL `trx_commit`, Redis `appendfsync`), and the hvx disk-fault policy (`ioerr:P`, `lying:P`, power-loss cache). The guest has a serve mode: it stops at S0 (root mounted) and S1 (provisioned + synced) and takes one command on the serial console. The host boots once, snapshots S0 and S1 (file-backed), starts each schedule in a fresh VM restored from S1, and after a cut carries only the flushed data disk into a recovery VM restored from S0. `explore.py` runs seeds on a worker pool, re-runs every failure (fingerprints must match), shrinks one failure per signature (drop elements, halve parameters), classifies findings (durable-defaults / unsafe-knob / lying-disk / io-errors), reads `HYDO-ASSERT` records, and writes `report.md`, `runs.jsonl`, `shrink.jsonl`. Throughput: fresh boots ~120 schedules/hour (6 concurrent); snapshot branching ~530/hour with 3 workers (4 physical cores; more workers is slower) — about 4×, with identical fingerprints (cross-checked on three failures). Run: on the bare-metal machine, `cd ~/hydo/hvx/explore && python3 explore.py --seeds 1-200 --out /var/tmp/explore/run1`; reproduce by hand with `~/bin/hvx-run sudo ./hvx_explore one <bzImage> <initramfs-kafka> ~/hydo/build/explore-disk.img <outdir> "<schedule>"`. Image `explore-disk.img` `f73f1a95…` (two builds identical; the default `stack` image stays `b3f1f81a…`).

**Results — 272 schedules, 92 failures, all reproduced bit-for-bit, every minimal schedule re-run identically:**
- **A real cross-service bug with every store durable and an honest disk (4 failures, one signature):** minimal `disk=none dseed=4d5cb825dfaab05b | run K=redis@11+1 C=24` (Redis killed before order 11, power cut before order 24). The front door publishes to Kafka, then fails the order because Redis — only a cache — is down; the cut lands before the client's retry, so Kafka keeps an order the client was told failed (`order.applied_but_not_acked`). A Kafka kill gives the same (the producer keeps retrying past the client's deadline). Fingerprint `2403499c9a3f8419`, identical from a snapshot and from a fresh boot (re-verified independently). Likely fix in the front door: treat the Redis write as best-effort or do it before Kafka, and bound producer retries by the request deadline. **This was found without anyone choosing the scenario.**
- **Unsafe settings (9):** MySQL `trx_commit=2` + cut loses the published flag (`c8ad6f24…`); Redis `appendfsync=everysec` + cut on an honest disk makes Redis **refuse to start**, worse than its documented ≤1 s loss (`3e4ec370…`).
- **Lying disk (26):** lost Kafka orders (`e5e266f7…`), MySQL or Redis unrecoverable, filesystem unmountable. **I/O errors (25):** ext4 goes read-only and stores fail to restart (noisy: errors also hit program reads).
- On the assertion image (`explore-sdk-disk.img`, 36 seeds) the same durable finding shows up as the `unreachable` property `order.applied_but_not_acked` being hit, and all 7 coverage properties were exercised.

**Open:** fix the front-door bug and re-explore; `hydo_assert_report` counts recovery consoles as separate runs; no guest emits `HYDO-SETUP-COMPLETE`; the report's hand-reproduction line always names `explore-disk.img`; a shrunk schedule can change finding class but keeps its original label; the stack's own baselines will move on the next default build (the front door now carries the assertions).

### Milestone 39 — DONE (the explorer's bug fixed and the fix proven by the explorer; 2026-10-15)

**Front-door fix (`hvx/stack/main.go`, commit `0112b48`).** Redis is best effort: a failed cache write (capped at 2 s, recorded as `sometimes server.cache_write_skipped`) no longer fails an order already durable in MySQL and published to Kafka — the exact bug. Every store call runs under the client's request deadline, and the Kafka producer stops retrying a record after 10 s. A failed `Place` now says which kind: "not applied" (only while no Kafka write was ever started for that id) or "outcome unknown"; the client records acked / failed / unknown. No outbox (the M37 idempotent retries already give exactly-once).

**The honest window and the new invariant.** An order can reach Kafka while the client never gets the ACK (timeout, lost reply, power cut in flight); no request/response protocol closes that, so "no un-ACKed order in Kafka" is not achievable. It is replaced by `unreachable order.told_failed_but_applied` (an order the client was last told DEFINITELY failed must not be in Kafka; failed ids are carried across a power cut on a `hydo-explore-failed:` console line) and a counted, non-violating `order.unknown_outcome_applied`.

**Proof by the explorer.** Same seeds 1–272, old image → fixed image (`explore-fix-disk.img` `df1ec991…`, four builds identical): durable defaults 147/**4** → 151/**0** (ok/fail); unsafe settings 29/12 → 29/12; lying disk 3/34 → 3/34; I/O errors 1/42 → 2/41. 328 new seeds (273–600): durable defaults 187/0; all 99 failures in the expected classes, all reproduced; minimal examples `disk=lying:1 dseed=ea0cf4c47365be7a | run C=5` (lost Kafka order), `disk=none dseed=144d103cda4aa2f8 | run C=27 M=0` (MySQL order lost, Kafka has it), `disk=none dseed=c25c8ed1a6d51f0e | run C=18 R=everysec` (Redis unrecoverable). The old minimal reproducer on the fixed image: `HYDO-ORACLE: OK acked=24/30 exactly-once`, `f87130453609a5d6` (re-verified independently); on the pinned old image it still gives `2403499c9a3f8419`. ~550–630 schedules/hour. One I/O-error run (seed 1) re-ran with the same verdict and byte-identical console but a different end-state fingerprint; seven further runs all gave the original — consistent with the known restore→halt few-bytes timekeeping gap. Explorer report fixes: runs = schedules (a cut's recovery boot no longer counts), the hand-reproduction line names the image used, shrunk reproducers carry their own class (original noted). `HYDO-SETUP-COMPLETE` is now emitted, but before the S1 snapshot point, so per-run consoles do not show it. The stack root image had the same superblock-time leak as MySQL's; fixed in `build-stack.sh`.

**New default stack baselines** (`stack-disk.img` `fbe9b574…`, two builds identical, 1024 MiB): `mode=all` twice — SANITY `1423ee3d57ba76f1`, F1 `39054edb4022cc32`, F2 `ef46cb84c1940e8a` (VIOLATION mysql order=8, kafka has it — MySQL's documented `trx_commit=2` window, expected; durable `8dda18cac19897bb`), F3 `92005b2c643e8d1e` / `02ba1d91a5559d89`. Fault scenarios, each two runs + branch identical, all 30/30 exactly-once, told-failed 0: kill mysql@10 `e90e824a4cf4bb5f`, kill kafka@10 `a8a166419a332f45`, kill kafka@20 `788c008040b131f4`, kill redis@10 `44b97b465c648c84`, net mysql `394b58497942a9f5`, net redis `9dfde060b3d88a2c`, net kafka all-durable `d8e1bc6e234e3be3`, net kafka MySQL relaxed `35b4bd77e0a8dfb4`. These SUPERSEDE the M35–M37 stack values.

### Milestone 40 — DONE (exact guest code coverage with Intel Processor Trace; 2026-10-15)

The bare-metal Kaby Lake supports PT inside VMX guests (`IA32_VMX_MISC` bit 14 = 1; ToPA, 2 IP-filter ranges, CR3 filter). **hvx (`hvx/hvx.c`, `hvx/hvx_abi.h`, commit `66bbd96`):** opt-in flag `HVX_VM_PT_TRACE` (bit 4) and new ioctls `HVX_SET_PT` (buffer size, kernel/user, two IP ranges, CR3 filter, no return compression) / `HVX_GET_PT` / `HVX_READ_PT`; no existing struct changed size. Module parameters `pt_force*` trace an unmodified harness; the last freed VM's trace is kept (`hvx_ptrun --last`). Host-owned ToPA in 2 MiB regions with STOP at the end (a full buffer stops and is reported). Uses the three "conceal VMX from PT" controls; this CPU lacks the entry "load RTIT_CTL" / exit "clear RTIT_CTL" controls, so a second trampoline `hvx_vmx_run_pt` sets TraceEn just before VMLAUNCH/VMRESUME and clears it first thing after the exit (the few host instructions in between are outside every filter range). Host RTIT state is saved and restored. The device name is a compile-time define (`HVX_DEVNAME`), so `hvx/pt/build-hvxpt.sh` builds a side-by-side copy `hvxpt.ko` on `/dev/hvxpt` for development (two modules coexist because each run's VMXON…VMXOFF is non-preemptible). **Merged into the main `hvx.ko` and reloaded; `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS (25 checks).** Tracing on vs off gives identical fingerprints (no-disk `7a5559028ffbf52c`, with-disk `36583d533d7c989b`).

**Tools (`hvx/pt/`, commit `8a7b30a`; libipt 2.0.6 from apt on the bare-metal machine):** `hvx_ptrun.c` boots a traced guest to a console marker (`HVX_PT_START` starts tracing at a later marker); `ptcov.c` decodes per instruction (not per block — exits cut blocks at host-timed points; return compression must be off); `ptcov_report.py` gives per-function counts, source lines (`go tool addr2line` for Go) and A/B diffs; `build-ptstack.sh` builds the front door with symbols, linked at `0x2000001000` so a range filter isolates it, into `pt-stack-disk.img`.

**Results.** Kernel, no-disk boot from "Trying to unpack rootfs" to the shell: 328 M instructions, 127,260 unique instructions in 2,811 functions; two traced runs identical at function level (3 instructions differ — a decoder attribution quirk at the SYSRET epilogue). Overhead: traced boot ≈ 1.03 s vs 0.84 s; traces 120–330 MB per boot; decode ≈ 23 s per boot. **The payoff — front door, clean vs kill-kafka@10:** 166,611 vs 174,264 unique instructions; 7,912 only under the fault; 98 functions only under the fault, incl. `main.retry`, `main.kafkaHasKey`, `main.outcomeUnknown`, `main.outcome`, franz-go retry/back-off and gRPC retry paths; 101 lines of `main.go` run only under the fault (saved diff `/var/tmp/pts-diff.txt` on the bare-metal machine, checked).

**Limits:** guest kernel MODULES are not traced (they share the host's module address range); very early boot needs tracing started after the kernel's runtime self-patching; Kafka's JIT Java is out of scope (needs a CR3/range filter + JVM perf maps); MySQL/Redis need debug symbols for line level; only the last trace is kept; buffers of ~1 GiB can fail to allocate (512 MiB works); host PT use is suspended during traced guest runs. Next natural step: coverage-guided exploration (prefer seeds/snapshots that reach new code).

### Milestone 41 — DONE, NEGATIVE RESULT (coverage-guided exploration; 2026-10-16)

**Built (commits `2f0fb3a`, `fb5499e`):** per-run coverage feedback — the workload VM is traced with Intel PT filtered to the front door's own text (a symbol-enabled `hydostack` linked at `0x2000001000` via `STACK_GO_LDFLAGS`), decoded with libipt into control-flow edges with hit counts (`hvx/explore/ptedges.h`, wired into `hvx_explore.c`); the decoder keeps the edge chain across VM-exit trace on/off transitions, otherwise counts jitter. Three traces of one schedule give byte-identical edge files; fingerprints identical with tracing on/off. Cost: ~16 MB trace and ~4 s decode per run; throughput with coverage 25–60 runs/hour. `hvx/explore/guided.py`: AFL-style loop (corpus seeded from random schedules; keep a schedule iff it hits a new edge bucket; mutate the least-used entry with kill/netem/cut/disk/durability operators kept separable); `--mode random` for the same budget. `hvx/explore/evaluate.sh` runs the comparison.

**Result: guidance did not beat random on this system.** Runs until the old durable-defaults bug (M37 front door): random 1 and 13 (third repetition unfinished); guided 14, and not within 30. Unique edges by run 15: random 131k / 145k; guided 130k (another guided run reached only 119k by run 30). Reasons: the front door's edge set saturates within a few runs and is mostly fault-independent start-up code, so "new edge" rarely fires; and the bug is about timing/ordering, not reachability — its code is covered immediately. A useful signal would need to be richer (whole-system or per-service coverage, state/assertion coverage). Not finished: coverage growth on the fixed front door. Default stack baseline unchanged (`1423ee3d57ba76f1`, re-verified).

**Also in the tree (commit `bbe170b`):** seeded PMU-tick jitter and a HLT fix — see Milestone 43. (An earlier version of this note said it was not loaded or verified; it was loaded with `deploy-metal.sh load` the same day, ALL PASS 25 checks.)

### Milestone 42 — DONE (swarm testing + exhaustive sweeps; 2026-10-16)

**Built (commits `bca45b7`, `936781e`):** `explore.py --strategy swarm` switches whole fault categories (kill, network, cut, disk, durability knob) on or off per run from the seed, keeping the schedule format (reproducible by hand, shrinkable). `explore.py --sweep kill|cut|net|killcut` enumerates small spaces exhaustively (durable settings, honest disk; point labels in `sweep-points.jsonl`). `compare.py sweep <dirs>` / `compare.py strategies /var/tmp/m42 old|fix` print the tables. Results under `/var/tmp/m42` on the bare-metal machine.

**Sweeps.** Fixed front door (`explore-fix-disk.img`): each service killed before every order (90 points), power cut before every order (30), netem on each service at 5 severities (15), kill then a cut 1–13 orders later (81) — **0 failures in all**, so the M39 fix holds exhaustively in these spaces. Old front door (`explore-disk.img`), kill-then-cut: **44 of 81 fail**, all reproduced bit-for-bit — Redis killed then any later cut always fails; Kafka killed at order ≥ 5 fails when the cut is ≥ 2 orders later; Kafka killed at order 0 and MySQL killed at any point never fail (consistent with the mechanism: nothing is published before MySQL succeeds).

**Random vs swarm, 3 repetitions × 60 runs** (run of first failure; "-" = not found; table re-generated independently on the bare-metal machine):
- Old image, durable-settings bug: random found it in 2/3 reps (6, 8), swarm in 3/3 (17, 19, 38). Distinct signatures: random 11/10/8, swarm 16/17/15.
- Fixed image: MySQL `trx_commit` + cut — random 1/3 (6), swarm 3/3 (11, 22, 24); Redis `everysec` + cut — random 3/3 (10, 39, 60), swarm 2/3 (7, 11); lying disk — random 19/10/1, swarm 1/3/2; I/O errors — random 20/2/4, swarm 4/5/5; distinct signatures random 8/8/8, swarm 10/12/12; failing runs random 18/18/13, swarm 28/32/31. Every failure reproduced bit-for-bit.
- **Verdict:** swarm is broader (more distinct failure kinds, more failing runs) and more consistent (finds most kinds in every repetition), but not faster on the specific old bug when random happens to find it. No new failure with durable settings and an honest disk on the fixed image; swarm-only signatures were a relaxed-MySQL "acked but not marked published" and an I/O-error test-setup failure (`tc setup failed`), not app bugs.

**Conditions:** the module on the bare-metal machine had been rebuilt by someone else from commit `bbe170b` (tick jitter) before/while this ran; the jitter feature was not used, and both pinned reproducers still gave `2403499c9a3f8419` (old) and `f87130453609a5d6` (fixed). Another explorer from `~/hydo-tj` shared the bare-metal machine, so repetitions were cut from 5 to 3.

### Milestone 43 — DONE (timing exploration: seeded tick jitter, explorer dimension, mid-run branching; 2026-10-16)

The explorer varied faults but never the order in which threads run. On one vCPU the guest kernel switches threads at timer ticks and wakeups, and hvx decides where ticks land, so moving ticks moves the interleaving — reproducibly. Commits `bbe170b` (module), `9dffd52`..`996ab07` (explorer, branching), all on `main`; the explorer work was done in the worktree `../hydo-timing` (branch `timing-explorer`) and, on the bare-metal machine, in `~/hydo-tj` (so another session's explorer binary in `~/hydo` was never overwritten).

**Module (`hvx.c`, `hvx_abi.h`; loaded on the bare-metal machine, `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS 25 checks, every fingerprint unchanged):**
- `HVX_SET_TJITTER`/`HVX_GET_TJITTER` (ioctls 45/46, `struct hvx_tjitter`). Needs the PMU clock. Tick k lands `d < period` branches LATER than its nominal grid point (never earlier, no drift); d is a pure function of `(seed, k)` (splitmix64, `h % 1000 < permille`, `d = 1 + (h>>16) % max_delay`, optional tick window `[tick_lo, tick_hi)`), or an explicit `tick:delay` list (≤ 32). The tick index comes from `pmu_next` (the grid point below it), so no snapshot field was added; the policy is deliberately NOT in the snapshot (restore, then set another policy). Off by default.
- HLT fix: an idle HLT with an interrupt already queued is stepped over for EVERY VM (was only with the idle heartbeat). Found when a tick landed on the STI of Linux's `sti; hlt` idle: the STI shadow blocked the injection, the HLT exited with the timer interrupt queued, and the guest stopped (2 of 600 boots in a clock-period sweep).

**Evidence that timing reaches the interleaving.** M31's `HydoWork` (4 threads, an order-dependent locked accumulator): 19 clock settings × 2 JIT configs gave 38 different lock orders, each identical in 4 boots; the order-independent sum never changed. Calibration race `hvx/race/hydorace.c` (two threads, check-then-act lost update, window size W out of a 50,000-iteration loop): 200 seeds each, every hit replayed bit-for-bit —
- clock-period sweep: 25% / 6.5% / 2% of settings hit for windows of 0.1% / 0.02% / 0.004%; the default timing hit none of the two smaller ones;
- seeded jitter on every tick: 21% / 4% / 2%; on 2% of ticks: 22% / 2.5%;
- shrinking (`hvx/race/tj_shrink.py`): sparse jitter shrank to ONE delayed tick (tick 186 delayed 154,033 branches → bug; without it, none) in 23 boots; jitter on every tick does not shrink (removing an early delay changes everything after it).

**Explorer dimension (`explore.py --timing [--tj-pm 50,100,200]`):** host tokens `tj=<seed> tjpm=<n> tjmax=<n> [tjlo=<k> tjhi=<k>]` or `tjx=<k:d,...>`, applied during the workload only (like the disk fault); RESULT gains `tjk=<first>-<last> tjn=<delayed>`; half of the schedules get timing from a separate random stream, so every other field equals the untimed schedule of the same seed (works with `--strategy swarm` and `--sweep`). The shrinker drops the timing, then turns it into an explicit list and removes entries. **Result on the fixed image (`explore-fix-disk.img`), seeds 1–272 (same seeds as M39):** untimed schedules reproduced their M39 fingerprints 138/138; at 5–20 per mille only 62/134 timed schedules were actually perturbed (workloads are often ~60 ticks — the guest idles and the clock jumps), at 50–200 per mille 131/134. **No failure with durable settings and an honest disk (73/73 timed OK)**; totals per class equal M39; 86/86 failures replayed; timing changed only HOW five expected failures showed (e.g. which store could not recover). So: no interleaving-dependent bug in the stack under these schedules.

**Mid-run branching.** `hvx_explore` `BRANCH <tag> <n> <pm> <max> <marker> <schedule>`: run the schedule from S1 to the first return after `<marker>` (no spaces; e.g. `KILLED`), snapshot (S2), finish that run (`-base`), then n seeded variants from S2 (`-v1..`), jitter on later ticks only. **Load-bearing:** every run uses a fixed run budget (`HVX_RUNC` every 16 events; host token `rb=<n>` gives a plain schedule the same cadence) — with plain `HVX_RUN` the return cadence restarts at each call, so a snapshot shifts every later stop point and the end states are not comparable (same stop-point artifact as M27's branch note). With it, variant v is exactly the plain schedule `<host> tj=<v> tjpm=.. tjmax=.. tjlo=<k> rb=16 | <guest>` (checked: base and variant fingerprints reproduce standalone). Power-cut schedules are refused: the volatile write cache is not in the snapshot. `explore.py --branch MARKER --variants N` drives it, re-runs every failing variant standalone, flags timing-only failures. Race guest (once-per-run window, `hvx_race_probe` branch mode): per-try hit rate from the snapshot was not higher (1% vs 1.7% over whole runs), but tries were 2.8× cheaper → ~1.7× hits per hour; a systematic one-tick delay sweep did worse (hits are isolated delays, not a band — whether a switch happens also depends on scheduler accounting). Stack, seeds 1–272 at `KILLED`, 8 variants: 170 schedules, 79 branch points, 802 runs (598/hour on 2 workers), variants 10.2 s vs 24.4 s for a full run; **0 failures among 488 durable-settings runs**; 209 failures, all with injected I/O errors (expected); 6 need the timing (which store fails to recover). One bug found and fixed (`996ab07`): a base run with no branch point went one run call too far; 20 such runs did not reproduce standalone before the fix, two re-checked after it do.

**Addendum (2026-10-17): branching power-cut schedules.** The disk's volatile write cache (writes acknowledged, not yet flushed) is now saved and restored with every explorer snapshot: new ioctls `HVX_GET_UNSYNC`/`HVX_SET_UNSYNC` (47/48, `struct hvx_unsync_io`; `HVX_UNSYNC_MAX`/`HVX_UNSYNC_ENTBYTES` moved to `hvx_abi.h`), additive (no struct changed; `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS 25). `hvx_explore` `snap_take`/`snap_restore` carry it (`HVX_EXPLORE_NO_UNSYNC=1` skips it, diagnostic), branching accepts power-cut schedules, and new branch points `@<N>` (N ticks into the workload) and `@held` (first return where writes are held); `BRANCHPOINT` reports `held=<n>`. **Verified:** two power-cut schedules (seeds 35, 49) branched at `@held` (1 write held): base and both variants equal their standalone schedules, 6/6. **Observed:** in this stack the cache is almost always empty (0 held at 30 sampled points in seed 49 — the guest flushes right after each write, and un-flushed data lives in the guest page cache, which is in RAM), and dropping the held write did not change the end state in those two schedules (the block is rewritten later). So the gap rarely mattered here; the fix is still required for correctness.

**Not done / open:** branch points from assertions — done in Milestone 45 (negative); extra (inserted) preemption ticks; real multi-vCPU interleaving (needs INIT/SIPI AP start-up). Files: `hvx/race/` (hydorace, hvx_race_probe with branch mode, sweeps, `tj_shrink.py`, `tjreg.sh` = regression against a side-by-side `/dev/hvxtj` module), `hvx/hvx_explore.c`, `hvx/explore/explore.py`, `hvx/hvx_jvm_verify.c` (clock knobs `HVX_TSC_STEP`, `HVX_PMU_CLOCK=1`, `HVX_PMU_PERIOD`, `HVX_PMU_TICK_TSC`). Results on the bare-metal machine: `/var/tmp/tj-run1`, `/var/tmp/tj-run2`, `/var/tmp/br-run1`, `/var/tmp/once-*.txt`, `/var/tmp/race_W*.txt`, `/var/tmp/tj_W*.txt`.

### Milestone 44 — DONE, MOSTLY NEGATIVE (coverage growth, whole-system + state signal, portfolio scheduler; 2026-10-17)

Three parallel workstreams (branches `cov-growth`, `rich-signal`, `portfolio`, merged as `e5a8384`, `0a69194`, `3c6233b`). No module change. The merged `hvx_explore` still gives the pinned reproducers `2403499c9a3f8419` (old image) and `f87130453609a5d6` (fixed image) — re-checked after the merge. **Result: none of the three beats plain random or swarm on this stack; no new failure with durable settings and an honest disk in any of them.**

**Coverage growth, random vs guided on the fixed front door (`guided.py`, `covgrowth.py`, `evaluate.sh`).** 3 reps × 40 runs each, same first 6 runs per rep. Mean unique front-door edges at runs 5/10/20/40: random 42.4k/44.4k/45.9k/46.5k, guided 42.4k/44.2k/44.5k/44.8k (features with hit-count buckets at 40: 151.9k vs 128.5k). Both saturate in 10–20 runs; guided's corpus fills with bucket-only variants (24–26 of 40 kept) and re-mutates them; random reaches more error-handling code via fresh disk faults; guided hits unsafe-knob failures more (20 vs 6) because it keeps a relaxed knob once in the corpus. 0 durable-settings failures in 240 runs; every failure reproduced. Confirms M41 on the fixed image. Results `/var/tmp/m44-cov` (`table.md`).

**Bug found and fixed (`hvx_explore.c` `pt_collect`):** when `HVX_SET_PT` fails (ENOMEM on a busy box), `HVX_GET_PT` returns `saved=1`, the module-wide last freed trace, possibly another process's. It used to be decoded as this run's coverage (one arm reported a false 50.8k edges; one decode spun 30+ min). Now the run writes `no_trace=1` and `guided.py`/`richguided.py` compact memory and re-run it (≤ 4 tries). Runs before the fix were discarded (`invalid-enomem/`, `foreign-*`).

**Whole-system + state signal (`hvx/richcov/*`, `hvx/explore/richguided.py`; DESIGN.md has Implementation + Results).** Images `richcov-old-disk.img` (`059021d7…`, pre-fix front door) and `richcov-fix-disk.img` (`cfc71a50…`) via `RICHCOV_VARIANT=old|fix bash hvx/richcov/build-richcov.sh`; serve mode via `build-stack.sh` for `richcov-*`. `hvx_explore` env (inert when unset): `HVX_EXPLORE_PT_RANGES` (two IP ranges), `HVX_EXPLORE_PTMULTI` (per-program decode), `HVX_EXPLORE_CMDLINE_EXTRA=norandmaps`. Guest signals from `patch-src.py`: `HYDO-STATE` lines (distinct per-order state across client/MySQL/Kafka/Redis; the old bug shows as `client=other mysql=published kafka=1 redis=absent`) and distinct-details assertions. With `norandmaps` mysqld sits at `0x555555554000`, Redis at `0x40001000`. Clean run: front door 40,132 edges, Redis 3,124, mysqld 36,780; mysqld never saturates (~70% of runs add edges). Cost: median trace 40 MB, decode 10 s, run 25 s; **129 of 439 traces filled the 256 MiB buffer and were cut** (mostly kill and I/O-error runs — the runs that matter); 512 MiB could not be allocated. Determinism: tracing on/off same fingerprint; two passes over 40 schedules 40/40 fingerprints identical, 28/29 bucketed feature sets identical (off-by-one hit counts on a few mysqld edges). Old bug, run first found (cap 60), 3 starts: random -/26/2, `rich` 48/8/12 (always found by the every-4th random schedule, never by guidance), `richk` (per-kind normalized score) 9/43/12 (twice by a mutation — a hint, not a result). Fixed image, 40 runs: state tuples rich 8 / edges 8 / random 12; no durable-settings failures. Verdict: a plain sum lets thousands of mysqld edges swamp the few state/property features; random still reaches more states. Results `/var/tmp/m44-rich` (`richreport.py`).

**Portfolio scheduler (`hvx/explore/portfolio.py`, `compare.py portfolio`).** Thompson sampling over arms random / swarm / timing / branching (swarm kill schedule branched at `KILLED`, base + 3 variants; still excludes power-cut schedules — one-line filter in `Arm.next` now that the addendum above allows them) / sweeps (finite). Reward = a never-seen failure signature or assertion outcome. Deterministic: pulls chosen in seeded batches of 4, posterior updated in pull order. 3 reps × 60 runs vs M42 data: old image bug found 3/3 (runs 34, 18, 18; swarm 17, 19, 38), distinct signatures 14/7/10 (swarm 16/17/15); fixed image signatures 5/8/7 (swarm 10/12/12). Rewards are too rare after run 10 for the bandit to separate arms in 60 runs; property firsts all arrive early and credit whichever arm ran then. 116/116 failures reproduced; 191 runs with schedules shared with M42 gave the same fingerprints. Results `/var/tmp/m44-port`.

**Open:** a reward that does not saturate early (per-kind normalized, `richk`, is the lead) with more starts; larger PT buffers (or per-program ranges per run) so fault runs are not truncated; branch points from coverage/assertions (the other session, now that the signal exists); the build machine's `~/hydo/build/richcov-disk.img` was rebuilt once with a substitute static musl gcc (its nix store had been garbage-collected) — the bare-metal machine copy `9d1e432c…` is untouched; rebuild the build machine copy before using it.

### Milestone 45 — DONE, NEGATIVE RESULT (branch points from assertions; 2026-10-17)

Branch points chosen from what the run did, using Milestone 44's signal, instead of a fixed console line. `hvx/explore/abranch.py`: run a batch of fresh schedules plainly (`rb=16`); for each run take the "event" assertions it satisfied during the workload (`server.store_error`, `server.kafka_recheck`, `server.outcome_unknown`, `client.retry_recovered`, ... — first occurrences, console order), pick the one seen in the fewest runs so far, and branch the same schedule at its first occurrence (marker `"id":"<event>","ok":true`) with N seeded timing variants. `hvx_explore` BRANCH with a negative variant count skips finishing the base run (the plain run already is that run). Measure, as in `richguided.py` without edges: distinct P/D/S features (assertion firsts, assertion outcomes with bucketed details, `HYDO-STATE` tuples) and distinct failure signatures, per run budget.

**Result (image `richcov-fix-disk.img`, same seeds, 150 runs each, 3 workers, 6 variants; `/var/tmp/m45-abranch` on the bare-metal machine):** random schedules 59 features / 11 signatures (1,052 s); whole-run timing 58 / 12 (1,028 s); branching at `KILLED` 53 / 6 (1,713 s, 174 runs); **assertion-guided branching 53 / 6 (1,552 s, 18 branch points over 12 event kinds)**. No durable-settings failure in any strategy; 186/186 failures reproduced. Variants of one schedule mostly repeat each other, so a run budget spent on them covers fewer situations than fresh schedules, and the snapshot/restore work makes them no cheaper here. Same conclusion as M41–M44: on this stack, variety of schedules beats exploring around one moment. The tool stays useful when a moment is already known to matter (reproduce, then explore around a found failure).

### Milestone 46 — DONE, NO NEW BUG (pause, clock-jump and one-way-loss faults; 2026-10-17)
