New guest fault tokens (branch `new-faults`, merged; only `hvx/explore/explore.py` and `hvx/explore/stack-explore.sh` changed; inert when absent): **pause** `P=svc@at+n` (SIGSTOP before order `at`, SIGCONT before order `at+n`) and `P=svc@at~off:dur` (timer: pause after `off` ms of guest time for `dur` ms while orders run; can land mid-request, `sometimes fault.pause_mid_request`), for mysql/kafka/redis or the front door itself; **clock jump** `T=at:delta` (guest REALTIME stepped by −30 d … +1 y before order `at`, like an NTP step; the virtual TSC is untouched so Linux's monotonic clock is unaffected; each step checked to have applied); **one-way loss** `O=svc@from-to:req|resp:netem` (loopback netem filtered on destination port = requests, or source port = replies; never drawn together with `N=`). Each emits a `reachable` record (`fault.paused`, `fault.clock_back/fwd`, `fault.oneway_req/resp`). `explore.py --new-faults` (separate random stream: 35% per fault under random, 50% under swarm), `--durable-only` (all stores durable, honest disk: lying disk → plain cut, no I/O errors or relaxed knobs), sweeps `pause` (48) / `clock` (18) / `oneway` (18), shrinker drops or weakens the new faults. Guest fix: the guest has no RTC, so realtime started near 1970 and backward steps were silently refused; every boot in the new image starts at 2026-01-01.

**Checks:** schedule strings for seeds 1–1000 (random, swarm, ±`--timing`) and all four old sweeps identical to main's; pinned reproducers still `2403499c9a3f8419` / `f87130453609a5d6`; image `explore-nf-disk.img` (`e5d8d6fb…`) byte-identical in two builds, front-door binary identical to `explore-fix-disk.img`'s; 15 smoke schedules covering every new fault (also with a kill and with a cut) each identical in two runs.

**Results (`explore-nf-disk.img`, 2 workers, `/var/tmp/m46-faults` on the bare-metal machine):** swarm + new faults seeds 1–200: 120 failures, 0 with durable settings (46 durable runs), 120/120 reproduced, all expected classes (54 lying disk, 50 I/O errors incl. setup errors, 16 relaxed knobs); durable-only swarm seeds 1–200 (166 with a new fault): 0 failures; durable-only random seeds 201–400 (~150 with a new fault): 0; sweeps 84 points: 0. Faults fired: 176 pauses (57 mid-request), 137 clock steps, 94 one-way faults; 25 unknown-outcome orders reached Kafka (allowed), 0 told-failed orders reached Kafka. **The fixed front door has no bug under these faults.** Clock steps changed nothing observable (every clock-sweep run 30/30 acked): the 30-order workload (~0.5 s guest time) is too short for wall-clock timers to matter — a longer workload or lease-based services are needed for clock faults to bite. The timed-pause offset was narrowed 0–3000 → 0–500 ms (`6a24038`) after the first swarm run landed only 7 pauses mid-request.

**Build note:** the build machine's nix store no longer has the pinned static musl gcc or static Redis (garbage-collected). The image was built in `~/hydo-faults` on the build machine with `HYDO_GCC_MUSL` (a wrapper copying the prebuilt `hvx_seedrng`) and `HYDO_REDIS_OUT` (prebuilt `redis-server`/`redis-cli`); a from-scratch rebuild of any image needing those pins must restore them first.

**Not done:** the bug benchmark (item 3 of this round) — its agent stopped at the start when a safety classifier blocked part of a response; nothing was built (empty branch `bug-bench`).

### Milestone 47 — DONE (onboarding v1: a project is one `hydo.yaml`; 2026-10-18)

`hvx/onboard/` (branch `onboard`, merged): `MANIFEST.md` (schema: files/store paths for the root; services with cmd, ready check, port, order, recovery start; a workload split into numbered steps; optional durability knobs the explorer may relax; invariants as shell commands, exit 0 holds / 1 violated / 2 could not check, run `every:` during the run and/or `final:`), `hydo-build` (plain-Python compiler with its own YAML-subset parser — the build machine has no YAML library; unknown keys are errors; byte-identical rebuilds checked for both examples), `hydo-init.sh` (the ONE guest init: CRNG seed, lo, ordered start with readiness waits, explorer serve mode S0/S1, every fault token applied by service name from the manifest, crash restarts, power-cut recovery; invariants become HYDO-ASSERT records and the HYDO-ORACLE line, so `explore.py` and the reports work unchanged; per-step `workload.timeout`). Examples: `examples/redis` (~60 lines) and `examples/stack` (the four-service stack). Results (`hvx/onboard/RESULTS.md`, `/var/tmp/m47-onboard`, images `~/hydo/build/explore-ob-*-disk.img`), each schedule twice with identical fingerprints: stack fixed no-fault OK `fd5d47a611683780`; fixed with Kafka kill + MySQL pause OK `f50893ec3f010153`; old front door + pinned reproducer VIOLATION order 11 `2eca7c7d0619e84d`; fixed + same OK `798ebf5e43758154`; the 7 M42 bug schedules 7/7 fail on old (same orders as M42) and 7/7 pass on fixed; Redis `everysec` + cut loses an acked key (documented window). 40 swarm schedules per image: 48 failures, all reproduced, all expected classes. **Limits:** no Docker import (`image:` reserved); guest RAM fixed 1 GiB by `hvx_explore`; `explore.py`'s generators know only the stack's service names (mysql/kafka/redis/front) and knobs M/R — hand-written schedules work for any name; the OK verdict shows only the first invariant's details; stack images reuse prebuilt front-door binaries.

### Milestone 48 — DONE (deterministic arm64 Linux on an Apple Silicon Mac via Hypervisor.framework; 2026-10-18)

The native-Mac path. `hvx-mac/PROBE.md` (capability probe on an M2 Pro, macOS 26.4.1, no root): exits deterministic (~1.3 M/s, host interrupts never leak); unmapped-memory exits arrive decoded; `CNTPCT_EL0` and every `CNTP_*` register trap; single-step exact (201/201, ~420 k steps/s); IRQ lands at the chosen instruction; snapshot/restore bit-identical; no `RNDR`. Does NOT work: `CNTVCT_EL0` does not trap (host time minus offset, 0.4–3 µs jitter), no guest PMU/instruction counter on M2, `WFE`/`YIELD` do not exit. `hvx-mac/BOOT.md` + `hvx-mac/hvm.c` (~680 lines): a small HVF VMM — GICv2, 16550 UART, PSCI emulated over MMIO/HVC in one snapshot-able struct; virtual time = fixed step per exit, seen only through trapped `CNTPCT_EL0`/`CNTP_*`; idle WFI jumps to the next deadline; the IRQ line is raised only at an entry where the guest has interrupts unmasked (after an idle WFI, single-step exactly to the unmasking instruction). **Real arm64 Linux 6.6.35 boots to the busybox shell bit-identically:** 20 boots `81711ada76a4a236`, 15 boots with all 12 host cores saturated the same, branch at 3 points (continue = restore into a new vCPU = fresh boot), 1 GiB `4ffe2f2cc064708e` and 4 GiB `ace950b0772ecdd5`; ~15.5 k exits, ~0.04 s to the shell. Guest build byte-reproducible. **Walls:** (1) kernel patch needed — `CONFIG_ARM_ARCH_TIMER_FORCE_CNTPCT` (~20 lines, `hvx-mac/linux/force-cntpct.patch`): the device tree alone moves the timer but not the counter, and the RNG mixes the virtual counter before boot parameters are parsed; the option also traps user-space `CNTVCT_EL0` (checked) and disables the vDSO counter path; (2) interrupts pending while masked landed at host-timed instructions — fixed by the unmasked-entry rule; (3) single-stepping an LL/SC loop never finishes (each step clears the exclusive monitor) — kernel built with LSE atomics, `hvm` stops loudly if a step lands on one. **Limits:** a pure memory-flag spin in user space with no exits cannot be interrupted deterministically (`/bin/spinwait` demonstrates it; a wall-clock watchdog marks the run invalid instead of hanging) — clock-polling loops are fine since every counter read exits; one vCPU; interrupt latency coarser (waits for an unmasked exit); no virtio devices or fault injection yet; branch copies RAM in memory. M3/M4 with guest EL2 (emulated PMU, `CNTHCTL_EL2`) is the hardware route around the spin limit — unprobed.

### Milestone 49 — DONE (finer preemption for narrow races: shorter tick period + inserted ticks; 2026-10-18)

Branch `fine-preempt`, merged; developed and verified on a side-by-side module `/dev/hvxpre` — loaded into the main `hvx.ko` on 2026-10-19 (`deploy-metal.sh load`, srcversion `7CA13762C89AF3BF16F2CCF`): ALL PASS (25 checks), pinned reproducers `2403499c9a3f8419` / `f87130453609a5d6` unchanged. Opt-in, inert when absent. **Shorter period:** host token `pp=<branches>` sets the PMU tick period for the workload only (old period restored after); each tick still adds the normal 2 ms of guest time (deliberately not scaled: scaling would keep switches per unit of work the same), `pptsc=` overrides. **Inserted ticks:** `HVX_SET_XTICK`/`HVX_GET_XTICK` (ioctls 49/50): seeded extra ticks between grid ticks (`xt=<seed> xtr=<slots> xtpm=<permille>`, optional window) or an explicit branch-count list (`xtx=`); not in the snapshot (the next tick target is enough); RESULT reports `xtn=`. `explore.py --preempt` gives half the schedules one lever from a separate random stream; the shrinker drops then weakens it; without it 16,600 schedule strings are identical (re-checked after merging with the Mac backend + jitter). `hunt.py --dev`, `devshim.so` + `side-metal.sh` run the regression suite against a side module (25/25 on `/dev/hvxpre`); pinned reproducers unchanged. **Calibration race** (`hvx/race/PREEMPT.md`, 200 seeds per cell, all 664 hits replayed): window 0.1% / 0.02% / 0.004% — default 0/0/0, jitter 21%/4%/2%, `pp`=2^16 + jitter 97%/59%/25%, inserted 16 slots @50% 84%/33%/10%; cost per race boot 1.5 s → 6.2 s (`pp`) / 4.0 s (inserted). **SQLite 3.51.2: still not found** in 600 blind runs (`pp` 2^14/2^12 + jitter, inserted 8/32 slots; up to 13,603 ticks and 6,347 involuntary switches per run vs ~58 and ~711 before) — the switch count grew far less than the tick count (unverified reason: faster guest time also shortens the work per run). Not done: targeted insertion near shared-memory activity; branch mode refuses `pp`/`xt`; the `xt` shrinker does not turn a seeded policy into a list.

### Milestone 50 — DONE (concurrent clients find three real bugs in the order service; 2026-10-18)

(Numbered 50 because the other session's preemption work uses 49.) Until now the stack's client placed orders one at a time, so the order service never handled two requests at once. Commits `029e4b3`..`6813027` (branch `timing-explorer`, merged into `main` as `5935cfd`). No module change.

**Built.** Client (`hvx/stack/main.go`): `HYDO_CONC=<n>` runs n requests at once, and `HYDO_DUP=1` sends every order twice (a client retry that overlaps the original). Explorer (`explore.py --concurrency`): guest token `Q=<n>[d]`, n in {2,4,8}, on 60% of schedules, `d` on 30% of those. It uses its own random stream, so without `--concurrency` the schedule strings are identical to before (checked: 8,000 strings against `main`). Timed kill `X=<svc>@<order>~<ms>` (SIGKILL after ms of guest time while orders run, so it can land mid-request; `fault.killed_timer`). Fixed list `--sweep frontkill`: the order service killed at orders 0, 3, ... 27, at 20 and 80 ms, each with and without `Q=4`, 40 schedules in all. `stack-explore.sh` prints the order service's `fatal error`/`panic` lines when it died. The shrinker can drop or weaken `Q` and `X`.

**Three bugs in the order service, all found and fixed:**
1. **A retry duplicated the order.** A franz-go v1.17.1 idempotent producer cannot give up on a record that is already in flight: neither the request context nor `RecordDeliveryTimeout` stops it. So `ProduceSync` returned long after the client's deadline (1 min 40 s in a diagnostic image), and the client's retry ran a second produce at the same time. Fix (`b9233d5`): only one `Place` per order id runs at a time. A second request waits, and if its deadline passes first it answers "outcome unknown (in-flight)".
2. **The shared Redis connection crashed the service** under concurrent requests (`panic: runtime error: slice bounds out of range [15:10]`). Fix (`b9233d5`): a lock around the Redis calls.
3. **A crash of the order service forgot which Kafka sends had an unknown outcome**, so a retry after the restart sent the order again. Fix (`6813027`): the state lives in MySQL. `published` is 0 when nothing was sent, 2 when a send started, and 1 when published. A `published=2` row is checked against Kafka (`kafkaHasKey`) before sending. The order is marked 2 with `UPDATE ... WHERE published = 0` (if that fails, the answer is "not applied"), then produced, then marked 1 (if that fails, the answer is "outcome unknown"). The in-memory maps are gone, and the oracle treats only `published = 1` as published.

**Evidence** (images built on the build machine by `build-stack.sh` and copied to the bare-metal machine: `explore-cc0` = no fix, `explore-cc1` = fixes 1+2, `explore-cc` = all three):
- No fix: every concurrent schedule produced a duplicate. With all three fixes, every check in `hvx_stack_verify mode=all` passed.
- The `frontkill` list of 40 schedules: `explore-cc1` failed 25 of 40 (duplicates after a kill, all 25 repeated exactly); `explore-cc` failed 0 of 40.
- Broad run on `explore-cc`: 200 schedules, swarm + `--concurrency --new-faults --timing`, 2 workers (`/var/tmp/cc-broad` on the bare-metal machine, about 3 hours with shrinking). 128 schedules were concurrent and 95 had timing jitter. **0 failures among the 46 runs with every store durable and an honest disk** (35 of them concurrent). The run found 109 failures, all of them repeated exactly and all in expected kinds (lying disk, injected I/O errors, relaxed durability settings). Every shortened schedule gave the same fingerprint twice. `order.kafka_exactly_once` passed in all 114 runs that checked it, and `order.told_failed_but_applied` was never hit.
- Both saved bug schedules are unchanged with the merged `hvx_explore`: `2403499c9a3f8419` (old image) and `f87130453609a5d6` (fixed image).

**Notes / open:** the stack's default baselines (M39) will move on the next default `build-stack.sh` build, because the order service changed. They have not been re-measured. With both producer fixes in place, a request whose original send is still in flight answers "outcome unknown" rather than waiting. That is honest, but a client may need several retries. The bare-metal machine tree `~/hydo-tj` had lacked `hvx/hydo_assert_table.h`, so the report step failed once; it is copied now.

### Milestone 51 — DONE (the Mac backend explores real projects; 2026-10-18)

Follow-ups to M48, all merged, all on the M2 Pro. **Devices** (`hvx-mac/virtio*.c`, `VIRTIO.md`, `cut-test.sh`): virtio-mmio blk + net; disk faults use hvx's seed→schedule rule (delay counted in exits, not instructions; classes delay/error/drop/reorder/torn + the flush-aware volatile cache, uncapped); net drop/corrupt/duplicate; completions at fixed virtual-time offsets; device state, disk image, cache and captured TX in the snapshot/fingerprint; env vars `HVM_DISK`/`HVM_DISK_OUT`/`HVM_FAULT*`/`HVM_NET*`. Proofs: disk test 3 boots `952991033b38776d` (also under load), branch identical, seeded DROP loses different records per seed, two-boot cut keeps only flushed bytes, lying disk loses fsync'd data while flush-aware does not (the M26 contrast). The lying-disk rows in VIRTIO.md were recorded before the initramfs changed for the `cut` phase; re-measured: seed `0x5017E0` `8afdcfbe731e3dd9`, `0x5017E1` `bb138c4d2d7e36bc` (same verdicts). **Services** (`hvx-mac/services/`): Redis 7.2.4 and miniflux 2.1.3 + PostgreSQL 15.7 (pinned aarch64 nixpkgs, `elfdeps.py` copies only loaded libraries) deterministic on the disk; durable setting + cut loses nothing, relaxed loses an acked key/category (documented windows; Redis loses `k:0` as in M27); no spin-watchdog hit in 70+ boots. **Explorer** (`hvx-mac/explore/`, `hvx/explore/hvm_backend.py`, `hvx/onboard/hydo_build_arm64.py`): `hvmx` speaks hvx_explore's serve protocol; `explore.py --backend hvm`; `hydo-build --arch arm64` (refuses `params:` for now); Redis 100 swarm schedules 37 failures, all expected, all reproduced; ledger example (planted non-idempotent retry) shrunk to `P=front@26+1`; ~11,500 runs/hour. Linux path byte-identical (16,600 schedule strings; pinned reproducers). **Load divergence fixed** (`c3a0092`): an Arm `WFI` completes in the guest when a host interrupt is pending, so the idle loop ran once more without an exit (RCU context-tracking counter and a `tick_cpu_sched` field differed); kernel option `CONFIG_ARM64_HVM_IDLE_HVC` (`explore/apply-hvcidle.py`, default in `build-guest.sh`) idles via HVC `0xC6000020`; plus the spin observer re-arms when a kick lands off its armed PC. 4,880/4,880 explore runs and 2,000/2,000 spin boots identical under heavy load. Remaining envelope: guest code using `WFE` or LL/SC loops could still diverge under load. **Spin limit** (`hvx-mac/spin/RESEARCH.md`): exact-repeat loops are resolved deterministically (single vCPU, no exits ⇒ no external memory change; observer = host-timer hint + hardware breakpoint + write-protect, delivers at a canonical loop state); counter loops via rewind to a copy-on-write checkpoint at the last exit (`hvm-spin-count.c`, `HVM_SPIN_REWIND=1`, boot 1.25 s vs 0.06 s); undocumented `_hv_vcpu_set_control_field` HCR_EL2.TWE makes `WFE` trap (YIELD does not). **Host PMU measured** (`pmuprobe`, run with sudo): configurable counters never count the guest; `FIXED_INSTRUCTIONS` counts guest + varying host work (spread thousands) — no exact guest counter on the M2. **Open:** packaging for other users, spin observer not yet in `hvm.c`/`serve.c`, network faults (guest root lacks `tc`), tick jitter on the exit clock — all four in progress (branches `mac-dist`, `mac-spin-int`, `mac-netfault`, `mac-jitter`).

### Milestone 52 — onboarding follow-ups and a negative known-bug result (2026-10-18)

- **Explorer reads the manifest** (`hvx/explore/manifest_space.py`, `explore.py --manifest`, auto for `explore-ob-*`): services, ports, steps, knobs and allowed faults from `hydo.yaml`; byte-identical without it (16,600 strings; pinned reproducers). Stack old image found the old bug blind at swarm run 50; fixed image 0 durable failures. Knob tokens colliding with built-in tokens (K P N O C T Q X) are rejected.
- **Faults hit a service's whole process tree** (`8ef674e`, `hydo-init.sh` `svc_tree`/`svc_sig`): found by miniflux, where pausing only the postmaster left backends serving and the cut freeze left the WAL writer flushing — hiding `synchronous_commit=off`'s documented loss window; with the fix it loses an acked category reproducibly.
- **Manifest `params:` and empty `services:`** (from the SQLite branch).
- **miniflux end to end** (`hvx/onboard/examples/miniflux/`, `RESULTS.md`): the first project we did not write, from the skill's manifest (miniflux 2.3.3 + PostgreSQL 15.6). Worked first time: `su … exec` start (PID = postmaster), `dynamic_shared_memory_type=mmap`, readiness, migrations, retries counting "already exists" as applied, the `S` knob, crash recovery. Needed fixing: faults reaching only the postmaster (the process-tree fix above); the periodic invariant needs a workload longer than its period. No bug in miniflux/PostgreSQL; the only violation without a disk fault is `synchronous_commit=off` + cut (`8f7290a59489aaac`); 30-seed swarm 10 failures, all I/O-error or lying-disk, all reproduced.
- **M47 baselines on the process-tree init** (images `explore-ob-{redis,stack-old,stack-fix}-pt-disk.img`; all verdicts unchanged, fingerprints moved because the init bytes changed): redis sanity `892c21e720aa888f`, `C=12 R=everysec` loses k:4 `d67dc6a6292054d7`; stack-old sanity `c326a384771a8444`, pinned reproducer VIOLATION order=11 `58f2f804b86f7e82`, 7/7 M42 schedules fail (same orders); stack-fix sanity `004b53662fdb8c5c`, pinned reproducer OK `64955f025db1d0cf`, 7/7 M42 schedules OK.
- **SQLite 3.51.2 WAL-reset corruption: NOT reproduced** (`hvx/knownbugs/sqlite/`): 1,150 hvx runs (~100 min, tick jitter + workload seeds, ~1.2 M thread switches) and ~5,800 native seeds, 0 violations. Likely reason (from the fix, after the blind runs): a window of a few hundred instructions, while hvx switches threads only at ticks (one per 2^20 branches). Follow-up: finer preemption (M49 in progress).

### Milestone 53 — DONE (tick jitter on the Mac VMM, counted in VM exits; 2026-10-18)

The Mac VMM can now vary thread interleavings as hvx does (M43), but it moves timer interrupts by exits, not by branches: the M2 has no PMU clock. **How** (`hvx-mac/hvm.c`, `tjit TJ`): the interrupt of a deadline in tick period k (k = deadline / (CNTFRQ/250), the guest's 4 ms tick) is raised d(k) exits late. d(k) uses hvx's formula and tokens (`tj=`/`tjpm=`/`tjmax=`/`tjlo=`/`tjhi=`, `tjx=k:d`), so `explore.py`'s shrinker works unchanged. The policy is host-side, outside `struct mach`, so it is in neither the snapshot nor the fingerprint. `hvmx` applies it for the workload only and reports `tjk=`/`tjn=`. `hvm` takes `HVM_TJ=<seed>,<permille>,<max>[,<lo>,<hi>]`. `hvm_backend.py` keeps `tj` and caps a generated `tjmax` at 1,000 exits; a busy guest takes about 1,500 exits per tick. **Inert when off:** the M48 boot is still `81711ada76a4a236`, the Redis `disk=none | run` is still `ca13bcd1d77e2b4b` (HVC-idle kernel `2506bb4d…`), and on the network-fault images (`f80c5655…`) main's `hvmx` and this one agree on plain, cut, network and kill schedules. **Limit, measured** (`hvx-mac/race/macrace.c`, once-per-run seat race, two processes; `seeds.sh`): a window with 50 clock reads (exits) was oversold in 10 of 200 seeds, and all 10 repeated exactly; a window of 50,000 iterations of plain work (no exit) was oversold in 0 of 200; with no jitter, neither was. A thread switch can only land at an exit, so windows without exits are out of reach. On this guest kernel every clock read exits, so kernel code, system calls that take time, and Go and Java programs are reachable. Workloads are short in ticks (Redis: 7), so use `--tj-pm 500,1000` on the Mac. `explore.py --backend hvm --timing` on Redis, 40 swarm seeds: 15 of the 17 timed schedules were perturbed, and 16 of 16 failures reproduced. Details: `hvx-mac/explore/README.md` "Tick jitter".

### Milestone 54 — DONE (an 8-defect bug benchmark; five strategies compared; 2026-10-19)

`hvx/bench/` (branch `bug-bench`, merged): d0 = the old front door (`explore-disk.img`), d1–d7 = one realistic defect each patched into a copy of the fixed front door (`patches/`, `build-bench.sh`; images `explore-bench-<name>-disk.img` on the bare-metal machine; an unpatched rebuild is byte-identical to `explore-nf-disk.img` `e5d8d6fb…`): d1 publish to Kafka before MySQL, d2 batched `published` flag, d3 blind retry, d4 a >5 s Kafka send reported "not applied" (needs a slow Kafka + a cut), d5 MyISAM orders table, d6 client treats its own timeout as definite failure, d7 off-by-one Kafka lookup skips the last record. Each confirmed by a hand-written durable-settings trigger that fails twice identically and passes on the control image (`confirm.py`; fingerprints in the README). `run.py` counts a detection only if the schedule passes on the control (the control itself carries the M50 duplicate-in-Kafka bug, `O=kafka@25-30:resp:loss,100%` → order 26 twice, `f1c39a886803a8fa`; never triggered a rejection). **Evaluation** (3 reps × ≤60 runs, early stop, module srcversion `53856EFDC33F55145778DA8`; 100/100 detections reproduced and matched their signature; results `/var/tmp/m46-bench/`): detections out of 24 — random 19, random+new faults 19, swarm 21, swarm+new faults 21, portfolio 20. Every strategy finds the same 7 defects and none finds **d7** (needs a fault covering the end of the workload; a sweep ending its faults at the last order would probably find it). Random fastest on easy defects but misses d0/d4 once each; swarm finds every reachable defect every time but slower (d0 reproduced M42's exact run numbers 17/19/38); the M46 faults add nothing to random and speed swarm on d1/d3/d6 (a plain Kafka kill already produces a send that fails then lands later, which is what d3/d4/d6 need); portfolio best on d4 (3/3, median 21) but slower on easy defects. Also noted: franz-go `ProduceSync` does not honour the request deadline for an in-flight record (the M50 root cause).

### Milestone 55 — DONE (why d7 was missed; end-of-workload sweep; fault-search literature; 2026-10-20)

`--sweep tail` (165 points: every fault kind starting at one of the last 3 orders) finds d7 in 9 minutes. d7 needs Kafka unavailable until the end of the workload (the last order's write is uncertain; its record is the last in the topic, which d7's lookup skips). Per-run trigger probability computed over 1 M generated schedules (`hvx/bench/d7prob.py`): random/swarm effectively never; random+new faults 1 in ~2,800 (~4.3 h); swarm+new faults 1 in ~5,900 (~9 h). A 1,500-run random+nf run hit it at run 1,420 as predicted, but the schedule carried an irrelevant `R=everysec`, so it was classed unsafe-knob — which led to the knob rule (M56). Harness artefact noted: Kafka killed at the last order makes the oracle read Kafka before it restarts ("kafka topic unreadable"). `hvx/explore/RESEARCH-search.md`: literature review (OSDI'14/'18 bug studies: bugs shallow but position-sensitive; random strong vs systematic; Jepsen recurring nemesis; FoundationDB BUGGIFY; CrashMonkey/ALICE crash points; PCT) with five ranked recommendations — items 1–4 done in M56; item 5 (PCT) done on the Mac in M57, with forced points counted in VM exits.

### Milestone 56 — DONE (fault placement beats search strategy: fault process, BUGGIFY, depth-1 sweep, cut at the k-th flush; 2026-10-21)

All merged; explore.py old schedule strings byte-identical (16,600), classes identical under `--knob-rule any`.
- **Knob rule (new default):** a relaxed durability knob counts as unsafe-knob only when the schedule also has a power cut or lying disk (`--knob-rule any` = old rule). Re-scored M54: still 7/8 per strategy, 10 cells faster (`hvx/bench/m56/tables.md`).
- **`--strategy process`** (Jepsen-style: 2–4 fault kinds in staggered windows over the workload, last window reaches the end half the time): **24/24** on the benchmark (23 signature-matching), d7 3/3 at runs 28/27/4, ~1 h box time; per-run d7 probability 1 in 25. **`--strategy swarm-pos`** (per-category position bias): 21/24, d7 1/3, slower — position alone does not help.
- **`--sweep depth1`** (1,274 single-fault points): trigger maps per defect (`hvx/bench/m56/map-*.json`, `depth1map.py`): d7 29 triggers (Kafka paused/killed until the end from many starts, last-order reply loss, one MySQL point), d3 196, d6 186, d4 and d0 none (they need two faults). The control's own M50 duplicate bug shows on 87 of 1,274 points (any long Kafka outage). 2 of 205 failing points re-ran with the same verdict but a different fingerprint (long Kafka outages; not investigated).
- **BUGGIFY** (`hvx/stack/buggify.go`, `hydoassert.Buggify`, guest token `B=<seed>[,<enable-pm>,<fire-pm>]`, `--buggify`; FoundationDB defaults 0.25 enable / 0.25 fire, read from `flow/include/flow/Buggify.h`; 14 points around every store call of `Place`, each taking the code's own error path): with BUGGIFY random and swarm both **24/24**, d7 random 4/7/2 and swarm 9/45/6; fixed front door (with M50 fixes) 100 durable runs, all 14 points fired, 0 failures. Images `explore-bench-bg-*`; `build-bench.sh` now builds from stored base front doors (`hvx/bench/base/`), because the patches no longer applied to main.go after M50.
- **Power cut at the k-th disk FLUSH** (`HVX_SET_FLUSHCUT`/`GET_FLUSHCUT`, ioctls 51/52; host token `cf=<k>`, `cfn` counts flushes; only the data partition loses power, the read-only root keeps being served): ~200–280 FLUSHes per 30-order workload. Verified on the side module `/dev/hvxcf` (25/25), then loaded into the main hvx.ko on 2026-10-21 (srcversion `4B0507C2F473F608587B632`, `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS 25, pinned reproducers unchanged, `cf=140` runs on the main module; `hvxcf.ko` unloaded). **Open:** on the fixed image with durable settings, 12 of 282 cut points give `mysql order=N acked but not marked published` (cf204–cf264) — not yet re-run or analysed; could be a real durability bug or an artefact of the cut model. Tables: `/var/tmp/m56-cf`, `hvx/cutflush/cftable.py`.
- **Verdict:** where and in what combination faults land matters more than how the next schedule is chosen; a simple fault process or in-code fault points find every planted defect, the strategies of M41–M45 did not.

### Milestone 57 — DONE (PCT-style forced preemption points on the Mac VMM; 2026-10-21)

PCT forces thread switches at d-1 random points among the k steps of a run, and its guarantee shrinks as k^(d-1). On the Mac a step is a VM exit, since switches can only happen there, so k is the number of exits. A race in `hvx-mac/race/macrace.c` spans about 20,500 exits, against about a billion branches. **Built:** `hvx-mac/hvm.c` `PCT`: m seeded exits in a range; at each, the timer deadline is made due at once, so the guest takes its tick there and switches if the running thread used its slice (about 2 in 3 points). Thread priorities are not modelled: the guest's scheduler picks. `HVM_PCT=` for `hvm`; `pct=`/`pctn=`/`pctk=` and `pctf=` in `hvmx`. Host-side, off by default; `hvmx` results without the tokens equal main's. **Measured** (200 seeds per cell, `hvx-mac/race/cells.sh`):
- a one-window race of 50 clock reads: jitter 11, 1 point 5, 2 points 6, 10 points 11, **40 points 17**;
- a race needing two such windows: 0 for every strategy in 200; then over 1,000 seeds, **40 points 7** and jitter 0;
- windows of plain work: 0 everywhere.

All 24 hits repeated exactly. Few points (PCT's own setting) did no better than jitter, because a forced point also moves the clock and the guest picks the next thread, so PCT's guarantee does not carry over. Many points did better, and only many points found the two-ordering bug. In the explorer: `explore.py --backend hvm --preempt` gives half the schedules `pct=`/`pctn=`/`pctk=` (ranges `--pct-exits`, default 3,000 and 10,000 exits; RESULT `pctw=` gives the workload length) and drops the Linux-only `pp=`/`xt=` there. On Linux, generated schedules are identical to before. On Redis, 100 swarm seeds: 39 failures, all reproduced, none with durable settings. Details: `hvx-mac/explore/README.md` "Forced preemption points".

### Milestone 58 — DONE (a real bug found by the flush cut; a harness stop-point bug fixed; 2026-10-21)

- **Bug in the order service, found only by cutting power at the k-th FLUSH** (`hvx/cutflush/FINDING.md`). With durable settings and an honest disk, 12 of 282 flush-cut points on `explore-nf-disk.img` and 13 of 118 (k=200..317) on the current front door (`explore-bench-bg-fixed-disk.img`, with the M50 fixes) end `VIOLATION mysql order=N acked but not marked published`; every point re-ran twice with identical fingerprints. Mechanism: the stack's MySQL runs with `skip-log-bin`, and without the binary log InnoDB makes a commit visible to other sessions before its redo fsync completes. The cut stalls the fsync of `UPDATE ... SET published = 1`; the first attempt hits the client deadline; the client's retry (new connection) reads `published = 1`, skips publishing and is ACKed; after recovery the row is not published. The M50 per-order lock does not close it (released when the first request returns on its deadline while its commit still waits). The trace is not a pure power loss (the ACK goes out ~160–200 s of guest time after the cut, because only the data partition loses power); it is the trace of a hung disk followed by a crash, and the same window opens during a normal fsync when two requests for one order overlap. Minimal: `disk=cut dseed=0 cf=204 | run` on explore-nf (`e592e6c6fb6e5f3a`), `disk=cut dseed=0 cf=242 | run` on bg-fixed (`5c04f27153945d7b`). Location: `hvx/stack/main.go` `place`, the branch where `published == 1` goes straight to the ACK. Proposed fixes, untested: commit a real write before ACKing a `published = 1` this request did not write, or run MySQL with the binlog on and `sync_binlog=1`. Results `/var/tmp/m57-cfcheck`.
- **Harness stop-point bug** (explains M56's 2 of 205 non-reproducing fingerprints, and probably M39's one I/O-error outlier): `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` did a 64 KiB `kzalloc` (order 5) that failed under host memory fragmentation (29+ failures in the journal during M56); `console_drain` ignored it, so a failure on the return where the stop marker first appeared made the harness stop one run call late (same verdict, different end state: background MySQL/Kafka writes and timekeeping). Reproduced both outliers bit-for-bit by simulating one dropped read. Fixes: `console_drain`/`console_rebase` retry and then fail loudly (`hvx_stack_verify.c`, merged); the module copies the console straight from `vm->console` with no allocation (`784dee0`; loaded 2026-10-21, srcversion `38E88060EEC5F955DFDFD03`, deploy-metal ALL PASS 25, pinned reproducers unchanged). New diagnostics: `HVX_EXPLORE_DUMP`, `HVX_EXPLORE_RETLOG`, `hvx/explore/statediff.py`. Follow-up (2026-10-21): 14 other harnesses now exit with an error on a failed `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` instead of ignoring it. The two older gaps were re-checked: neither was this cause; the Aug 2026 restore→halt RAM difference no longer reproduces (22 + 8 identical runs; the spread3 guest now needs `tsc=reliable`), and the M34 MySQL durable-disk outlier did not recur in 20 cycles (no allocation failure was logged that day; the first-run-after-reload case is untested).
- **Flush cut** now in the main module (see M56). PCT-style forced preemption on the Mac: see Milestone 57.

### Milestone 59 — DONE (onboarding: configurable guest RAM, liveness properties, Docker/OCI images; 2026-10-21)

All merged; existing manifests build byte-identically, explore.py schedule strings unchanged (16,600), pinned reproducers unchanged.
- **Guest RAM** (`guest.ram_mib`, 256–3072 MiB on Linux, 256–4096 on the Mac; `explore.py --ram-mib`, `hvx_explore --ram-mib` / `HVX_EXPLORE_RAM_MIB`, `hvmx --ram-mib` / `HVM_MEM_MB`): passed to the engine only when not 1024, so default commands are unchanged. Proved at 2048 MiB on both backends (clean + cut twice identical; forcing 1024 fails the example's MemTotal invariant). Example `examples/redis-ram`. Limit: the size is not in the schedule string; a hand-run `hvx_explore one` on a non-default image needs `--ram-mib` (report repro lines and `hydo run` include it).
- **Liveness** (`eventually` properties; `hvx/ASSERTIONS.md`): one record per episode (`ok:true` if satisfied by the deadline), FAIL if any episode misses, NOT EVALUATED if none resolved. Go `hydoassert.Eventually`, shell `hydo_eventually`; `hydo_assert_report` and `explore.py` report them (Liveness table). Manifest invariants `type: eventually` with `within:` (seconds of guest time), `after: heal|start`, `poll:`; anchor `heal` = end of fault injection (after a cut, the recovery boot). Implemented by `hvx/onboard/hydo-liveness.sh`, inserted into `/sbin/init` only for manifests that use it (wraps `step_range`, `start_periodic`, `start_all`, `finish`; the build fails loudly if those or the `# ---- main` marker change). Proof `examples/redis/hydo-live.yaml`: holds on clean/kill/pause/cut, fails deterministically when Redis stays paused past 10 s.
- **Docker/OCI images** (`services[].image:` = a `docker save` tar or OCI layout, or `{x86_64:, arm64:}`; `image_ref:`; `hvx/onboard/hydo_oci.py`): no registry pulls; digests and diff_ids verified; layers flattened with whiteouts; one root per service at `/oci/<service>`, run chrooted with `/proc /sys /dev /tmp /data` bind-mounted; Entrypoint/Cmd/Env/WorkingDir/User from the image config, overridable; `hydo-oci <svc> <cmd>` runs tools inside; owners/modes from the layers (debugfs on x86_64, cpio headers on arm64); architecture mismatch refused. Proof: official `redis:7.2.4` by digest (`examples/redis-oci`, `fetch-image.sh`), builds byte-identical twice, three schedules twice identical on the bare-metal machine (incl. the documented `everysec` loss). arm64 image booted on the Mac (all schedules identical from snapshot ×2 and fresh boot; `everysec` + cut loses `k:1`); owners, modes and setuid/setgid verified in the archive (4,254 entries) and at run time (`examples/redis-oci/hydo-metacheck.yaml`: redis runs as uid 999, setgid `chage` reads `/etc/shadow`); `fetch-image.sh` fixed (picks the skopeo path containing `bin/skopeo`). Fixed later (branch `arm64-dirlinks`): `hydo_build_arm64.py`'s directory walk now archives every symlink to a directory as a symlink (not followed), not only those under `/oci`; no arm64 example image changed (ledger `deb1525d…`, ledger-spin `42a30746…`, redis `10f7858a…`, redis-ram `2dff8109…`, redis-oci `f575448a…`, disks unchanged), because none of their roots has a directory symlink outside `/oci`.

### Milestone 60 — DONE (a replicated system: three-node etcd; a known upstream bug found without being told; 2026-10-22)

`hvx/onboard/examples/etcd/` (`hydo.yaml`, `build.sh [VERSION]` with pinned release-tarball sha256s for 3.6.15 / 3.6.9 / 3.6.8 / 3.5.6 / 3.5.5, `RESULTS.md`). Three members on their own loopback addresses (127.0.0.11/12/13, client/peer ports 2379/2380, 12379/12380, 22379/22380, data `/data/etcd1..3`, each its own user with a per-user source-address route); workload: 30 steps × three concurrent clients doing put + read-back through a rotating member, every 5th step a put followed at once by an online defrag of every member. Invariants: acked puts readable after recovery; each client's revisions never go back (also reported to the console at once); members agree on revision and the last key's `mod_revision`. **Link faults** (new): a service may have `addr:`; `N=a-b@…` (both directions between two services), `N=a-*@…` (isolate one), `O=a-b@…:req|resp:…`; tc u32 on `lo` by source/destination address; `hvx/onboard/hydo-init-links.sh`, spliced into `/sbin/init` only when a manifest uses `addr:`; `manifest_space.py` adds pairs/isolations as targets; arm64 refuses `addr:` for now; existing examples unchanged. All images built with the pinned tools.
**Results** (explore.py `--strategy process --new-faults --timing`, seeds 1–100): 3.6.15, 3.5.6, 3.6.8, 3.6.9: 0 failures; **3.5.5: 19 failures, all reproduced** — 18 members disagree on the revision (e.g. 99/99/98), 1 client revision going back. This is etcd issue 14685 (after an online defrag, a member that crashes replays already-applied entries and its revision diverges), fixed in 3.5.6, which passes the same 100 schedules. Minimal: `disk=cut dseed=4127cc1347fae6ba | run C=30` (`b802065d149ebda2`), `disk=none dseed=890abaea1c4d92d3 | run K=etcd1@10+1 P=etcd3@26+1 T=2:-30` (`20ec47337eaf03f8`). Hand-written check (kill each member after each defrag, 15 schedules): 3.5.5 5 violations, 3.5.6 0. Caveat: the workload was tuned once (a put right before the defrag, so a kill at the next step lands in the window) — a fair "defrag while writes flow" pattern but tuned toward this bug. 3.6.8 vs 3.6.9 (stale reads with a paused leader): no difference found — the process strategy pauses only between steps, never mid-request; needs timed pauses aimed at the leader. Results `/var/tmp/m59-etcd` on the bare-metal machine.

### Milestone 61 — DONE (re-baseline on the pinned toolchain; 2026-10-22)

From 2026-10-17 the build machine lacked the pinned static musl gcc, so images carried a stand-in `hvx_seedrng` (`01adf1bd…`) nobody can rebuild. The pinned tools were restored (`hvx/BUILD-PINS.md`: exact store paths re-fetched or rebuilt from the bare-metal machine's nixpkgs, GC roots in `~/hydo-gcroots` on the build machine; the main build script's outputs byte-identical to August; pinned `hvx_seedrng` = `c2a440c5…`). The user chose to re-baseline: **33 images rebuilt from their original commits with the pinned tools** (`hvx/REBASELINE.md`: inventory by extracting and hashing `hvx_seedrng` from every image; new images `~/hydo/build/<name>-pin-disk.img` on the bare-metal machine, old ones kept; in all 33 the only file difference is `/bin/hvx_seedrng`). 204 schedule/image pairs re-checked, 359 runs, every pair twice identical. Verdicts unchanged except: (1) the M58 flush-cut points moved a few FLUSHes (explore-nf: minimal now `cf=209`, `8666c33298537074`; bg-fixed: `cf=242` names order 23, `368731d6ef0e33d6`) — the bug is unchanged; (2) the Redis example's `everysec` cut loses `k:2` instead of `k:3`. Each change was confirmed to come from the new image (the old image still gives its recorded value today).
