Listed only: `explore-cc0/1/cc` (M50; results depend on order-service code; rebuild from `029e4b3`/`b9233d5`), `explore-dx` (undocumented diagnostic). **Use the `-pin` images and the values in REBASELINE.md for new comparisons;** older milestone fingerprints stay valid for the original images.

### Milestone 62 — DONE (five harder benchmark defects d8–d12; an explorer gap; 2026-10-22)

`hvx/bench/` (README "M59" sections, `build-bench-m59.sh`, `patches-m59/`, `base/main-m59-control.go`). New control = the current order service plus a front-door fix for the M58 bug (a request that only read `published = 1` commits a real write before ACKing); note this is the bench control only — the stack itself gets the MySQL-config fix (binlog on, `sync_binlog=1`, the other session). Images `explore-bench-m59-*-pin-disk.img` (pinned toolchain). Defects: d8 no per-order lock (`Q=2d`), d9 Redis connection unlocked (`Q=4`, front door panics; `run.py`/`confirm.py` now count a front-door `panic`/`fatal error` as a violation for d8–d12 and their control), d10 "send started" kept in memory (`X=front@12~20`), d11 the M58 bug (`disk=cut dseed=0 cf=238`), d12 shared Kafka key buffer (`Q=2`). Each trigger fails twice identically; the control passes all. **Results** (3 reps × ≤60 runs, `--concurrency`): random, swarm, process and swarm+BUGGIFY find d8, d9, d12 in 3/3 (median 1–5 runs; swarm+bg 30 on d9); without `--concurrency` d8 and d9 are never found in 180 runs, d12 still is. **d10 and d11: 0/3 for every strategy, by construction — no generator emits `X=` or `cf=`** (they exist only in `--sweep frontkill` and the cutflush sweeps; `P=front` keeps memory, and `C=` freezes all servers so no ACK follows the cut). Being closed by the `draw-x-cf` work. Control sweeps: 40/40 frontkill points OK (d10 fails 26/40); 20 of 118 flush-cut points OK (d11 fails at cf=238, 279).

### Milestone 63 — DONE (MySQL binary log on closes the flush-cut finding; Kafka retention deleted acked orders after a clock jump, fixed; 2026-10-22)

Fixes the Milestone 58 finding (`hvx/cutflush/FINDING.md`) in MySQL's configuration, not in the order service. Branch `stack-binlog`. No module change.

**The fix (`build-stack.sh`, `hvx/explore/stack-explore.sh`).** The stack's MySQL ran with `skip-log-bin`. With the binary log off, InnoDB shows a commit to other connections before its redo fsync finishes, so a retry could ACK an order whose `published = 1` was not yet durable. Now `log-bin=binlog`, `sync_binlog=1` (MySQL's default), `server-id=1`, and `innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit=1` stays for the durable setting. With the binary log on, a commit becomes visible only after the prepare (redo fsync) and the binary-log fsync, and crash recovery commits every transaction in the binary log, so anything visible is durable. The relaxed knob (`hydo_flush=off`, explorer `M=0`/`M=2`) now relaxes both logs: `sync_binlog=0` together with `trx_commit=2`/`0`, MySQL's documented fast setting. `build-mysql.sh` (M34) and `hvx/onboard/examples/stack/hydo.yaml` still use `skip-log-bin`; changing them would move unrelated baselines.

**Images** (pinned tools, no M46 stand-ins; a second build is byte-identical): `explore-binlog-pin-disk.img` `ece9a45c…` (the current order service; its `hydostack` is byte-identical to `explore-bench-bg-fixed`'s, `792aab37…`), `explore-binlog-old-pin-disk.img` `3194162a…` (the M39–M46 order service, `hvx/bench/base/main-0112b48.go`), `stack-binlog-pin-disk.img` `2d908c19…` (default stack).

**Proof:**
- **Flush counts.** Each commit now also syncs the binary log. A 30-order workload makes 585 flushes on the current service (was 317) and 491 on the old one (was 281).
- **Flush cuts** (`cf=k`, every k from 200 to the end, results `/var/tmp/m59-binlog` on the bare-metal machine): the current service passed **386 of 386** (before the fix 13 of 118 failed), and the old service passed **292 of 292** (before 12 of 282 failed). Every cut fired. So the configuration alone closes the gap. These runs used the first build of the images, made with the M46 stand-ins; that build has the identical MySQL configuration and differs only in the `hvx_seedrng` binary.
- **Stack checks** on `stack-binlog-pin`, `hvx_stack_verify mode=all`, twice identical, MILESTONE 35 PASS:
  - SANITY `affcc50d0d504a17`;
  - F1 `4e25670bca3d3fd7`;
  - F2 `07ff052d1e8fcd8b` (`VIOLATION mysql order=9`, Kafka has it: the relaxed window, expected; durable `971dedc7b4745fa6`);
  - F3 `35d12da760ad7e12` / `86c31c3bc468cc25`.
  These supersede the M39 stack baselines.
- **Kill list** (`--sweep frontkill`, 40 schedules) on `explore-binlog-pin`: 0 failures.
- **Pinned reproducers** unchanged: `2403499c9a3f8419`, `f87130453609a5d6`.
- **Broad check** on `explore-binlog-pin` (200 swarm schedules with `--concurrency --new-faults --timing`, 2 workers): 125 failures, all reproduced. 124 are in expected classes (lying disk, I/O errors, relaxed settings). One is not, below.

**Second finding, fixed: Kafka deleted acked orders after a large forward clock jump** (`hvx/cutflush/KAFKA-CLOCK.md`). The broad check found one durable-settings failure on `explore-binlog-pin`: Kafka's log started at the first order placed after the guest's realtime clock was stepped forward, and every earlier, ACKed order was gone. Minimal schedule: `disk=none dseed=e80bd3fbb759c1c1 | run K=redis@3+3 N=kafka@21-25:delay,20ms,reorder,11% P=kafka@16+1 T=21:15768000 Q=2d` (`770096791c66765f`, twice).
- **Cause, confirmed with Kafka's log at INFO.** The producer stamps records with the guest's wall clock. Two default settings then act together:
  - after a step of more than 7 days, the next record is over `log.roll.ms` (7 days) past the open segment's first record, so Kafka rolls the segment;
  - the next retention check (first after about 30 s, then every 5 minutes) deletes the closed segment, because its newest record is older than `retention.ms` (7 days). The broker logs "Deleting segment LogSegment(baseOffset=0 …) due to retention time 604800000ms breach" and "Incremented log start offset to 21".
- **The odd threshold.** 6.99 days passes and 7.002 days fails. Above that it is a race between the first retention check and the oracle's read. That is why 90 days passed and 120 failed on one image, while on another image 8 days failed. With a 10-second wait before the oracle, every jump of 7.002 days or more fails.
- **A real deployment would hit it:** any broker whose clock steps forward by more than the retention, such as a machine that boots with its clock far behind and is then corrected by its time service, or a VM restored from an old snapshot. Kafka works as designed; keeping orders in a topic with 7-day retention was the stack's mistake.
- **Fix (`build-stack.sh`):** `log.retention.ms=-1`. On the fixed image, all nine jump sizes up to 365 days pass, twice each. The broad check (seeds 1–60) gave 37 failures, all reproduced, all in expected kinds, and all 22 durable-settings runs passed. New diagnostic switches `STACK_KAFKA_DIAG`, `STACK_ORACLE_WAIT` and `STACK_KAFKA_EXTRA` change nothing when unset.
- **Same exposure elsewhere, left unchanged** because fixing it moves unrelated baselines: the single-Kafka image (`build-linux.sh` §2l) and `hvx/onboard/examples/stack/hydo.yaml`.

**Current stack baselines** (both fixes; pinned tools). Images: `explore-binlog-ret-disk.img` `2d748bd1…` (byte-identical to the investigation's fixed image) and `stack-binlog-ret-disk.img` `b7eb93ee…`. `hvx_stack_verify mode=all`, twice identical, MILESTONE 35 PASS:
- SANITY `ceee1ce465e5424b`;
- F1 `3ddc9bd9aa95cf99`;
- F2 `00af090e634fefb5` (VIOLATION mysql order=9, the relaxed window; durable `df945fb47a53e280`);
- F3 `88fa2915c90b452f` / `e9f60d6301f75013`.

The kill list (40 schedules) gives 0 failures. These supersede the `*-binlog-pin` values above and the M39 stack values. The pinned explorer reproducers (`2403499c9a3f8419`, `f87130453609a5d6`) use older images and are unchanged.

### Milestone 65 — DONE (a rare fingerprint gap was the firmware: SMIs counted on the branch clock; fixed; 2026-10-22)

The gap seen before ("a clean schedule of one example gives a different fingerprint in 1 of 5–10 runs") was found and fixed. A side branch, commits `c370a02` (module) and `f9135bc` (tools), merged as `009ada7`.
- **Cause.** A System Management Interrupt (SMI) runs the firmware's handler with the guest's `IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL` still loaded, and the PMU branch clock (M33) kept counting the handler's branches: inside the guest `IA32_DEBUGCTL.FREEZE_WHILE_SMM` was 0, because hvx did not use "load debug controls" and every VM exit clears DEBUGCTL. One SMI adds about 225,000–285,000 branches, so the next tick lands early at a host-chosen point. On this box reading one EFI variable causes 4 SMIs; fwupd does that when its daemon starts and during `fwupd-refresh` (every 1–2 hours).
- **Evidence.** Without SMIs: about 67 serve runs and 1 fresh boot all `fe24b9342a961c9f`, every return identical. The only two unexplained outliers of that schedule in the journal since 2026-10-20 overlap an fwupd start (06:38) and an `fwupd-refresh` (07:05). 4 injected SMIs changed exactly the hit runs; the first differing return shows +905,573 branches and one extra tick, every earlier return identical. Counter test `hvx/tdet/smitest.c` (a loop of exactly 400,000,000,000 branches): 356 SMIs gave 101,281,973 too many on the old module, exactly 400,000,000,000 on the fixed one.
- **Fix (`hvx/hvx.c`).** At load hvx checks `PERF_CAPABILITIES` bit 12 (freeze in SMM) and the "load debug controls" entry control; if both, every PMU-clock entry loads `GUEST_IA32_DEBUGCTL = FREEZE_WHILE_SMM` (and DR7 from the register file, 0x400, which is what every exit leaves anyway). The load message ends "freeze in SMM yes/no". VMs without the PMU clock are unchanged.
- **Verified** on a side module `/dev/hvxtd`: 50 of 50 serve runs `fe24b9342a961c9f`, runs with injected SMIs identical, side-metal ALL PASS (25 checks), pinned reproducers unchanged. **Loaded into the main `hvx.ko`** together with M66 (srcversion `C907B1D09F5370F6CC93B45`, dmesg "freeze in SMM yes"; see M66).
- **Confirmed on the main module** (same day, after the reload): 10 serve runs all `fe24b9342a961c9f` (369 return lines identical), and 6 runs with 4 injected SMIs in runs 2, 4 and 6 all identical; results `/var/tmp/m65-tdet/mainA`, `mainB`.
- **Ruled out** (that schedule each time): random host-timed exits plus forced re-entries (module knobs `chaos_ptimer`, `chaos_preempt`, off by default), forced CPU migrations, host memory and CPU pressure, RDRAND/RDSEED (knob `probe_rand`: the guest runs neither).
- **Tools:** `HVX_EXPLORE_RETLOG` also prints the host SMI count; `hvx/tdet/` has `retsplit.py` (first differing return), `smirun.sh` (SMI reproducer), `smicount.py`, pressure/migration helpers, `build-hvxtd.sh`.
- **Open:** fwupd still runs on the bare-metal machine (harmless to the fixed module); older single outliers (M34 MySQL, M39 I/O-error) were not checked against fwupd times.

### Milestone 66 — DONE (several virtual CPUs, stage 1: exact branch-counted turns on hand-written guests; 2026-10-22)

(Numbered 66 because the System Management Mode clock work on main is "M65".) Stage 1 of `hvx/SMP_PLAN.md`; full write-up `hvx/SMP_STAGE1.md`. Built by an agent on a side-by-side module (`hvx/smp/build-hvxsmp.sh`, `/dev/hvxsmp`, box tree `~/hydo-smp`); the main `hvx.ko` was not touched during development.
- **Opt-in:** `HVX_VM_SMP_SCHED` (flags bit 5) with `n_vcpus > 1`. It needs the PMU clock: CREATE_VM returns EINVAL without it.
- **In-kernel round-robin:** one `HVX_RUN`/`HVX_RUNC` runs every vCPU in turns of exactly Q guest conditional branches, or seeded lengths in [1, Q]. The PMU landing target is min(next tick, turn end). A turn ends only where there is no STI/MOV-SS shadow and no half-delivered event. THROTTLE and max_insns stops also wait for such a point, and the THROTTLE count is scheduler state, so stops never depend on where an ioctl returned.
- **One VM clock:** TSC, instruction clock and the tick grid are shared; each vCPU's own branches are counted separately.
- **Halted and spinning vCPUs:** HLT ends the turn, and the vCPU is skipped until an interrupt can be delivered to it. The clock jumps only when every vCPU is halted. K consecutive PAUSE exits end a turn; hardware pause-loop exiting is not used.
- **State:** `HVX_GET_SCHED`/`HVX_SET_SCHED` (ioctls 53/54) carry the scheduler state, the shared clock and per-vCPU cr2, halted and re-injection state; `struct hvx_snapshot` is unchanged. Only vCPU 0 claims the boot-CPU bit. The vCPU index enters the tick-jitter and inserted-tick hashes as 0 for vCPU 0.
- **Fixes along the way (scheduler mode only):** an IPI to a vCPU that was not running was stamped with that vCPU's stale clock; and a stale STI shadow after a stepped-over instruction blocked interrupts. The one-vCPU path keeps the stale-shadow behaviour, because fixing it there would move existing fingerprints. The VM structure is now allocated with `kvzalloc`, because opening the device failed with ENOMEM on a fragmented host.
- **Tests:**
  - `hvx_sched_pmu_test`: per-vCPU branch counts exact (150,000,000 + 50,000,017), 0 overshoots, identical in 3 runs; one tick grid; a snapshot at turn 2,693 of 5,386 gives `73a465516c4e0094` for the straight, continued and restored runs.
  - `hvx_sched_sweep_test`: turn lengths 1..2000 replay and branch-from-snapshot identical at all 894 turn boundaries and at 189 mid-turn stops.
  - `hvx_sched_race_test`: a race in plain computation, with the tick period made huge so no tick falls in the window. 21 of 40 (turn length, seed) cells lose sales; every cell is identical when run twice; grid hash `8cb7c61a2393f44e`. Turns alone hit a window that timer jitter cannot reach.
- **Gate:** `race/side-metal.sh` against the side module: ALL PASS (25 checks), every existing fingerprint unchanged. The same three tests and the same 25 checks passed again on the module merged with main (with the M65 SMM clock freeze), with identical numbers.
- **Loaded into the main `hvx.ko`** on 2026-10-22 (with the M65 SMM clock freeze; srcversion `C907B1D09F5370F6CC93B45`, dmesg "freeze in SMM yes"): `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS (25 checks), every fingerprint unchanged; pinned reproducers `2403499c9a3f8419` / `f87130453609a5d6` unchanged.
- **Not yet:** stage 2 (real Linux with several CPUs: AP start-up by INIT/SIPI, MP table and CPUID topology, device interrupts routed by destination) and stage 3 (explorer tokens for turn lengths). The several-CPU tests are not in `deploy-metal.sh`.

### Milestone 67 — DONE (strategies draw timed kills and k-th-flush power cuts; d10 and d11 found; 2026-10-22)

Closes the M62 gap. Branch `draw-x-cf` (commits `397cef1`, `219dda9`), merged as `59f5546`; only `hvx/explore/` changed (`explore.py`, new `more_faults_ident.py`, `bench_more_faults.py`).
- **`explore.py --more-faults`:** `add_more_faults()` on its own random stream (`MF_SALT`), applied after the generator and the new faults, so it combines with random, swarm, swarm-pos and process. `X=<svc>@<order>~<ms>` (order 0–29, 0–250 ms, never the `K=` service; random 30%, swarm 50%, process 40%) and `cf=<k>` (only with an honest disk; turns it into `cut` and drops `C=`; random 40%, swarm 50%, process 60%). `--cf-range auto` (default) first runs one `disk=cut dseed=0 cfn` schedule and draws k from 1..N flushes (bench control 309, d10 266, d11 312, explore-binlog-pin 591, explore-binlog-ret 596); `--cf-range lo-hi` by hand. Rules: no `cf=` with `K=` (a store restarted after the cut waits on the dead disk until the 900 s timeout), and with `cf=` only the order service gets `X=`. The shrinker drops `cf=` or halves k.
- **Identity:** without the option, 76,800 schedule strings identical (4 generators × 64 combinations of the other streams; classes under both knob rules; shrink candidates) — re-checked after the merge; `manifest_space_ident.py` 16,600 identical.
- **Benchmark** (3 reps × ≤60 runs, `-pin` images, `bench_more_faults.py` around unchanged `run.py`): d10 process+xcf 3/3 (14, 1, 10; the run-1 hit had a different verdict, so 2/3 by signature), random 2/3, swarm 1/3, swarm+BUGGIFY 1/3; d11 process 1/3, random 1/3, swarm 2/3, swarm+BUGGIFY 0/3. Every detection reproduced and passed on the control. Most d10 hits used `X=front`; three of four d11 hits combined `cf=` with `X=front`. Cost on d0–d9, d12 (process): all 33 cells still detect; d0 faster; one slowdown (d4 rep 1, 13 → 44). The swarm+BUGGIFY cell overlapped the M65 SMI injections (its one hit reproduced).
- **Current order service** (`explore-binlog-ret-disk.img`, process `--more-faults --durable-only`, seeds 1–100): 0 violations; 5 runs ended `HYDO-ORACLE: ERROR front door restart` — `cf=` with `X=front` after the cut, the order service cannot restart on the dead disk (harness limit; about 40% of such combined runs). The one candidate found during the runs was the M63 Kafka retention bug (fails on the bench control and `explore-binlog-pin`, OK on `explore-binlog-ret`). Results `/var/tmp/m62-xcf/` on the bare-metal machine.
- **Harness fix (branch `cut-kill-recovery`, commit `941f9f3`, merged).** The restart failed because MySQL's sessions hang on the dead data disk (`Error 1040: Too many connections`). Now, when the order service is dead at the end of the workload and cannot restart, the guest (`hvx/explore/stack-explore.sh`) leaves the un-ACKed orders un-ACKed, skips the retry and ends the workload; a `cf=` schedule then gets the usual recovery boot and oracle. Without a cut the old `ERROR front door restart` stays. New current explorer image `explore-binlog-ret-xr-disk.img` (`b7975f6b…`, two builds identical; only `/etc/stack-explore.sh` differs from `explore-binlog-ret`; in `~/hydo/build` on the bare-metal machine). The 5 former ERROR schedules give OK, each twice identical (seed 8 `faf8f747263f1d58`, 12 `1cc350d1726fed76`, 31 `7b168aff88fb1388`, 37 `2fe17bc3bca6b36d`, 83 `e8defa38ced21b02`); process `--more-faults --durable-only` seeds 1–100: 100 OK, 0 errors, 0 violations. The fix cannot raise d11 detection (d11 needs a running order service after the cut); a d11 re-check on rebuilt bench images (`explore-bench-m59-{control,d11-ack-unflushed-flag}-pin-xr-disk.img`) gave process 0/3, swarm 1/3 — the d11 window is sensitive to small timing shifts, and one process repetition was stopped by the M63 Kafka retention bug, which the m59 bench images still carry. Results `/var/tmp/m67-cfx`. Do not merge branch `cut-kill-recovery-bench` (built on pre-binlog `57864c6`).

### Milestone 68 — DONE (opt-in guest AVX/AVX2, x86-64-v3; verified on a side module; 2026-10-22)

Branch `guest-avx` (worktree `../hydo-avx`; box tree `~/hydo-avx`, results `/var/tmp/m68-avx`). Built and tested only on the side module `hvxavx.ko` (`/dev/hvxavx`, `hvx/avx/build-hvxavx.sh [load|unload]`); the main `hvx.ko` was not touched.
- **Flag `HVX_VM_X86_64_V3` (flags bit 7; bit 6 is reserved for `HVX_VM_SMP_BOOT`).** Implies v2. CPUID adds XSAVE, OSXSAVE (read from the guest's CR4 at each CPUID), AVX, FMA, F16C, MOVBE (leaf 1), BMI1/AVX2/BMI2 (leaf 7.0), LZCNT (0x80000001) and leaf 0xD for x87|SSE|AVX only (mask 0x7, standard 832-byte area, sub-leaf 1 = 0: no XSAVEOPT/XSAVEC/XSAVES). `HVX_CREATE_VM` returns ENODEV unless the host has AVX2 and XCR0 includes 0x7 (this box: host XCR0 = 0x1f, so XCR0 is switched on every entry and exit).
- **XSETBV** (exit 55) is emulated per vCPU (`hvx_xsetbv`): #GP(0) at CPL > 0, ECX != 0, bit 0 clear, AVX without SSE, or any bit outside 0x7, injected through the re-injection slot; enabling AVX initialises the upper halves (VZEROUPPER in the host). Guest XCR0 is loaded just before VMLAUNCH/VMRESUME and the host's restored just after, with interrupts off. Guest CR4.OSXSAVE is guest-owned (no CR4 mask change).
- **Extended state per vCPU in `fx_page`** (standard-format XSAVE area): XRSTOR after `kernel_fpu_begin`, plain XSAVE at the end of each run with RFBM = guest XCR0 | x87 | SSE. A component XSAVE left in its init state (XSTATE_BV bit 0) gets its init values written, and XSTATE_BV is kept, so the bytes are a pure function of the guest and the next XRSTOR restores the same init tracking. The area is sanitised (XSTATE_BV & 7, header bytes 8..63 zero, MXCSR & 0xffff) before every XRSTOR, since a #GP there is in the host.
- **Snapshot:** `struct hvx_snapshot` gains `xcr0` and `xsave_ext[320]` (bytes 512..831 of the area) at the end; zero for other VMs. The harnesses I touched (`hvx_stack_verify.c`/`hvx_explore`, `hvx_boot_verify.c`) hash them only when `xcr0 != 0`.
- **Wiring:** env `HVX_CPU_V3` (hvx_stack_verify, hvx_boot_verify, hvx_linux), `hvx_explore --cpu v2|v3`, manifest `guest.cpu: v3` (`hydo-build` validates, x86_64 only; nothing else in the image changes), `explore.py --cpu` (default: the manifest's value; adds `--cpu v3` to the engine and to the report's repro line). Generated schedule strings are unchanged against main (3,000 seeds each for the default space and the redis/stack/etcd/miniflux manifests). Fix on the way: `manifest_space.py` gave the generator an empty service list for a manifest without services (division by zero); it now binds a placeholder that `restrict()` drops. `explore.py` on the avx2 image (8 random schedules, `--timing`): all OK, summary `"cpu": "v3"`.
- **Evidence:** `hvx_avx_test` (new, in `deploy-metal.sh`): CPUID values, XGETBV 1 -> 7, five invalid XSETBVs each #GP, YMM0-15 across an HVX_RUN boundary, FMA/F16C/BMI1/BMI2/LZCNT/MOVBE results equal the host's, AVX off/on zeroes upper halves, snapshot restored into a fresh clobbered VM equals the straight run; `3bb428a134ce542a` in every pass; flag off: no XSAVE/AVX in CPUID and XSETBV still exits to userspace. `side-metal.sh` on `/dev/hvxavx`: ALL PASS (25 checks), every fingerprint unchanged; pinned reproducers `2403499c9a3f8419` / `f87130453609a5d6` unchanged.
- **Real Linux** (`examples/avx2`, image `explore-ob-avx2-disk.img` `68267cf4…`, two builds identical): the guest kernel prints "x86/fpu: Enabled xstate features 0x7, context size is 832 bytes, using 'standard' format", `/proc/cpuinfo` lists avx avx2 fma f16c bmi1 bmi2 movbe abm xsave, glibc 2.39 reports "x86-64-v3 (supported)"; a static four-thread AVX2+FMA program checks its vector results against scalar code in 8 steps. Two runs from the snapshot and a fresh boot: `093a9e05ee7b925d`; branches mid-workload (ticks 238 and 244, marker `step=3`) restored into fresh VMs equal their base runs (`8f7771e4fb927035`); tick-jitter seeds 5 and 6 change the thread order and each replays (`2c909daa451f54b2`, `e6cb76f2db5be9b9`); without v3 the program finds no AVX2 and the invariant fails.
- **Official Restate image** (`examples/restate-oci`, `restatedev/restate:1.7.13` linux/amd64 `sha256:d25e52b5…`, built with target-cpu=broadwell): with v3 the server becomes ready and answers its health and admin endpoints: twice from the snapshot and a fresh boot `425161b89fc10878` (first image); on the final image (`2842986a…`, invariant = answers at the end) `disk=none | run` gives `6b072daee13ce9bc` and `K=restate@2+1` (killed before step 2, restarted, 5 of 6 steps answered) gives `9bb12cc5166f7425` twice from the snapshot and from a fresh boot. Without v3: `traps: restate-server[96] trap invalid opcode` (the M64 failure).
- **Merged with smp-join (M69) on `guest-avx`.** XCR0 and the XSAVE area are per vCPU and the scheduler calls the run loop once per turn, so each vCPU's state is loaded and saved around its own turns; a STARTUP IPI now also resets the XSAVE header and YMM upper halves (XCR0 is kept, as INIT keeps it). `hvx_explore`/`hvx_stack_verify` copy whole snapshot structs per vCPU and hash through `fnv_snapshot`, so the new fields are saved, restored and hashed for every vCPU (only when `xcr0 != 0`). Two test files hashed the raw snapshot struct (`hvx_sched_util.h`, `hvx_smp_boot_verify.c`), so the appended bytes changed their hashes although they are zero; they now hash up to `xcr0` unless `xcr0 != 0`, which gives back the M69 numbers. `hvx_smp_boot_verify` takes `HVX_CPU_V3`. Checks on the merged side module: `side-metal.sh` ALL PASS (26 checks, `hvx_avx_test` added); `hvx_sched_pmu_test` (snapshot `73a465516c4e0094`), `hvx_sched_race_test` (grid `8cb7c61a2393f44e`), `hvx_sipi_test` (`eef5c1ebd4df2e7d`, sweep `c8d015ab3b57252d`), `hvx_sched_sweep_test 2000` (0 mismatches, sweep `39971eed9c2f35fe`, the same as main's module source run as a side module) and the 2-vCPU Linux boot `6584c954c2839491` all PASS; pinned reproducers unchanged; avx2 example plain `093a9e05ee7b925d` and branch `8f7771e4fb927035` unchanged. Real Linux on 2 vCPUs with v3 ("Brought up 1 node, 2 CPUs", xstate 0x7): `hvx_smp_boot_verify` two boots + both branches `135dc7392e191782`; the avx2 example with `hvx_explore --vcpus 2 --cpu v3`: twice from the snapshot and a fresh boot `b4ffb84288370ed3`.
- **Not done / open:** not loaded into the main module; no AVX-512/XSAVEOPT/XSAVEC/XSAVES/PKRU; the volatile pending #GP of an invalid XSETBV is not in the snapshot (like any pending re-injection); with the flag off a misbehaving guest that sets CR4.OSXSAVE anyway still reads the host's XCR0 with XGETBV (unchanged behaviour).
- **Loaded into the main `hvx.ko`** on 2026-10-22 together with M69 (srcversion `CA5BF8BF2A0D66B312A68EF`, "freeze in SMM yes"): `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS (31 checks) — the earlier 25 unchanged, `hvx_avx_test` `3bb428a134ce542a`, and the several-vCPU tests now in the script (`hvx_sched_pmu_test`, `hvx_sched_race_test` `8cb7c61a2393f44e`, `hvx_sipi_test` `eef5c1ebd4df2e7d`/`c8d015ab3b57252d`, `hvx_sched_sweep_test 2000` `39971eed9c2f35fe`, 2-vCPU boot `6584c954c2839491`). The snapshot struct grew, so every userspace binary must be rebuilt against the new `hvx_abi.h`.

### Milestone 69 — DONE (several virtual CPUs, stages 2 and 3: real Linux on 2–4 vCPUs, the four-service stack and the explorer on 2 vCPUs; 2026-10-22)

(Numbered 69: 67 is `--more-faults`, 68 is the other session's guest AVX work.) Built by two agents in parallel, joined on branch `smp-join`. Write-ups `hvx/SMP_STAGE2.md` and `hvx/explore/TURNS.md`. Not yet in the main `hvx.ko`: all box work used the side module `/dev/hvxsmp2` (`hvx/smp2/build-hvxsmp2.sh`, box tree `~/hydo-smp2`).

**Stage 2, module and Linux (`hvx.c`, `hvx_abi.h`, `hvx_boot.h`).** Opt-in flag `HVX_VM_SMP_BOOT` (bit 6), which needs `HVX_VM_SMP_SCHED` and more than one vCPU.
- vCPUs 1.. start waiting for a startup signal (`halted == 2`, no ABI struct changed size). INIT resets the target's LAPIC and makes it wait again; STARTUP with vector V starts a waiting vCPU in real mode at V<<8:0, and a second STARTUP is ignored. This needs the unrestricted-guest control, CR0.PE/PG no longer forced, and the 64-bit entry control following the guest's EFER.LMA.
- MP table with one entry per CPU (`build_mptable_n`, `load_bzimage_n`); one-CPU callers write the same bytes. CPUID leaf 1 and 0xB report the vCPUs as cores of one package.
- Device interrupts routed by the IOAPIC entry or MSI-X address destination; seen: COM1 on vCPU 1, the virtio-blk queue on vCPU 3.
- Real Linux 6.6.35 boots with 2, 3 and 4 vCPUs ("smp: Brought up 1 node, N CPUs"), and two boots plus every branch-from-snapshot are identical (`hvx/hvx_smp_boot_verify.c`): 2 vCPUs `6584c954c2839491`, 3 `e1a2bb0dd744a9ab`, 4 `1dfafae6887e3a01`, 0.85–0.87 s per boot against 0.75 s for one vCPU. Also identical with `tsc=reliable`, quantum 65,536, seeded turns, disk boots, branches at 10 points inside the second CPU's start-up, and 12 boots under host load. `hvx_sipi_test` (hand-written): turn lengths 1..300, replay plus 44 branches identical.
- Gate on the side module: `side-metal.sh` ALL PASS (25 checks), every fingerprint unchanged; the three stage-1 tests give their recorded numbers.

**Stage 3, explorer (`hvx_explore.c`, `hvx_stack_verify.c`, `explore.py`, `hvm_backend.py`).**
- `hvx_explore --vcpus N` / `HVX_EXPLORE_VCPUS`, and `HVX_VCPUS` for `hvx_stack_verify`: the VM gets N vCPUs, `SMP_SCHED` and `SMP_BOOT`. Linux starts the others, the MP table lists N CPUs, and 4 consecutive PAUSEs end a turn (`HVX_PAUSE_K`). Every snapshot and restore covers every vCPU plus the scheduler state; fingerprints add vCPUs 1.. and the scheduler state only when N > 1. `HVX_VCPU_PARK=1` keeps vCPUs 1.. halted instead.
- Host tokens `q=<branches>` (turn length) and `qs=<hex>` (seeded lengths in [1, q]), workload only, RESULT `qn=<turns>`; refused on a one-vCPU VM.
- `explore.py --vcpus N`, and `--turns`: half the schedules get turn tokens from their own random stream, mixed as in rr's chaos mode (about 80% long, 10% at most 100, 10% at most 10,000). The shrinker drops them, then makes seeded turns fixed, then lengthens them. `hvm_backend.py` drops them.
- One-vCPU path unchanged: 179,616 generated schedule strings identical to main, serve-mode fingerprints identical to main's binary, pinned reproducers `2403499c9a3f8419` / `f87130453609a5d6` through the joined binary on the main module.

**The four-service stack on 2 vCPUs** (`stack-binlog-ret-disk.img`, joined side module): `hvx_stack_verify mode=all` twice identical, MILESTONE 35 PASS, about 22 s for the sanity run:
- SANITY `05915e49a5506c58` (30/30 consistent, "Brought up 1 node, 2 CPUs");
- F1 `1e4fedf03f81f941`;
- F2 `c5e01886e97b32b8` (VIOLATION mysql order=2, the relaxed window; replay and branch identical; durable `c9b1cfeb274d4e3d`);
- F3 `2c522171088a9da5` / `2a01253d0d5a4eb8`.

**Explorer on 2 vCPUs:** `explore.py --vcpus 2 --turns --concurrency --strategy swarm`, seeds 1–40 on `explore-binlog-ret-disk.img`. 21 schedules had turn tokens. 26 failures, all reproduced, all with a lying disk, I/O errors or a relaxed setting; 0 of 14 durable-settings runs failed; no harness errors.

**Open:**
- Linux marks the TSC unstable with 2 vCPUs, as it already does with one vCPU on the PMU clock; `tsc=reliable` removes the message.
- Each PAUSE exit moves the virtual clock, so guest time depends on the PAUSE setting (4 keeps it small).
- Not done: NMIs between CPUs; logical-destination interrupt routing (falls back to vCPU 0); virtio-net routing looks only at the first deliverable queue; no paravirtual lock help; nothing beyond 4 vCPUs.
- Module wishes in TURNS.md: a per-turn length list, a per-turn short/long mix, a starve mode.
- The several-vCPU tests are not in `deploy-metal.sh`.
- **Loaded into the main `hvx.ko`** together with the guest AVX work (M68), by the other session, 2026-10-22: srcversion `CA5BF8BF2A0D66B312A68EF`, "freeze in SMM yes", `deploy-metal.sh load` ALL PASS (31 checks), which now include the several-vCPU tests (sched_pmu, sched_race `8cb7c61a2393f44e`, sipi, sched_sweep `39971eed9c2f35fe`) and the 2-vCPU Linux boot `6584c954c2839491`. Userspace binaries built against the older `hvx_abi.h` must be rebuilt (the snapshot ioctl numbers changed with M68).

### Roadmap — booting Linux as L2 (phased; from a planning pass over hydo's KVM boot path)

The KVM monitor already boots Linux; hvx must replicate its recipe. Key facts to reuse (from `src/boot.rs`/`src/detlinux.rs`): 64-bit **bzImage** protocol — protected-mode kernel loaded at `0x100000`, **entry `0x100200` directly in 64-bit long mode** (so we DO NOT need real-mode entry or the unrestricted-guest control — a major de-risk), `boot_params`/zero-page @`0x10000` (`RSI`), e820 + cmdline @`0x20000` (`console=ttyS0 earlyprintk=ttyS0 loglevel=8 nokaslr acpi=off`), MP table @`0x9FC00`, initial long-mode GDT/paging (PML4→PDPT→PD with 512×2 MiB identity), CPUID/MSR **synthesized deterministically** (hvx owns CPUID now). Remaining phases, smallest-first:

2. ~~Guest RAM beyond 2 MiB~~ — **DONE (M11)**.
3. ~~Deterministic CPUID + MSR emulation~~ — **DONE (M12)**. Reason 10 → fixed synthetic table (FPU/PAE/APIC/FXSR/SSE/SSE2, LM+NX; no XSAVE/AVX); RDMSR/WRMSR (31/32) → EFER via `GUEST_IA32_EFER`, IA32_TSC via the virtual TSC, curated software store for STAR/LSTAR/CSTAR/SFMASK/KERNEL_GS_BASE/MISC_ENABLE/SYSENTER_*, unknown MSR reads 0 / drops write. Folded into the snapshot+fingerprint.
4. ~~Full privileged-state persistence across runs + `HVX_GET/SET_SREGS`~~ — **DONE (M13)**. Software `struct hvx_sregs` (all CRs, EFER, DR7, 6 segs+LDTR+TR, GDTR/IDTR) seeded on CREATE, loaded in `setup_vmcs`, VMREAD-saved on exit; folded into snapshot+fingerprint; kernel GET/SET_SREGS handlers implemented. Loader notes honored: `fixed_cr()` still ORs the CR0/CR4 FIXED bits; raw `0xffffffff` segment limits; module TR seeded (sel 0x18/base `GPA_TSS`/limit 0x67/ar 0x8b), LDTR forced unusable (ABI `ar` is u16). (The `kernel_fpu_begin/end` guard bundled here is now **DONE (M14)**; the M13 segment-AR-truncation assumption was also corrected in **M14** via `seg_ar()`. Optionally holding VMXON across ioctls, CPU-pinned, for single-step throughput remains a deferred optimization.)
5. ~~Wire up the bzImage loader → first Linux serial output~~ — **DONE (M14)**. Free-run boots the NixOS 6.6.35 bzImage through the decompressor + full early kernel init (version banner, e820, MP-table/RAMDISK parse) to the LAPIC-MMIO wall; four kernel fixes landed (FPU guard, hide CR4.VMXE, reflect FS/GS-base MSRs into the VMCS, `seg_ar()` unusable-segment round-trip). `HVX_GET_CONSOLE` shows the earlyprintk/"Decompressing Linux"/kernel banner. `build-linux.sh` stages the bzImage+initramfs.
6. ~~**LAPIC/IOAPIC** + timer → boot to the initramfs shell~~ — **DONE (M15)**. Not by trapping LAPIC MMIO but by **forcing x2APIC** (LAPIC emulated through the RDMSR/WRMSR trap, MSRs 0x800–0x8FF) + a KVM hypervisor signature (so Linux keeps x2APIC without IR), a minimal IOAPIC/PIC/16550 model, the TSC-deadline timer wired onto M8 injection with an interruptibility gate + LAPIC in-service/EOI, and loading the guest SYSCALL/KERNEL_GS_BASE MSRs into hardware around each run. Reaches the busybox `~ #` prompt (output side). `HVX_CONSOLE_SZ` bumped to 64 KiB. **P6.4 (serial input → interactive shell) — DONE (M18)**: a UART RX ring + `HVX_PUT_INPUT` + COM1 IRQ via `redir[4]` through the M8 path makes the shell interactive (typed/scripted commands run and echo); the transient input ring is kept out of the snapshot, so the M16 fingerprint `1bdf266a0db52bf7` is unchanged.
7. ~~**Determinism harness**~~ — **DONE (M16)**, in FAST free-run (single-step bisection deferred to the PMU milestone below, and moot here — free-run is already fully deterministic, no divergence to bisect). `hvx_boot_verify.c` fingerprints the full end state (console + snapshot + 256 MiB RAM); two fresh boots AND a mid-boot branch are bit-identical (`1bdf266a0db52bf7`), past the KVM RDTSC-calibration wall. Required three determinism fixes: deterministic THROTTLE cadence (retired exits only), deterministic virtual-TSC creep (moved to the retired block so `vm->tsc` is a pure function of `icount`, not perturbed by host interrupts), and the 16550 UART latch added to the snapshot. **Next milestone — PMU fast instruction clock: attempted, BLOCKED by hardware (see the "Milestone 17 — BLOCKED" section above).** The port of `src/pmu.rs`'s `step_insn_fast` (guest-only INST_RETIRED.ANY counter) needs a vPMU in L1; the capability probe found the nested L1 has NO architectural PMU (CPUID.0AH=0, PMU MSRs #GP) because the L0 CPU is **hybrid** (i7-13700HX) and KVM hardcodes guest-PMU-off on hybrid silicon — reloading `kvm` with `enable_pmu=1` succeeds (all rc=0) yet `enable_pmu` stays `N`, so it is NOT a config toggle. The VMX load/save-`PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL` controls ARE present, so the design is directly implementable on a non-hybrid host. On THIS box, stay on the exact MTF clock (M5); whole-boot free-run determinism (M16) does not need the fast clock. Expected walls once on a vPMU-capable host: nested-counting correctness (the M5 MTF cross-check), then LAPIC-timer phase / jitter entropy.

**Risks (nested L1):** guest with IF=1 + long boot takes many external-int exits — the `sti;nop;cli` service window must let L1's host handler run or L1 RCU-stalls (restart via `run-l1.sh`); keep IRQ-off batches bounded (the 65536-exit THROTTLE cap means many re-entries, so Phase 4 state save/restore must be correct first); always surface exit reason+qualification+`guest_phys` for early faults; expect many L1 restarts on triple-faults.

## Key files

- `README.md` — full architecture & usage (authoritative).
- `src/vmm.rs` — determinism engine: clocks, single-step + PMU fast-path (`step_insn_fast`), instruction-counted interrupt injection, snapshot/restore.
- `src/state.rs` — full-state Snapshot + canonical BLAKE3 fingerprint + disk save/load.
- `src/pmu.rs` — guest-only perf counters + overflow/descheduling handling.
- `src/detlinux.rs` — the Linux divergence harness (`boot-verify`).
- `src/boot.rs` / `src/linux.rs` / `src/uart.rs` — the real-Linux boot path.
- `src/verify.rs`, `src/pv.rs`, `src/det.rs`, `src/serial.rs`, `src/memory.rs`, `src/main.rs`.
- `hvx/` — the from-scratch VT-x hypervisor kernel module. `hvx.c` (the module: VMX/EPT + `/dev/hvx` char device + deterministic RDTSC + MTF instruction clock + instruction-clock TSC + in-kernel CPUID/MSR emulation + privileged-state (sregs) persistence + x2APIC/IOAPIC/16550 device model + M18 serial input + M19 deterministic SMP (per-vCPU/shared-VM split, `HVX_SET_VCPU`, instruction-quantum round-robin, cross-vCPU IPIs) + **M20 deterministic virtio-PCI block device: transport-agnostic virtio-blk core (`struct hvx_vblk`, one split virtqueue against a snapshot-able backing image, `virtio_blk_process`), instruction-timed completion via the M8 path, Phase-A PIO doorbell + Phase-B PCI config (0xCF8/0xCFC) + modern virtio-pci MMIO BAR (`hvx_virtio_mmio`) + INTx-through-IOAPIC (`vblk_irq`); `HVX_SET_DISK` also maps the 4th GiB into the guest PT so a hand-written guest can reach MMIO; inert with no disk so the boot is byte-identical; **M21 MSI-X: MSI-X capability/table/PBA in BAR0 + edge LAPIC delivery of the queue vector at the instruction-timed completion (`vblk_msix_irq`), an Intel host bridge at 00:00.0 (`hvx_pci_read_bridge`) so Linux's conf1 sanity check accepts the bus, the HLT handler jumps the clock to a pending disk completion so an idle disk read wakes, and `HVX_DISK_NO_IDMAP` so a real OS's page tables are not poked — real Linux binds `virtio_blk` over MSI-X and reads `/dev/vda`**; **M22 deterministic disk fault injection: a seeded splitmix64 PRNG (`fault_mix`/`fault_roll`) keyed by the per-device request index drives `vblk_fault_decide` in `virtio_blk_process` — delay/error/drop/torn per request + seeded Fisher-Yates completion reorder — `HVX_SET/GET_FAULT`, all fault state folded into the snapshot, inert (byte-identical to M21) when disabled**; **M23 deterministic virtio-net PCI device: a SECOND virtio-PCI function at dev 4 (`struct hvx_vnet`, vendor 0x1af4/device 0x1041) with two virtqueues [receiveq/transmitq], MSI-X, MAC+VERSION_1 (minimal features, NO F_STATUS so the link comes up RUNNING), scripted RX via `HVX_PUT_RX` delivered by MSI-X at an instruction-timed completion (`virtio_net_rx_deliver`/`vnet_msix_irq`), TX captured via `HVX_GET_TX` (`virtio_net_tx_process`), `HVX_NET_ATTACH` [pokes the same `pdpt[3]` A/D-preset idmap as the disk], and an opt-in `HVX_NET_PACED` run-loop RX trickle so real Linux answers a scripted ARP; all virtio-net + RX-FIFO + captured-TX state folded into the snapshot, inert when no net device attached**; **M24 deterministic network fault injection: a seeded PRNG (`net_fault_roll`/`net_reorder_roll`, reusing the M22 `fault_mix`) keyed by direction + per-direction packet index drives `vnet_fault_decide` in `virtio_net_rx_deliver`/`virtio_net_tx_process` — per-packet drop/delay(RX)/corrupt/duplicate + seeded Fisher-Yates used-ring reorder on RX and TX — `HVX_SET/GET_NET_FAULT`, all net-fault state folded into the snapshot, inert (byte-identical to M23) when disabled; the non-paced RX kick now drains the whole batch [`max_consume` param] so the fault engine sees a multi-packet batch, paced/single-frame paths stay one-per-kick**; **M26 flush-aware DROP + bigger disk: `HVX_FAULT_DROP_UNFLUSHED` holds a rolled write in a per-device VOLATILE WRITE CACHE (`struct hvx_vblk.unsync[]` + a lazily-`kvmalloc`'d buffer) that a `VIRTIO_BLK_T_FLUSH` commits (durable) — `virtio_blk_process` stashes/overlays[read-your-writes]/invalidates/commits, `VIRTIO_BLK_F_FLUSH` advertised only when the class is armed, cache cleared on `HVX_SET_DISK`/reset; `HVX_DISK_MAX` 1 MiB→64 MiB. All additive + inert when the class is disabled, so every prior fingerprint holds**; **M27 deterministic idle heartbeat: when the guest idle-HLTs fully tickless (`tsc_deadline==0`, no pending completion) AND the VM set `HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT` (`struct hvx_config.flags`, ex-`_pad`, default off → `vm->idle_hb`), the run-loop advances the virtual clock by `HVX_IDLE_HEARTBEAT_TSC` and injects the LAPIC-timer vector — a synthetic tick that runs Linux's timer ISR + softirqs [loopback delivery] + the scheduler, so a co-located loopback service [redis] blocked in `epoll_wait` does not deadlock. OFF by default so every prior boot — incl. the no-disk baseline that also idles unarmed — is byte-identical**; **M28 Rung-3 scale-up: the backing disk is a PAGE ARRAY (`struct hvx_vblk.disk_pages[]`+`n_disk_pages`, the M11 recipe — `vblk_disk_alloc`/`_free`/`_from_user`/`_to_user`, `disk_write_bytes`, page-split `vblk_disk_rw`) + a zero-copy disk `mmap` window (`hvx_disk_fault`/`hvx_disk_ops` at `vm_pgoff >= HVX_DISK_MMAP_PGOFF`, dispatched by `hvx_mmap`); `MAX_GPAGES` 256 MiB→3 GiB with the EPT PD level FANNED OUT (`ept_pd[]`/`n_ept_pd`, `build_ept` places `ept_pd[k/512][k%512]`) and a ≤3-GiB guard (`HVX_MMIO_HOLE_BASE`, two `BUILD_BUG_ON`s + `-EINVAL`) keeping RAM below the 4th-GiB MMIO hole; all additive + inert, every prior fingerprint holds**; **M31 gated x86-64-v2 CPUID: a new opt-in flag `HVX_VM_X86_64_V2` (`struct hvx_config.flags` bit 1 → `vm->cpu_v2`) makes `hvx_cpuid` OR in MMX + SSE3/SSSE3/CX16/SSE4.1/SSE4.2/POPCNT + LAHF/SAHF so a real glibc-2.38 userland [a distro OpenJDK JRE] loads [it aborts "CPU ISA level is lower than required" otherwise — the M12 CPUID omits MMX]; deterministic integer/SSE only [NO AVX/XSAVE, guest still never XSETBVs]; default OFF so the kernel records the exact M12 flags in RAM → every prior boot fingerprint byte-identical**), `hvx_abi.h` (shared kernel↔userland ioctl ABI; M19 added `n_vcpus`/`HVX_SET_VCPU`; M20 added `HVX_GET/SET_DISK`, the virtio-blk/transport constants, and the device state appended to `struct hvx_snapshot`; **M21 added the MSI-X constants, `HVX_DISK_NO_IDMAP`, and the MSI-X state appended to `struct hvx_snapshot`**; **M22 added the `HVX_FAULT_*` fault-class constants + `GOLDEN`, `struct hvx_fault`, `HVX_SET/GET_FAULT`, and the fault policy/PRNG state appended to `struct hvx_snapshot`**; **M23 added the virtio-net constants (`HVX_VNET_*`, `VIRTIO_NET_F_MAC`), `struct hvx_net_config`/`hvx_rx`/`hvx_tx`, `HVX_NET_ATTACH`/`HVX_PUT_RX`/`HVX_GET_TX`, `HVX_NET_NO_IDMAP`/`HVX_NET_PACED`, and the virtio-net device state appended to `struct hvx_snapshot`**; **M24 added the `HVX_NFAULT_*` net-fault-class constants, `struct hvx_net_fault`, `HVX_SET/GET_NET_FAULT` (ioctls 23/24), and the net-fault policy/PRNG state appended to `struct hvx_snapshot`**; **M26 added `HVX_FAULT_DROP_UNFLUSHED` (bit 5), `VIRTIO_BLK_T_FLUSH`/`VIRTIO_BLK_F_FLUSH`, and bumped `HVX_DISK_MAX` 1 MiB→64 MiB**; **M27 renamed `struct hvx_config._pad`→`flags` and added `HVX_VM_IDLE_HEARTBEAT` (opt-in idle heartbeat; default 0 = byte-identical)**; **M28 raised `HVX_DISK_MAX` 64 MiB→4 GiB, added `HVX_DISK_MMAP_PGOFF` (the disk-mmap window base), and bumped the documented `mem_size` max 256 MiB→3 GiB**; launches with `-cpu host,+vmx,pmu=on` — the vPMU request that is currently host-gated off, see M17) + `cloud-init/`.
- `dev.sh`, `build-guests.sh`, `build-linux.sh` — workflow scripts. **`build-linux.sh` (M21)** now stages TWO initramfs images — `build/initramfs.cpio.gz` (the original busybox shell, no-disk) and `build/initramfs-disk.cpio.gz` (the virtio-blk driver stack + `/init` that reads `/dev/vda`) — plus `build/vblk-disk.img` (a marker disk), and builds them **byte-reproducibly** (fixed mtimes + sorted names + `gzip -n`) so the boot fingerprints are stable and re-verifiable. **M23** adds a THIRD initramfs — `build/initramfs-net.cpio.gz` (the virtio_net driver stack + an `/init` that brings up `eth0` with a static IP 10.0.0.2) — and **PINS the busybox store path** (the shared box's nix store gained newer `busybox-static` builds, and the old `ls | head -1` selection drifted → changed initramfs bytes → changed boot fingerprints; the pin restores `640ece2f900fe5b3`/`36583d533d7c989b`). **M25** builds `build/hydokv` (the flagship SUT) STATICALLY with the pinned musl gcc (via `-B` to its companion musl crt/libc; both store paths pinned with a fallback scan, like busybox — so the binary and thus the initramfs are byte-reproducible) and stages a FOURTH initramfs `build/initramfs-kv.cpio.gz` (the virtio-blk stack + `/bin/hydokv` + an `/init` that runs the workload + oracle) plus a zeroed `build/kv-disk.img`. **M26** stages the flagship-on-SQLite assets (§2g): `build/sqlite3` (a pinned static upstream sqlite3 3.45.3 CLI from `pkgsStatic.sqlite`), a byte-reproducible empty ext2 `build/sql-disk.img` (`mke2fs` run under a pinned `libfaketime` — this e2fsprogs ignores `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, so faketime freezes all superblock/inode times — with a pinned UUID + `hash_seed` + PINNED `mke2fs`/`faketime` store paths), and `build/initramfs-sql.cpio.gz` (virtio-blk + ext2 module stack + sqlite3 + an `/init` that mounts the fs, runs committed `synchronous=FULL` INSERTs, drops the page cache, and an oracle re-reads every committed row → `HYDO-ORACLE` + `hydo-sqlite-marker`). **M27** (§2h) stages the flagship-on-REDIS assets: `build/redis-server` + `build/redis-cli` (a pinned static musl `pkgsStatic.redis` 7.2.4, built `doCheck=false` to skip a sandbox-flaky AOF test), `build/hvx_seedrng` (the deterministic CRNG seeder, static musl like hydokv), a byte-reproducible empty ext2 `build/redis-disk.img` (same pinned `libfaketime`-frozen `mke2fs` recipe as §2g: pinned UUID + `hash_seed` + PINNED `mke2fs`/`faketime` store paths), and `build/initramfs-redis.cpio.gz` (virtio-blk + ext2 stack + redis + seedrng + an `/init` with three phases via `hydo_phase=` on the cmdline: `solo` [workload+oracle, no crash], `workload` [SETs over `127.0.0.1` then `SIGSTOP` redis], `oracle` [AOF-replay + oracle] — brings up `lo`, seeds the CRNG, runs redis with `appendfsync` from `hydo_fsync=`, prints `HYDO-ORACLE` + `hydo-redis-marker`). **M29** (§2i) stages the Rung-3 rootfs assets: `build/rootfs-ext4.img` (a byte-reproducible ext4 READ-ONLY root, busybox + `/sbin/init`, built via `mke2fs -t ext4 -d` in a SIMPLE layout [`-O ^metadata_csum,^64bit,^flex_bg,^resize_inode`] + a pinned-`python3` pass that ZEROES every inode's `i_ctime` — the one field `mke2fs -d` copies from the source file's real `st_ctime`, which `touch`/libfaketime cannot freeze — plus the same pinned UUID/hash_seed/`libfaketime`/non-lazy-init discipline as the ext2 images), `build/rootfs-data.img` (a small reproducible ext2 rw-data disk via `mkext2_reproducible`), `build/rootfs-disk.img` (the two partitions assembled behind a hand-crafted deterministic MBR: p1 ext4 ro-root @ LBA 2048, p2 ext2 rw-data @ LBA 1050624), and `build/initramfs-rootfs.cpio.gz` (the virtio-blk + `crc32c_generic` + ext4/jbd2/ext2 module stack + an `/init` that mounts `/dev/vda1` ext4 `ro,noload`, `mount --move /dev`s into the new root, and `switch_root`s; the real root's `/sbin/init` mounts `/dev/vda2` ext2 rw, runs the committed-record workload + oracle → `HYDO-ORACLE` + `hydo-rootfs-marker`). Two-build sha256 identity: `rootfs-ext4.img`=`6df9f82c…`, `rootfs-disk.img`=`a4aefaf1…`. **M30** (§2j) stages the Rung-4 multi-service system assets: `build/hydoapp` (the static-musl app tier + client, built with the same pinned musl gcc as hydokv/seedrng), a byte-reproducible empty ext2 `build/system-disk.img` (`mkext2_reproducible` with a distinct UUID/hash_seed + a python pass that ZEROES all superblock/inode time fields — see below), and `build/initramfs-system.cpio.gz` (virtio-blk + ext2 stack + redis + hydoapp + seedrng + an `/init` with `solo`/`workload`/`oracle` phases via `hydo_phase=`, `hydo_fsync=` that bring up `lo`, seed the CRNG, start redis + the app tier, and drive the client→app→redis workload/oracle over `127.0.0.1`). §2h (redis) is byte-untouched. The `system-disk.img` time-zeroing pass fixes a latent `libfaketime`-advance jitter that made this image's mke2fs timestamps land on different seconds across builds (the existing images happen to complete inside one second; this one straddled a boundary) — localized to §2j so no existing image moves. Two-build sha256 identity: `hydoapp`=`63850b54…`, `system-disk.img`=`b5b949f6…`, `initramfs-system.cpio.gz`=`dd6ce66e…`. **M31** (§2k) stages the Rung-5 JVM assets: `build/HydoWork.class` (compiled by the pinned `jdk17_headless` javac — a bare `.class`, no timestamp → byte-reproducible), `build/rootfs-jvm-root/` (the ext4 ro-root tree: busybox + `hvx_seedrng` + `/app/HydoWork.class` + the FULL pinned `openjdk-minimal-jre-17` runtime closure [598 MiB] replicated under `/nix/store` so `bin/java`'s absolute ELF-interp + patchelf'd gtk/cups/X `DT_NEEDED` resolve verbatim + `/sbin/init` that seeds the CRNG, mounts the ext2 rw data disk, and runs `java -Xint -XX:+UseSerialGC -XX:-UsePerfData -XX:-UseContainerSupport -Xms64m -Xmx64m … -cp /app HydoWork /data` then the oracle), `build/jvm-ext4.img` (720 MiB ext4 ro-root via the same M29 `mkext4_rootfs_reproducible` + `i_ctime`-zero recipe), `build/jvm-data.img` (16 MiB reproducible ext2 rw-data), `build/jvm-disk.img` (MBR + p1 + p2, 737 MiB, sha256 `3169d614…`), and `build/initramfs-jvm.cpio.gz` (the M29 virtio-blk + ext4 + ext2 `switch_root` stack). Pins: `jre17_minimal` (runtime, staged) + `jdk17_headless` (build-time javac), both with `nix build` fallbacks. The nix-store closure copies are mode-0555, so §2k `chmod -R u+w` before `rm -rf` to stay idempotent. **M32: §2k `/sbin/init` is now JIT-selectable** — a `hydo_jit=<int|c1|c1sync|tiered|tieredsync>` kernel-cmdline token picks the HotSpot compiler flags (default/unknown → `int` = the M31 `-Xint`), so all JIT configs are testable from ONE rebuilt disk via the cmdline; the JIT is proven bit-deterministic under hvx (M32 Part 1). A full rebuild changes ONLY the jvm assets; the pristine M31 `-Xint` disk (`4b8ffacde50f1169`) is preserved as `build/jvm-disk-m31.img`.

### Milestone 70 — DONE (freeze a service at an exact packet of its traffic; 2026-10-23)

Branch `freeze-call` (commits `1398f27`..`9a027c3`), merged as `4832a82`. Userspace and build only.
- **Token** `F=<svc>><peer>@<step>[#<n>][:<match>]:<dur>` (`>` = packets `<svc>` sends to `<peer>`, `<` = packets it receives): from workload step `<step>`, at the n-th such packet (optionally only packets containing `<match>`), stop `<svc>`'s whole process tree for `<dur>` ms of guest time. `#0` fires nothing and prints a compact traffic trace (`hydo-fc-trace:`) to pick n. A static helper (`hvx/onboard/hydo_fc.c`, pinned musl gcc) runs SCHED_FIFO, holds the matched packet in a netfilter queue, stops the tree, waits until every thread is stopped, then releases the packet, so the service cannot run past it. Opt-in: `freeze` in the manifest's `faults:` (plus optional `freeze_match:`); `hydo-build` then stages the helper, a pinned `iptables` (`/nix/store/xnrrq638…-iptables-1.8.10`) and the netfilter modules, and splices `hvx/onboard/hydo-init-freeze.sh` into the init. Other manifests build byte-identically.

### Milestone 71 — DONE (lifting a network fault drains the netem queue; 2026-10-23)

Branch `netem-drain` (commits `0be22e6`, `5b673b4`, `2bea519`), merged. Guest scripts only.
- **Cause:** deleting a netem qdisc in kernel 6.6.35 discards its delay queue (`netem_reset` → `tfifo_reset`); `tc qdisc change` keeps it. `net_off` in `hvx/onboard/hydo-init.sh` (used by `N=`, `O=` and the `addr:` link faults), four heals in `hvx/explore/stack-explore.sh` and the stack's `hydo_net=` mode in `build-stack.sh` deleted at once.
- **Fix:** `net_heal` (in `hydo-init.sh`, a copy in `build-stack.sh`'s init): change the netem to `rate 100gbit corrupt 0%` (no delay or loss; queued packets keep their send times and order), wait in guest time until the queue is empty (bound: delay + 4 × jitter + 500 ms), remove the filters, wait ≤ 50 ms, delete. Console line `queued=<n> drained_ms=<ms> left=<n>`. After a heal new packets wait behind queued ones (order kept, at most one delay).
- **Evidence:** `examples/redis/hydo-netem.yaml` (Redis + invariant `RetransSegs == 0`): `N=redis@3-6:delay,300ms` VIOLATION retrans=2 on main's init (`c568d85e8e4f70ba`, twice) → OK retrans=0 with 2 packets drained (`5b226954f8df4479`); `O=redis@3-6:resp:delay,300ms` → OK `dd440bce5c239ea2`; `N=redis@5-20:delay,300ms` → OK `3ed5964ccdd8e5f4`. etcd 3.6.15 link faults drained 34, 37 and 6 packets with 0 lost. Without network faults, verdict kinds unchanged; Redis `C=12 R=everysec` now loses `k:2` (was `k:4`), stack `disk=cut C=20 M=2` names order 9 (was 0) — timing shift from the longer init, not proven.
- **Images** (`~/hydo/build` on the bare-metal machine, each two builds identical): `explore-binlog-ret-xr-nq-disk.img` `4a6a8493…` (current stack explorer image; vs `-xr` only `/sbin/init` and `/etc/stack-explore.sh` differ; 40 swarm `--new-faults --durable-only` 0 failures, 9 of 21 heals had queued packets, none lost), `explore-ob-redis-netem-nq-disk.img` `c7bc532a…`, `explore-ob-redis-nq-disk.img` `03beff6c…`, `explore-ob-etcd-3.6.15-nq-disk.img` `021d350a…`, and main-init comparison images `explore-ob-{redis-netem,redis,etcd-3.6.15}-main-disk.img`. Results `/var/tmp/nq`.
- **Open:** older onboarding images with network or link faults keep the old behaviour until rebuilt; the default stack image's next build needs its M63 baselines re-measured.

### Milestone 72 — DONE (benchmark images with the Kafka retention fix, `-pin-kr`; 2026-10-23)

The m59 benchmark images (control, d8–d12) rebuilt from `57864c6` with only `log.retention.ms=-1` (M63) and the M67 `stack-explore.sh` change, without MySQL's binary log (which closes d11 by itself); branch `bench-m59-kr` (build recipe only, do not merge). Each built twice byte-identically; only `/etc/kafka/server.properties` and `/etc/stack-explore.sh` differ from `-pin`. Now the default m59 set in `hvx/bench/run.py`; `confirm.py` uses d10 trigger `X=front@9~20` on it (the old `@12~20` passes there). The M63 clock-jump candidate passes on the new control. d10: process 3/3, swarm 2/3; d11: 0/3 for both (M67: 1/3, 2/3) — d11's window is ~3 of ~310 cut points, so 3-repetition cells cannot measure it. Full numbers in `hvx/bench/README.md` ("-pin-kr").

### Milestone 73 — DONE (M70 follow-up: recovery clock; freeze measurement; 2026-10-23)

Branch `freeze-call-2` (from `3762613`). Userspace, build and docs only; no module change.
- **The 7 blind hits of M70 are not the bug M70 reproduced.** All have a power cut after the last step (`C=10`); the acknowledgement was processed only in the recovery boot, whose clock restarted at `guest.clock_start`, so it looked earlier than events the first boot had written. Only the check that compares those times failed; the other checks passed. `disk=cut dseed=0 | run C=10` alone reproduces it (twice `b4b7098cc190070b`); the shrinker reduced seed 12 to `C=10` and seed 20 to `C=10 P=kafka@3+1`. The process strategy still does not find that bug blind.
- **Freeze changes:** `freeze_match:` may be `replies:` / `requests:` lists, and a drawn string uses its own direction; peer `workload` (the workload's client) for a project whose services do not call each other (`examples/redis/hydo-freeze.yaml`). Identity checks (`freeze_call_ident.py`, `manifest_space_ident.py`, `more_faults_ident.py`) pass against main.

### Milestone 74 — DONE, NEGATIVE RESULT (do several vCPUs find races that one vCPU misses? 2026-10-23)

Two experiments on the main `hvx.ko` with several vCPUs (M66, M69), by two agents. Write-ups `hvx/bench/SMP-RESULTS.md` and `hvx/knownbugs/sqlite/README.md` (new section). Tools: `hvx/bench/run.py` and `confirm.py` take `--vcpus N --turns`, and `hvx/knownbugs/sqlite/hunt.py` takes `--vcpus N --turns`, weighted toward short turns. Without the new flags the schedule strings are unchanged (5,760 and 36,000 strings checked). `hvx_explore` gained two read-only per-vCPU statistics prints on stderr: the scheduler counters (`schedstat`) and `HVX_EXPLORE_VCPUSTAT`. Results on the bare-metal machine: `/var/tmp/bench2`, `/var/tmp/sqlsmp`.

**Benchmark race defects** (images `explore-bench-m59-*-pin`; swarm, 3 repetitions of up to 60 runs; run of first detection per repetition):

| defect | 1 vCPU, concurrent clients | 2 vCPUs + turns, concurrent clients | 1 vCPU, no concurrent clients | 2 vCPUs + turns, no concurrent clients |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| d8 (no per-order lock) | 5, 2, 46 | 5, 2, 46 | 0/3 | 0/3 |
| d9 (Redis connection unlocked) | 5, 31, 1 | 5, 31, 8 | 0/3 | 0/3 |
| d12 (shared Kafka key buffer) | 5, 2, 1 | 5, 2, 1 | 23, 19, 1 | 18, 13, 1 |

- **Checks:**
  - All 33 detections re-ran with the same fingerprint, matched their signature, and passed on the control.
  - The second CPU was really used: every 2-vCPU boot printed "Brought up 1 node, 2 CPUs", and vCPU 1 retired 13–733 M branches per run.
  - The identical run numbers have one cause: in those schedules the concurrent clients trigger the defect whatever the vCPU count.
  - d9's run 8 on 2 vCPUs: the run-1 crash did happen, but its restart could not reach Kafka, so it counted as a harness error.
- **Random strategy, 2 vCPUs, concurrent clients:** d8 4, 1, 7; d9 1, 1, 2; d12 1, 1, 2. Random with 2 vCPUs and no concurrent clients was stopped early (d8 not found in 79 runs).
- **Details:**
  - d9 with `Q=2` crashes on 2 vCPUs in 6 of 9 turn settings, but 1 vCPU with tick jitter or inserted ticks crashes it too, in 5 of 10.
  - Some d12 schedules fail only on 2 vCPUs: seed 2013, the same string, fails on 2 and passes on 1. That gives slightly earlier detections, too few repetitions to call it a speed-up.
  - A 2-vCPU run cost about 3× a 1-vCPU run (about 43 s against 15 s, box load varied).
- **Verdict:** d8 and d9 need two requests for the same order at once; extra CPUs cannot create that. d12 is found both ways at similar speed.

**SQLite 3.51.2 WAL-reset bug, blind** (`hvx/knownbugs/sqlite/`):
- **Turn mix.** Each turn end costs about 0.8 ms of host time. On the heaviest workload a run takes 13 s with default turns, 32 s at q=10,000, 216 s at q=1,000, and over 900 s at q≤300. So the mix per schedule is 5% default turns, 15% long, 70% q=1,000–10,000 (half seeded), and 10% q=300–999.
- **Runs:**
  - 2 vCPUs: 144 runs in 3.5 hours, 19.1 M turn switches, 80 M checkpoints, 0 violations.
  - 4 vCPUs with the short mix: 32 runs in 1.8 hours, 0 violations.
  - 4 vCPUs with default turns: 912 runs in 46 minutes, 0 violations.
- **Sanity checks:** "Brought up 2/4 CPUs"; walrace threads seen on every CPU (vCPU 0 retired 19.5 G branches, vCPU 1 28.1 G); runs identical from a fresh boot and from the snapshot (`3173108c39ad8004`, `958429cf955867e6`, `6cac0c27871d3ad9`).
- **Verdict:** not found. Short turns are too expensive for this window at an affordable budget, and the workload rarely produces the trigger; native runs on 4 real cores also missed it in about 5,800 seeds.
- **Possible next steps (not started):** make the workload reset the WAL far more often; make turn ends cheaper, or use short turns only around WAL activity.

**Overall:** on these targets, several vCPUs found nothing that one vCPU with concurrent clients, tick jitter or inserted ticks does not find. They work and are deterministic, but buy no new reach here. Lesson about cost: the benchmark experiment ran 13.5 hours because cells whose outcome was already clear kept running; set a time limit and check halfway.

### Milestone 75 — DONE (the Linux release: one `hydo` command and a self-contained tarball for x86_64 + VT-x; 2026-10-23)

Branch `linux-release` (worktree `../hydo-lrel`, rebased on `d9cefa7`; not merged). Userspace, packaging and docs; no module change. Files: `hvx/dist/` (`hydo`, `install.sh`, `build-release.sh`, `libexec/{hvx-run,faketime,bin/debugfs}`, `assets.sha256`, `README.md` for maintainers, `QUICKSTART-linux.md` shipped as the release's README). Two small changes to existing tools, both inert by default: `explore.py` reads `HYDO_BUILD` / `HYDO_HVX_RUN` / `HYDO_SNAP_DIR` (defaults `~/hydo/build`, `~/bin/hvx-run`, `/var/tmp/hvx-snap`) and, with `HYDO_CLI`, prints a `hydo run` hand-reproduction line; `explore/ptedges.h` compiles without libipt under `-DHYDO_NO_LIBIPT`.
- **The command** (same verbs as the Mac release): `hydo doctor` (Intel + `vmx`, bare metal, a usable non-hybrid PMU or "exit-count clock is used", Python 3.9+, `cc`/`make`/`flock`/`xz`/`sudo`/`modinfo`, password-less sudo, kernel headers, lockdown, module built/loaded/same `srcversion`, `kvm_intel` not loaded, release files, disk space); `hydo module build|load|unload|status [--name N] [--dry-run]` (exclusive `flock` on `~/.hvx-module.lock` = `$HYDO_LOCK`, the bare-metal machine's `hvx-run`/`hvx-reload` convention; `load` removes `hvx`, `kvm_intel`, `kvm` then `insmod`; `--name` builds and loads a side copy with its own device and lock file, never touching `hvx` or KVM; `status` reads lock holders from `/proc/locks` without taking the lock); `hydo build` (imports `hydo-build`); `hydo run [IMG] "<schedule>"` (`hvx_explore one` under the shared lock, engine options from the image's manifest, writes `run.json`); `hydo repro <run dir | explore dir | schedule>` (re-runs from a fresh boot and compares fingerprints); `hydo explore` (`explore.py`, default 20 seeds, 2 workers); `hydo report`.
- **No Nix for users.** The release carries the guest kernel and `initramfs-kafka.cpio.gz` (sha256 `0a9ede35…`, `d074d764…`), busybox 1.37.0, `hvx_seedrng` compiled with the pinned musl gcc (comes out as the pinned `c2a440c5…`), `hydo_fc`, the guest's netem/u32/netfilter modules, the iproute2 + iptables closures as one GNU tar with the store's read-only modes, and e2fsprogs 1.47.0 + libfaketime 0.9.10 with their glibc. `libexec/faketime` runs the Nix-built `mke2fs`/`debugfs` with the bundled loader (`--library-path`, `--preload libfaketime.so.1`). `hydo build` points `hydo-build` at these through its `HYDO_*` variables, a module-local `os` view and a replacement `closure()`; `root.nix:` still needs `nix-store`. `build-release.sh` fetches every asset in one remote session from the build machine (all gc-rooted pins), checks 34 files against `assets.sha256`, and builds a 45 MB tarball offline; `install.sh` checks `SHA256SUMS`, unpacks the store tar, compiles `hvx_explore` (with libipt if present) and the module, and runs doctor. It never loads the module.
- **Image reproducibility across machines (three findings).** With `HYDO_KEEP_OWNERS=1`, the release on the NixOS build machine builds the Redis and Redis-netem images byte-identical to a plain pinned `hydo-build` (root `bcea6b1d…`, netem root `6d768c1c…`, data disks `2d52cd04…`, `6b1dab4d…`). On the Ubuntu bare-metal machine the first builds differed in 8 bytes: the mtimes of 4 inodes were 4 hours apart. Cause: libfaketime reads hydo-build's `FAKE_TS` "2020-09-13 16:26:40" in the local zone, and it equals `FIXED_MTIME` 1600000000 only in the build machine's zone (UTC+4); so even a plain `hydo-build` gives different bytes in another zone. The wrapper sets `TZ=UTC-4`. The build also ignores the machine's `/etc/mke2fs.conf` (`MKE2FS_CONFIG` names no file; Ubuntu has one, NixOS does not), fixes the umask at 022 (Ubuntu's 002 changed directory modes), and sets every root inode's owner to 0:0 with `debugfs` (mke2fs -d records the builder's uid/gid). After that the Ubuntu bare-metal machine (no Nix, UTC, umask 002) and the NixOS build machine build the same bytes for the repository's Redis manifests: root `aec24305…`, netem root `b0fe5081…`.
- **Quickstart on the bare-metal machine** (clean install into `~/hydo-lrel/prefix2`; repeated after the rebase with tarball `373c609a…` in `~/hydo-lrel/prefix3`, same image sha and fingerprints; doctor "all checks passed"; the Redis example with `files/` sources): image `8c828d3e…` (two fresh build directories identical); `disk=none | run` OK acked=20, `c513f10aee733aae` twice; `disk=cut | run C=12 R=everysec` VIOLATION key k:2 lost, `ecef6f8fe6c39ec0` twice; Redis-netem `N=redis@3-6:delay,300ms` OK retrans=0, `e518f6b9fd845ee5` twice.
- **Explore + repro** (earlier install, same code; image built in a directory whose staged root was first made under umask 002, so its sha differs: `05db0de2…`): `hydo explore --seeds 1-20 --strategy swarm` (2 workers, 506 s on a loaded box): 11 failures, all expected classes (io-errors 3, lying-disk 6, unsafe-knob 2), 11/11 reproduced by the explorer, 4 minimal schedules each twice identical; `hydo repro hydo-out/explore` re-ran all 11 failures and 4 minimal schedules from fresh boots: **15 of 15 with the recorded fingerprint**. `hydo repro <run dir>` reproduced the cut run.
- **Module handling** (main module never touched; srcversion stayed `CA5BF8BF2A0D66B312A68EF`): `status` reported the loaded `hvx` as the release's source; `load`/`unload --dry-run` printed `sudo rmmod hvx && sudo insmod …` and `sudo rmmod hvx`; a side module `hvxlrel` built, loaded (`/dev/hvxlrel`), and unloaded; with a shared holder on its lock, `unload` waited 7 s until the holder finished.
- **Not done:** runs use `sudo` (no udev rule for `/dev/hvx`); AMD is not supported; a staged root directory left by an earlier non-release build keeps its old directory mode (the reason for the `05db0de2…` image above); the Mac release (`hvx-mac/dist/build-release.sh`) copies only `hydo-init.sh` of the init extensions, so an arm64 build of a manifest with `eventually` invariants or `guest.clock_after_cut` would fail to find `hydo-liveness.sh` / `hydo-init-clock.sh` (not checked, not fixed).

### Milestone 76 — DONE (the freeze sweep: every packet point of a run; 2026-10-24)

Branch `freeze-sweep` (from `2e0e3fe`, rebased onto `cd5e551`); only `hvx/explore/explore.py`, `hvx/explore/manifest_space.py` (sweep kind `freeze`) and `hvx/onboard/MANIFEST.md` changed. No module or image change; `hvx_explore` compiled from main in `~/hydo-fsw` (identical binary to `~/hydo-fc`'s). Results `/var/tmp/m74-fsw` on the bare-metal machine (`analyze.py` there groups the failing points).
- **`explore.py --sweep freeze`.** A packet stream is (pair, direction, and either every packet with payload or one `freeze_match:` string in its own direction). For each stream and each swept step `s` (plus `s+1` as the boundary) it first runs `F=<pair>@s#0[:match]:1` and reads the guest's "freeze never fired: <N> of 0 matching events after step <s>" line, which hydo-init-freeze.sh counts in the helper's full log, so the console's 600-line trace limit does not matter. Step `s` holds N(s) − N(s+1) packets (+2 margin only where `s+1` is not swept; the last step N(s)), and the sweep runs one schedule `F=<svc><dir><peer>@<s>#<n>[:<match>]:<dur>` per packet. Options `--fc-pairs`, `--fc-dirs`, `--fc-steps`, `--fc-match all,manifest` (default), `--fc-durs` (default 150000), `--fc-margin`, `--sweep-every k`, `--sweep-with "<tokens>"` (one more fixed fault on every trace and point; a power cut is refused because its trace never reaches the count), `--sweep-dry` (trace and write `points.txt`), `--fc-traces` (reuse an earlier run's `fc-traces.jsonl`), and `--repro N` (re-runs per failure; default 2 for this sweep, 1 elsewhere, report key `repro_runs` only when ≠ 1). Point labels go to `sweep-points.jsonl`, so `compare.py sweep` tabulates them.
- **Redis control** (`explore-ob-redis-fc-disk.img`, `F=redis<workload`/`>`, all 20 steps, 3000 ms): 40 trace runs, 40 points, 40 freezes fired, 0 failures (the frozen step's SET times out and is not acknowledged), 186 s.
- **Benchmark d10/d11: not run.** `hvx/explore/stack-explore.sh` has no `F=`; the stack images carry neither `hydo_fc`, the pinned `iptables` nor the netfilter modules. Supporting it means porting `hydo-init-freeze.sh` into `stack-explore.sh` (parse `F=`, map mysql/kafka/redis/front to their ports and pid files, arm before an order, heal at the end, kill the helper at a cut), staging the three items in `build-stack.sh` as `hydo-build` does, and rebuilding the m59 `-pin-kr` control, d10 and d11 images — not small. By mechanism a freeze cannot trigger d11: d11 needs MySQL to keep serving reads while its FLUSHes stall, and a freeze stops the whole MySQL process; only the k-th-flush cut models it. d10 could be reachable as `F=kafka<front@s#n` (a produce held in flight) with `--sweep-with K=front@<s+1>+1`.
- **Not done:** the stack port above; a sweep combined with a power cut; the M73 open items (backward clock steps, the user's own manifest) are unchanged.

### Milestone 77 — DONE (images rebuilt with the netem drain; stack baselines re-measured; 2026-10-23)

Branch `rebaseline-nq`. Documentation only: the images were built from main at `2e0e3fe` (the image recipes are unchanged on main up to `1e708b9`). No module change. Full record: `hvx/REBASELINE.md` section 7.
- **Images** (pinned tools, `hvx_seedrng` `c2a440c5…`, each built twice byte-identical, `~/hydo/build` on the bare-metal machine; against the previous image only `/sbin/init` differs, plus `/etc/hydo/init-links.sh` for etcd): `explore-ob-stack-fix-nq` `ecdcb4fa…`, `explore-ob-stack-old-nq` `58de1559…`, `explore-ob-miniflux-nq` `7b694c9f…`, `explore-ob-etcd-{3.5.5,3.5.6,3.6.8,3.6.9}-nq` `2d4d9301…` / `1df90f16…` / `bf213ea5…` / `fd2e9c58…`, default stack `stack-nq` `161996bb…`.
- **Onboarding examples** (`hvx_explore one`, each schedule twice on the new image and once on the old image the same day; 95 pairs, every pair identical; the 32 old-image fingerprints recorded earlier all reproduced): stack fix/old, miniflux, etcd 3.5.6, 3.6.8, 3.6.9 — no verdict changed. Every lifted fault ended with `left=0`; 14 of 20 heals delivered queued packets (4–41 on etcd link delays).
- **etcd 3.5.5 (issue 14685): six verdicts changed, the bug is still found.** M60's first minimal reproducer `disk=cut dseed=4127cc1347fae6ba | run C=30` still fails (`2bd06b8446559fe9`); the second (`K=etcd1@10+1 P=etcd3@26+1 T=2:-30`, client revision going back) now passes; the hand-written kill check fails 3 of 15 (was 5 of 15). The changed kill schedules have no network fault; the longer init shifts guest timing and with it which kill lands in 14685's window. The M60 explorer campaign (process, seeds 1–100, identical schedule strings) on the new image: 17 failures, all `etcd.members_agree`, all reproduced, shrunk to the same minimal schedule (M60: 19, one of them the revision-going-back symptom); 3.5.6: 0 failures.
- **Default stack** (`stack-nq`; supersedes M63's values, which the old image still reproduces today): `mode=all` twice, MILESTONE 35 PASS: SANITY `2235f9d76a592a07`, F1 `815cc64346e728fc`, F2 `32024d0b245aff3c` (VIOLATION `mysql order=8` MISSING, Kafka has it — was order 9; durable `7323951e90d21b4f`), F3 `96e2e7ddb963ee66` / `e8c16382540eba2e`. The eight M39 scenarios, each two runs + branch identical (relaxed: two runs), all OK 30/30 exactly-once: kill mysql@10 `959e90dd32e720f4`, kafka@10 `e00b67e3c5fb1898`, kafka@20 `1ba55098f467b928`, redis@10 `f235adac001ad733`, net mysql `6a944fa762d93cc7` (2 packets delivered at the heal; first pass 29/30, was 30/30), net redis `e92c9e6b38852983`, net kafka `b6c82b0ca917e0f2`, net kafka MySQL relaxed `349b826362a275ec`. The old image's values for the eight scenarios are in REBASELINE §7.2 (never recorded before).

### Milestone 78 — DONE (etcd 14685's second symptom reproduced again on the `-nq` image; 2026-10-24)

Branch `etcd-14685-rev`. No module, image or tool change; `hvx_explore` built from main `19fb8f6` in `~/hydo-etcd2` on the bare-metal machine. Full record: `hvx/onboard/examples/etcd/RESULTS.md`, last section.
- **Schedule:** `disk=none dseed=1 | run K=etcd1@10+1 P=etcd3@26+1` (etcd1 killed before step 10 and restarted before step 11; etcd3 paused before step 26 and resumed before step 27). On `explore-ob-etcd-3.5.5-nq` it fails `client.revisions_monotonic`: client 2 read revision 83 through etcd1 (step 25), then 82 through etcd2 (step 26), the same details as M60; the final check also finds `members disagree: revisions 101 100 100`. Fingerprint `0470512bb297c87f` in two fresh boots and from the S1 snapshot. On `explore-ob-etcd-3.5.6-nq` it passes (`63e112769072c0c1`, two fresh boots and the snapshot).
- **How found:** the cheapest approach, a hand sweep of M60's reproducer (68 schedules, about 15 minutes on 2 workers). Dropping M60's clock step `T=2:-30` was enough; with the clock step kept, 1 of 49 kill/pause step shifts failed (`P=etcd3@29+1`) and 3 of 10 other clock-step positions failed (steps 3, 4, 6). The explorer campaign was not needed. On 3.5.6-nq all six failing schedules pass.
- **Observed, not explained:** the kill alone and the pause alone both pass (the kill alone leaves the members agreeing); the one-revision lead of etcd1 appears only with a later pause of etcd2 or etcd3, and a client sees it go back only for some pause steps (etcd3 at step 26 or 27).
