### Milestone 80 — DONE (the JavaScript/TypeScript assertion SDK for Node.js; a Node.js example on the Mac backend; 2026-10-24)

Branch `js-sdk` (not merged). No module or engine change.
- **The package** `sdk/js` (`hydo-assert`, `"private": true`, not published; no dependencies; CommonJS `index.js`, ESM `index.mjs` that re-exports it so both entries share one state; TypeScript types `index.d.ts`/`index.d.mts`, checked with `tsc` 6.0.3 under `node16` resolution from an `.mts` and a `.cts` file). API = the Go SDK's in camelCase: `always`, `alwaysOrUnreachable`, `sometimes`, `reachable`, `unreachable` with `check`/`hit`/`cmp` and the named comparison helpers `greaterThan`, `greaterThanOrEqualTo`, `lessThan`, `lessThanOrEqualTo`; `eventually(id, msg, withinMs)` with `begin`/`done`/`expire`, `awaitCond` (async) and `awaitCondSync` (blocks with `Atomics.wait`); `buggify(id, msg).fire(d)`; `catalog()`, `setupComplete()`; `emitLine(tag, value)` for other one-line record kinds. Records are written synchronously (`fs.writeSync`, one write per record) to `/dev/ttyS0`; `HYDO_ASSERT_OUT` = a path, `stderr` or `off` as in Go. Details are encoded as Go's `encoding/json` encodes the matching Go values (keys in UTF-8 byte order, HTML characters and U+2028/U+2029 escaped, Go's float format with `-0`, bigints as 64-bit integers, `Uint8Array` as base64, a lone surrogate as `�`; a NaN, an infinity or a cycle drops the record, as `json.Marshal` fails). BUGGIFY uses the Go configuration (`$HYDO_BUGGIFY`, else `/tmp/hydo-buggify`, parsed as Go parses it) and the same splitmix64/FNV-1a decisions in 64-bit `BigInt` arithmetic.
- **Byte-for-byte proof** (`sdk/js/test/conformance.sh`): a Go program built against `hvx/stack/hydoassert`'s own sources and a Node program make the same calls (every type, dedup, comparisons, NaN/±Inf records, eventually, fault points sharing one details object, a second catalog, and details with nested maps and arrays, bytes, uint64/int64 extremes, floats at every formatting boundary, control and HTML characters, U+2028/U+2029, astral characters as keys, invalid UTF-8). Five BUGGIFY configurations (seed in the environment, seed in the file, none, an odd enable value, an invalid seed) × the CommonJS and the ESM entry: all 20 console/stdout pairs identical to Go 1.26.5 (Node 24.16.0 on the Mac). **Go itself changed:** Go 1.27 writes invalid UTF-8 as a raw U+FFFD instead of the escape `�` (checked: Go 1.26.5 on the build machine and on the Mac escapes); the SDK writes the Go 1.26 form, and the conformance script pins `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.5`. `node --test` unit tests (8): JS-only values (`undefined`, `Map`/`Set`, `Date`, typed arrays, cycles), drop-but-seen of an unencodable record, non-object details as `{"value": ...}`, `emitLine`, async liveness, the three sink switches, BUGGIFY determinism and rates over 400 seeds.
- **Example `hvx/onboard/examples/node-kv`:** a key/value server over HTTP on loopback with an append-only log (`nkv-server.mjs`, ESM) and its client and oracle (`nkv.cjs`, CommonJS), sharing their declarations in `props.cjs` (so the oracle's catalog lists every property): `always` (acked PUT durable; log size via `lessThanOrEqualTo`), `always_or_unreachable` (log lines parse), `unreachable` (overwrite), `sometimes` (recovered, PUT unacked), `reachable` (torn tail), `eventually` (serves within 5 s), two BUGGIFY points (lost reply, slow fdatasync); knob `S=never` turns off fdatasync. Node is nodejs-slim 22.2.0 from the pinned nixpkgs (24.05, `89c4987`), gc-rooted on the build machine (`hvx/BUILD-PINS.md`). Run on the **Mac backend** (the bare-metal machine was crowded): `hydo-mac.yaml` lists the aarch64 node and the 21 files its loader opens, staged by `fetch-node-arm64.sh` and checked against `files.sha256`; built with the Mac release 0.1.0 installed into a scratch prefix, with this branch's `hydo-build`, `hydo-init-buggify.sh` and `manifest_space.py` copied over it. Image `explore-ob-node-kv-arm64` (cpio `b804d39d…`, disk `d031f382…`), two builds identical. Each schedule twice, identical: `disk=none | run` OK acked=20 `82b15f75dbeac422`; `disk=cut | run C=12` OK acked=12 `fa37081f1641a8b4`; `disk=cut | run C=12 S=never` VIOLATION `key k:0 lost` `df57c744f7e40b68`; `disk=none | run K=nkv@8+2 B=5abc` OK acked=18 `75af1df9fdd3812c`; `disk=none | run B=5abc,1000,300` OK acked=8 `f7bf57f6112f73b4` (both fault points fire, `HYDO-BUGGIFY` line present). `hydo_assert_report` over those consoles shows all 13 nkv properties (`nkv.acked_durable` FAIL from the `S=never` cut, `nkv.torn_tail` NOT EXERCISED, the rest PASS, `nkv.serves_after_start` as liveness). `hydo explore --strategy swarm` seeds 1–10 twice: same 10 fingerprints. With `--buggify`, seeds 1–20 twice: same 20 schedules (all with `B=`) and fingerprints, same report apart from wall times; 10 failures, all reproduced, all lying-disk, relaxed `S=never` or I/O errors; none with durable settings and an honest disk; both fault points in the report's property table. `hydo.yaml` is the same example for the Linux backend (`root.nix`, x86_64 nodejs-slim 22.2.0); built twice on the build machine with the pinned `hvx_seedrng` (`c2a440c5…`), byte-identical (ext4 `20758f5a…`, disk `617aeae3…`), not run (no VM was started on the bare-metal machine).
- **Also on this branch:** a cherry-pick of `rust-sdk`'s `bff5c01` (manifest key `buggify: true` turns the schedule token `B=` into `/tmp/hydo-buggify`; identical patch, so merging both branches does not conflict), and a fix in `hvx/explore/manifest_space.py`: `restrict()` dropped `B=` from every onboarded project's schedules, so `explore.py --buggify` never reached an SDK's fault points there; it now keeps `B=` for a manifest with `buggify: true` (other manifests' schedules unchanged).

### Milestone 81 — DONE (Linux release runs without sudo; image builds independent of the time zone; the other seven 2-vCPU stack scenarios; 2026-10-24)

Branch `product-fixes` (from main `230ad15`). Three small fixes, each in its own commit. No module change; the main `/dev/hvx` and the bare-metal machine's live setup were not touched.
- **Runs without sudo in the Linux release** (`hvx/dist/hydo`, `libexec/hvx-run`, `install.sh`, `QUICKSTART-linux.md`, `README.md`). New `hydo module udev install|remove|status [--name N] [--group G] [--mode 0660] [--no-adduser] [--dry-run]`: `install` writes `/etc/udev/rules.d/70-hydo-N.rules` (`KERNEL=="N", SUBSYSTEM=="misc", GROUP="G", MODE="0660"`), creates the group (default `hydo`) if missing, adds the user, reloads udev and applies the rule to a loaded device; `remove` deletes the rule and sets the device back to root:root 0600. `install.sh --udev [--udev-group G] [--udev-name N]` runs `install` (sudo once); without `--udev` nothing changes. `libexec/hvx-run` drops the leading `sudo` when the user can read and write `$HVX_DEV` (default `/dev/hvx`; `HYDO_SUDO=1` keeps sudo) and passes `HVX_DEV` through sudo when it keeps it, so `hydo run` and `explore.py` both use sudo only when needed. `hydo` honours `HVX_DEV` (a side module, with its own lock file), uses `/var/tmp/hvx-snap-<uid>` when a run without sudo cannot write the root-made `/var/tmp/hvx-snap`, waits for udev after loading a module that has a rule, and `hydo doctor` / `module status` say who can open the device. Security meaning, stated in the rule file and the docs: anyone in the group can run guests through the kernel module, which was not hardened against hostile users, so the group is for people one would give sudo.
  **Checked on the bare-metal machine** with a release built from this branch (tarball `f01b1392…`, every asset matched `assets.sha256`) installed in `~/hydo-pf/prefix`, side module `hvxpf` (srcversion `CA5BF8BF2A0D66B312A68EF`, the main source), the login user's own group (so no group or membership was created): loaded first, `/dev/hvxpf` was `crw------- root root`; `install.sh --udev --udev-name hvxpf --udev-group <that group>` and `hydo module udev install` made it `crw-rw----` owned by root and that group (the trigger path), and an unload + load gave the new node the same (the add path); `hvx-run sudo id -u` printed 1000 on `/dev/hvxpf` and 0 on `/dev/hvx`. Then, as the login user with a `sudo` on PATH that only logs and fails: the Redis example image `8c828d3e…` (as in M75), `hydo run "disk=none | run"` OK `c513f10aee733aae` and `hydo run "disk=cut | run C=12 R=everysec"` VIOLATION k:2 `ecef6f8fe6c39ec0` (both the M75 values), `hydo explore --seeds 1-3 --strategy swarm --workers 1` (1 failure, lying-disk, reproduced and shrunk to `disk=lying:3 dseed=eac0b527c5ac67d9 | run C=20`, `715770dac37b9163`) and `hydo repro hydo-out/explore` (2 of 2 with the recorded fingerprint). The fake sudo was called 0 times; every sampled `hvx_explore` of these runs ran as the login user; snapshots went to `/var/tmp/hvx-snap-1000`. Afterwards `hydo module udev remove` gave `/dev/hvxpf` back to `root root 0600` (doctor then says runs use sudo), the side module was unloaded, and `/etc/udev/rules.d` is empty again; `/dev/hvx` stayed `crw------- root root` throughout.
- **Image builds independent of the time zone** (`hvx/onboard/hydo-build`, `hydo_oci.py`, `build-linux.sh`, `build-stack.sh`, `build-kafka.sh`, `build-mysql.sh`, `build-spread{,2,3}.sh`). libfaketime reads the frozen build time `2020-09-13 16:26:40` in the local zone, which is 1600000000 only in UTC+4 (the build machine's zone, UTC+4 all year). Every faketime'd call now gets `TZ=UTC-4` (POSIX for UTC+4; hydo-build's `FAKE_TZ`, `faketime_env()`), which on the build machine is the same instant. On the build machine, pinned tools, `hvx_seedrng` compiled with the pinned gcc (`c2a440c5…`; the `01adf1bd…` stand-in in `~/hydo/build` was not used), from main `230ad15` plus the fix: `examples/redis` gave `03beff6c…` (= `explore-ob-redis-nq-disk.img`) with no TZ, `TZ=UTC` and `TZ=America/New_York`; `examples/etcd` 3.6.15 gave `021d350a…` (= `explore-ob-etcd-3.6.15-nq-disk.img`) with no TZ, `TZ=UTC` and `TZ=Pacific/Auckland`; main without the fix gave the right Redis bytes with no TZ but `bfe8db2d…` under `TZ=UTC`. The hand-written scripts had the same dependency: `build-linux.sh`'s `mkext2_reproducible`, run from the script itself, gives the recorded `sql-disk.img` `b3b2b9c0…` and `redis-disk.img` `64c7e3f5…` in all three zones with the fix, and `c94536dd…`/`f96ed349…` (UTC) or `95384aa7…`/`0381d795…` (New York) without it. The other scripts were not rebuilt; their change is the same one-variable prefix on the same kind of call, and the pinned faketime gives 1600000000 both with no TZ and with `TZ=UTC-4` on the build machine (1600014400 under `TZ=UTC`). The release's wrapper already set `TZ=UTC-4` (M75) and is unchanged in effect.
- **The other seven stack fault scenarios on 2 vCPUs** (`HVX_VCPUS=2`, `stack-nq-disk.img`, harness from main in `~/hydo-pf`, byte-identical to M79's; full table in `hvx/REBASELINE.md` §7.6). Each `mode=fault` scenario two fresh runs plus a branch, all identical; the relaxed case `mode=sanity` twice, identical; all OK 30/30 exactly-once: kill mysql@10 `5787e8c2ab91c8dd`, kill kafka@20 `70f2262407cef26e`, kill redis@10 `124cc3dad8b93dfb` (27 cached), net mysql `c07c4bbbcde6d0d9` (first pass 29/30), net redis `962604a54396c17c`, net kafka `cbdbd77370547093` (first pass 28/30), net kafka with MySQL relaxed `51d0cedb269b4693` (first pass 22/30, 24/30 on 1 vCPU). No verdict differs in kind from 1 vCPU. With M79's Kafka kill at order 10, all eight scenarios now have 2-vCPU values.
- **Bare-metal machine use:** at most two of these VMs at a time, every VM through `~/bin/hvx-run`, and no VM started while `/var/tmp/hydo-QUIET` existed (it existed 08:27–08:39 UTC; both of my queues waited). Raw results: `/var/tmp/pf-smp/` (2-vCPU runs), `~/hydo-pf/sudoless/` and `~/hydo-pf/redis/hydo-out/` (sudo-less runs) on the bare-metal machine; the build machine build directory `~/hydo-pfb` was deleted after the builds.
- **Not done:** the main `/dev/hvx` keeps mode 0600 on the bare-metal machine (by instruction); `install.sh --udev` was checked only with a side device and the user's own group, so creating a new group and adding a user (`groupadd`, `usermod -aG`, then a new login) ran nowhere; `build-stack.sh`, `build-kafka.sh`, `build-mysql.sh` and `build-spread*.sh` were not rebuilt after the TZ change (only the shared call pattern and `build-linux.sh`'s ext2 helper were checked).

### Milestone 82 — DONE (the Rust assertion SDK; a Rust example on both backends; 2026-10-24)

Branch `rust-sdk` (worktree `../hydo-rsdk`, main merged in after M81; not merged). No module or engine change.
- **The crate** `sdk/rust` (`hydo-assert`, `publish = false`, its own workspace, std only; features `noop` and `serde_json`). The Go SDK's run-time API in Rust names: `always`, `always_or_unreachable`, `sometimes`, `reachable`, `unreachable` (return `&'static Assertion`, registered at once), `check`/`hit`/`cmp` (any ordered value), `eventually(id, msg, within)` with `begin`/`done`/`expire`, `await_cond` (blocking) and `await_async` (takes a check future and a sleep future, so it works with tokio or any runtime), `buggify(id, msg).fire(d)`, `catalog()`, `setup_complete()`, and `emit_line(tag, value)` for other one-line record kinds. Rust additions: call-site macros that capture file and line (`hydo_always!(cond, "id" => "msg", {"k": v})`, without an id the message is the id; `hydo_sometimes!`, `hydo_reachable!`, `hydo_unreachable!`, `hydo_always_or_unreachable!`, `hydo_always_cmp!`/`hydo_sometimes_cmp!` and the named `hydo_{always,sometimes}_{greater_than,greater_than_or_equal_to,less_than,less_than_or_equal_to}!`, `hydo_buggify!`, `hydo_eventually!`) and `declare!` for named statics. Both register before `main` with a std-only constructor (`.init_array` on ELF, `__mod_init_func` on Mach-O, lock-free intrusive list, no allocation), so properties in code that never runs are in the catalog; macro records carry one more field, `"loc":"<file>:<line>"`, last. Catalog order: `declare!` entries and macro sites sorted by file, line, column (a `declare!` block in the order written), then run-time declarations in declaration order. Details: `details!{..}` / `Details` (a `BTreeMap`, bytewise key order), encoded as Go's `encoding/json` encodes them (HTML characters and U+2028/U+2029 escaped, Go's float forms such as `1e+21` and `1.5e-7`, `f32` as Go's `float32`; a NaN or an infinity drops the record). Output, deduplication and BUGGIFY configuration and decisions are the Go SDK's.
- **Byte-for-byte proof** (`sdk/rust/tests/conformance.sh`): a Go program built against `hvx/stack/hydoassert`'s own sources and `examples/conformance.rs` make the same calls (every type, dedup, comparisons with an unknown operator, NaN/Inf records, eventually, 8 fault points × 50 rounds, details with nested maps and lists, uint64/int64 extremes, floats at the formatting boundaries, f32, control and HTML characters, U+2028/U+2029). Three BUGGIFY configurations (seed in the environment, in the file, none): consoles and stdouts identical with Go 1.26.5 and with Go 1.27.3 (console sha256 prefixes `3cc3f8d11d37a639` / `3d3e4ea08c555c4e` / `09906536cadbbe64`). `cargo test` (also `--features noop` and `--features serde_json`): encoder, BUGGIFY parse rules and rates over 400 seeds, the macros, catalog order and `loc`, `await_async` under tokio, `emit_line`.
- **Example `hvx/onboard/examples/rust-kv`:** `rkv`, a key/value server over TCP on loopback with an append-only log, plus its client and oracle (`serve`, `put`, `ping`, `check`): `always` (acked PUT durable; log size via a comparison macro), `always_or_unreachable` (log lines parse), `sometimes` (recovered, PUT without reply, oracle had to ask again), `reachable` (torn tail), `eventually` (serves within 5 s), three BUGGIFY points; knob `S=never` turns off fdatasync; the oracle writes the catalog (the server starts before S1, so its records then are not in a run's console). `build-rkv.sh [aarch64]` links a static musl binary with rust-lld (Rust 1.90.0, no C toolchain); two clean builds identical (x86_64 `a26ef466…`, arm64 `e5d97b55…`). `hydo-build` gains nothing new for it beyond the M80-merged `buggify: true`.
- **Mac backend** (bare-metal machine crowded; Mac release 0.1.0 installed into a scratch prefix with this branch's `hydo-build`, `hydo-init-buggify.sh` and `manifest_space.py` copied in): image `explore-ob-rust-kv-arm64` (cpio `500ecb2f…`, disk `e19be506…`), two builds identical. Each schedule twice, identical: `disk=none | run` OK acked=20 `64de839903b96e45`; `disk=cut | run C=12` OK acked=12 `6a7e5947973e5770`; `disk=cut | run C=12 S=never` VIOLATION acked=12 lost=12 `6f29e36a1cd516be`; `K=rkv@8+2` OK `375ca29fb8f1ab23`; `B=5abc,1000,500` OK acked=2 `de31ab2024fb243f`; `B=1234` OK `244342f8f8aaa1e9`; `K=rkv@8+2 B=1234` OK `0be47ac30f845cb6`; `P=rkv@5+3` OK `d45afcdc04b39994`. `hydo_assert_report` over the 20 consoles lists all 13 rkv properties (`rkv.acked_durable` FAIL from the `S=never` cut, `rkv.torn_tail` NOT EXERCISED). `hydo explore --seeds 1-16 --strategy swarm --buggify --workers 2` twice: all 16 schedules carry `B=`, same 16 fingerprints, same property table; 8 failures, all reproduced, all lying disk / relaxed `S` / I/O errors; the three fault points fired (6, 4, 1). With `B=5abc,1000,500` the first oracle reported a false loss (a GET timed out while the server was busy with abandoned slow PUTs); the oracle now re-asks for up to 30 s and exits 2 if the server never answers.
- **Bare-metal backend** (before moving to the Mac): `explore-ob-rust-kv-v2-disk.img` (`118c9f2c…`, from commit `ff4a4dd`: an earlier rkv without the oracle retry, pinned `hvx_seedrng` `c2a440c5…`, two builds identical; `~/hydo/build` on the bare-metal machine), `hvx_explore one` twice each: `disk=none dseed=1 | run` OK `ecfd264f2e19865f`, `disk=cut dseed=1 | run C=12` OK `0d8d109d8a1eca66`; the SDK's records in both consoles. The `.init_array` registration was also checked natively on the build machine (static musl binary prints the full catalog).
- **History recorder:** not on main when this was written (branch `history-checkers`), so the crate has no history API; `emit_line` is the extension point.
- **Not done:** the history-recorder line format; the remaining box schedules on the final image; Windows/wasm constructors (there a static assertion joins the catalog only at its first evaluation).
